Please enter 1-8 valid characters." and the prompt repeats. The 8 game character codes go straight into `Save_Get(land_info).name` after the vanilla new-town initialiser ran. If stdin is closed (EOF) before a valid name, the fallback "Village" is used. A restart or a legacy adoption never asks: the console shows `Loading town "<name>"...` (from server.ini's land_name codes), `Town loaded.`, `Server ready.` (`Town "<name>" adopted from the legacy save.` for an adoption). There is no `townname` command: a town's name is fixed once generated. `--server-id` was retired (the internal id is `default`).

## Shop purchase correctness + catalog generation (Patch 1)

The host already validated every SHOP_BUY (slot / item / host price / funds / journal replay / atomic wallet + item + sold mark, single threaded: the loser of a race gets NOT_AVAILABLE). Patch 1 closes the remaining gaps and gives the physical shop an explicit **catalog generation**:
- **Catalog generation.** The host's shop state is `Save_t.shop` (level, 39 stock slots + rare item, sold markers `RSV_SHOP_SOLD_*`, exchange day). The generation (`s_restock.cat_gen`, HOST_CONFIG byte 3 = low 8 bits) is bumped when the stock is regenerated: a restock finishes, the daily exchange day changes, or the shop level changes (an upgrade). A SHOP_BUY carries `1 + gen % 255` in `tag.flags` (0 = not claimed); the host refuses another generation with STALE_CATALOG (32) before looking at the item, so a client can never buy from a catalog the host replaced.
- **Closed gate.** A SHOP_BUY while the shop is restocking is refused RESTOCKING (31); while the shop status is neither OPEN nor OPENEVENT (raffle day, upgrade closure) NOT_AVAILABLE.
- **Host counter window.** The host's own player writes the item into its pocket at the "yes" and debits / marks the slot sold when the thank-you row ends; in between a client's SHOP_BUY of the same unique item still validated. The counter now reserves the item (`pc_net_game_host_shop_reserve`, 60 s cap, ignored when a second copy exists) and publishes the sale at once (`pc_net_game_host_shop_sold_notify`) instead of at the next 500 ms digest poll.
- **Not implemented (by design).** The shop upgrade itself: the physical building tile (`aSL_RewriteShopFg`, host-only), `mEv_SAVED_RENEWSHOP` closure mirroring, the displaced-items police-box clearing and the flyers are described in the synchronization audit; the generation / level plumbing above is what an upgrade will use. **Since then:** implemented as the announced 5-minute closure (section "Shop upgrade: announced 5-minute closure").

## Indoor villagers (Patch 2)

A villager standing in its own house is a NPC2 actor (`ac_npc2_move.c_inc`) that every process with the room loaded simulates on its own (schedule, action, RNG), and a dedicated host has no avatar and no room simulation. So the minimum authoritative state is **one pose stream per room** (position, facing, coarse action), not host simulation:

- `PC_NETGAME_MSG_ROOM_NPC` (id 67, unreliable, 28 B; v8 still unreleased, no version bump). The room is `SCENE_NPC_HOUSE` + `house_owner_name` (= the villager's npc_id).
- The in-room process that starts first **holds a host-arbitrated lease** and keeps simulating; it samples the actor (20 Hz) and sends the pose to the host. The host accepts it only if the sender announced exactly that room (PLAYER_SCENE) and holds the lease (released after 1.5 s of silence or when the holder leaves the room), drops stale / zero / non-finite frames and relays to every OTHER in-room READY peer.
- Other occupants (`pc_net_game_room_npc_follow`) apply the delay-interpolated pose instead of running schedule / action / angle code; a late joiner snaps to the pose on the first sample; if the holder leaves, the next process that stops receiving poses simulates from where the actor was and takes the lease.
- Not synchronized (unchanged): dialogue, choices, interaction scripts, a villager in a local talk state (never overridden), whether the villager is home (relies on the existing NPC_STATE `is_home` stream), room furniture of the villager.

Test: `tools/net_spike/test_room_npc_protocol.py` (real host + scripted clients; actor hooks only source-audited, no two-process visual verification).

## Nook Work Mode foundation (Patch 3)

"I'd like to work" (Nook's shop, Other things) for **every** player: resident, guest and the host's own player. The job belongs to the **character** (the bound PersonalID of the resident / guest record), never to the connection, peer slot, address or a PID alone; the **host** owns it.

- `TXN_COMMIT` kind **16 WORK** (the same one-phase machinery, journal replay and pre/post images as the shop kinds): `aux_cond` = op (1 ENTER, 2 DELIVER, 3 LEAVE), `aux_item` = the low 16 bits of the job id the client believes it is completing, `slot`/`item` = the pocket slot and objective item for DELIVER. New reasons 33 WORK_NO_JOB, 34 WORK_STALE_JOB, 35 WORK_WALLET_FULL.
- `WORK_STATE` (id 68, host -> the requesting client, reliable, 28 B) carries the job exactly as the host holds it (mode, state, job_id, objective, reward, jobs_done, last_rewarded), sent right **before** every WORK result (any outcome). The client only mirrors it.
- Host job table (`work_jobs.dat` next to the other host files, `servers/<id>/` on a dedicated server): host-generated, never reused job ids (counter persisted, never 0 mod 65536), job type table (`s_work_defs`: one row = one job type; today a delivery of one fruit, 300-600 Bells), per-character record, fnv-checked file written **before** the result is sent. ENTER creates or resumes the job (idempotent), LEAVE keeps an active job, DELIVER checks Work Mode + ACTIVE job + job id + objective item + wallet cap and, in one step, empties the pocket slot, pays the host reward, retires the job and writes the tombstone `last_rewarded`.
- Duplicate-reward protection: journal replay of the same (nonce, seq), retired job (WORK_NO_JOB) for a new sequence number, stale job id (WORK_STALE_JOB), tombstone across a host restart. A guest that buys a house keeps its record (re-keyed in the shared promotion core).
- Nook dialogue: "I'd like to work" in the Other things menu (the 6-choice window is full for a guest: "Say code" gives its place to it there; the choice string window holds 16 characters, so no final period), job row ("Bring me an apple ... I'll pay N Bells"), Here it is! / I'll fetch it. / I'm done working.
- Not done (by design): any job type besides the fruit delivery, job timeouts / expiry, per-job NPC state, WORK_STATE push at connect time (the state arrives with every op; ENTER is the query), the host player's work state is not mirrored to other peers.

Test: `tools/net_spike/test_work_mode_protocol.py` (real host + scripted resident / guest clients, host restart). The Nook dialogue and the client apply are covered by the build, the message selftest and source audits only: no GUI run.

## Nook Work Mode: errand registry + first-page menu (Patch 3 revision)

- **Menu.** "I'd like to work" (no job) / "Check my job" (active job) is the 4th choice of the FIRST page of Nook's talk (vanilla message 0x1092, 4 -> 5 choices), not under Other things. It is added when the message is loaded (`pc_nook_msg_patch` from `mMsg_LoadMsgData`) from the authoritative job state, so there is no local "working" flag; the previous Other-things entries are gone (the guest menu is vanilla + house / refresh again). The job state (`WORK_STATE`, now 36 B) is pushed once a record is SYNCED (connect, reconnect, host restart) and before every work result, so the label follows start / complete / quit / reconnect.
- **Job registry.** FRUIT (the town's native fruit: `Save_Get(fruit)` of the host save), FETCH_VILLAGER (Nook names a villager + an item; the villager hands it over in ITS talk: op VILLAGER_GIVE, then DELIVER to Nook) and DELIVER_VILLAGER (Nook's parcel: TAKE_PARCEL, handed to the named villager: VILLAGER_RECEIVE which pays the optional host-decided tip, Bells / an item / none, then REPORT to Nook). Nook's Bell reward is always paid by DELIVER / REPORT; the tip never replaces it. LEAVE quits (abandons) the job. Ops 1..7; the host validates job id + state-machine step + item + villager + wallet cap in one pure plan.
- **Villager side.** `aQMgr_TALK_KIND_WORK` (ac_quest_manager.c + ac_quest_talk_work.c_inc): when the character's current step points at the villager it is talking to, the hello plays while the work transaction goes to the host; the continue message then becomes the generated row ("Here you go!" / the parcel + tip) which ends the talk. The item appears / leaves the pockets from the host post-image; since "Hand-over animations" (below) the vanilla hand-over plays after the APPLIED.
- Test hooks: `AC_TEST_WORK_TYPE=1,2,3` (host, with AC_TEST_HOOKS) forces the type of the next created jobs; `AC_TEST_DUMP_MSG=0x1092` prints the (patched) message bytes of a running game.
- Tests: `test_work_mode_protocol.py` (real host, scripted clients, all three types, refusals, duplicates, quit, reconnect push, guest, host restart), `test_work_menu_patch.py` (the real `pc_nook_house.c` harness: first-page patch per state). The Nook dialogue and the villager talk have NOT been driven through the GUI.

## Event NPC authority (Patch 4)

Before: the EVENT service mirrored the host's DECISIONS (who visits, when), but every process created, placed and ran its own visitor (placement jitter came from the local clock, rewards from the local RNG), so two players could meet different visitors and both claim a once-per-visit reward, and a dedicated host (whose parked player is never "near" a visitor) never had them at all.

- **Presence** (`EVNPC_STATE`, id 69, host -> every READY client, reliable, 104 B, on change and at READY): the host scans ITS NPC actors for an allowlist of event NPC ids (Gulliver, K.K., the Wisp, Saharah, Joan, Redd, Artist, Designer, Gypsy) while its scene is the town field and sends who is present + where + facing. A READY client (1) never creates an allowlisted NPC itself (`aNPC_setupActor_proc` returns without creating: `pc_net_game_evnpc_suppress_spawn`), (2) creates / removes its local actor to match the table, at the host's place and facing. The host's event manager creates these NPCs without the "a player is nearby" gate (`aEvMgr_HOST_UNBOUNDED`), so a dedicated host has them whatever its player does.
- **Outcome** (`TXN_COMMIT` kind 17 EVNPC, reason 36 EVNPC_DONE): Gulliver's gift (a TOWN-level once-per-visit flag: the host rolls the item with its own RNG / shop state, grants it into the claimant's free slot and sets `dozaemon_completed` in the same step; the race loser is refused) and K.K.'s song (a per-PLAYER once-per-concert bit, now HOST-held: a reconnect or a client restart cannot ask twice; the requested song 0..0x36 becomes `MINIDISK + song` in the post-image; K.K.'s random "surprise" song is no item and stays local). The dialogue of a READY client asks the host (`pc_net_game_evnpc_claim_begin` + the normal `pc_net_game_ts_poll`); refused = Gulliver's generated "I gave it away" row / K.K.'s vanilla "already got one" line. The host's own player keeps the vanilla path (it is the authority and updates the same flags).
- Late join / reconnect: the table is sent to a peer that has not got the current one (a joiner, a reconnect); the claim flags are in the host save, so a host restart or reconnect cannot repeat a reward.
- Not covered (documented; Redd / Saharah / Wendell / Gracie / the Wisp are now host claims, see "Shared event NPCs" below): the other event NPCs' interactions (Joan / Gypsy, Kapp'n, Resetti, Pete, Blanca) -- their PRESENCE follows the host's table (allowlisted ones) but their talk still runs locally; a walking visitor's pose after its creation is the client's own local AI (only presence and the appearance place are mirrored); the event-actor `area[]` runtime areas are still not mirrored; a visitor whose acre the host's field has not loaded cannot be created by the host; while the host's player is inside a building the table is frozen at the last town state.
- Tests: `test_evnpc_real.py` (REAL host + REAL client process: the host creates Gulliver, the client creates it at the same place and removes it when it leaves; a real client claims the gift and the host's roll lands in its pockets), `test_evnpc_protocol.py` (race, duplicate, reconnect, per-player song bits, guests, no visit). Hooks: `AC_TEST_EVNPC_SPAWN`, `AC_TEST_EVNPC_DESPAWN_MS`, `AC_TEST_EVNPC_CLAIM` (all need AC_TEST_HOOKS=1).

## Shared event NPCs (worker E: host claims for Redd, Saharah, Wendell, Gracie and the Wisp)

Before: only Gulliver's gift and K.K.'s song were host decisions (kind 17). Every other visitor ran its talk on the client's own copy of the town: the client rolled the item with its own RNG, wrote the pockets / wallet itself and wrote the visit record (`event_save_data.special.*`) into its LOCAL save, which the host never saw and the next EVENT push (TS service 5, host -> client only) overwrote. Two clients could therefore buy the same one-of-a-kind Redd item, get Wendell's / Gracie's once-per-player gift twice (after the push erased the local record) and each return spirits to "their" Wisp.

### Classification (every event NPC of the decomp)

| NPC (file) | vanilla flow | shared / town state | per-player state | 0.2.1 multiplayer | decision |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Crazy Redd (`ac_ev_broker2_move.c_inc` `aEBR2_sell_after`) | A at a display piece -> price (`mSP_ItemNo2ItemPrice` x 4) -> yes -> wallet + money bags pay, item into a free slot, display piece removed (`aBD_ReportFtrSales`) | `special.event.broker`: `items[3]` (the ONE-OF-A-KIND lineup), `used`, `pid[2]` / `sold_items[2]`, `end_time` (mirrored by service 5, host -> client) | none (a buyer is recognised by `pid[]`) | every client sells from its own copy: the same piece can be sold to every client (and the host) | **claim op 1 REDD_BUY** |
| Saharah (`ac_ev_carpetPeddler.c_inc`) | yes -> pick a carpet (TAKE menu) -> pay 3000 x 2^used -> a random EVENT carpet replaces it | `special.event.arabian.used` (town-wide price doubling, cap 4) | none | price counter local (cheaper duplicates), carpet rolled by the client | **claim op 2 SAHARAH_TRADE** |
| Wendell, "Artist" (`ac_ev_artist_move.c_inc`) | give a fish -> a random EVENT wallpaper not given this visit replaces it | `special.event.artist`: `pids[2]`, `walls[2]`, `used` (3 gifts per visit; > 2 = done for everybody) | once per PersonalID | record local and overwritten: unlimited gifts | **claim op 3 ARTIST_TRADE** |
| Gracie, "Designer" (`ac_ev_designer_talk.c_inc`) | car-wash minigame (local skill, result 0/1/2) -> result 0/1: a random cloth not given this visit | `special.event.designer`: `pids[3]`, `gifted_cloths[3]`, `used` (vanilla has NO bound: a 4th player writes past `pids[3]`) | once per PersonalID | record local and overwritten: unlimited gifts, overflow with > 3 players | **claim op 4 DESIGNER_GIFT** (the minigame result stays the client's: it is skill, not state) |
| Wisp (`ac_ev_ghost_talk.c_inc`) | >= 5 spirit points (SPIRIT(k) counts k+1) -> all spirits handed over -> choice: weeds / roof colour / an uncollected item | ghost SAVE area flag `RETURNED_SPIRITS` (`event_save_common.area[]`, NOT mirrored), `ghost_day = 0`, `clear_grass` (weeds, consumed by the host's growth) | the roof of the player's own house; "uncollected" = the player's collect bits | spirits deleted and the reward granted locally, outside any transaction; the weeds reward was lost on a client; the host's Wisp never learned | **claim op 5 WISP_RETURN** |
| Joan (`ac_ev_kabuPeddler_move.c_inc`) | 10/50/100 turnips at the Sunday price | price = `kabu_price_schedule` (already host-mirrored, `pcnetgame_client_apply_market_state`); `weekly.kabu_peddler.spoken_pids` only picks the greeting | none | correct price, unlimited stock: a purchase cannot duplicate anything | **no claim** (documented) |
| Katrina, "Gypsy" | 50 Bells (wallet) -> fortune (`Private_c.destiny`) | placement only | destiny | per player, nothing shared | no claim |
| Gulliver / K.K. | (Patch 4) | | | kind 17 | unchanged |
| Jingle (`ac_ev_santa_move.c_inc`) | 5-talk questionnaire -> presents to the FIRST player of the town only | santa SAVE area (pid, present_count, cloth[10]) NOT mirrored | | every client is "the first player" of its own copy | **skipped** (S4 candidate: needs the santa save area on the host + a claim per present) |
| Night stall, Fireworks (`ac_ev_yomise_move.c_inc`) | 8-item paid lineup | yomise SAVE area (goods[8]) NOT mirrored | | every client has the full stock | **skipped** (S4 candidate, same shape as REDD_BUY on a save area) |
| Chip, Jack, Franklin, Miko, Tortimer (trophy / bridge / speeches), Resetti (Groundhog), castaway, dokutu | records / per-player trophies / unlimited candy & fork trades / paid fortune letter / talk only | Chip's record and Franklin's bitfield in non-mirrored save areas; nothing unique | per player | local; no one-of-a-kind stock | skipped (no unique stock; Chip's record is S4) |
| Kapp'n, Blanca, Porter, Brewster, Pascal, Lyle, Harriet, Phyllis/Pelly, Lottie | not in this decomp as item sellers (Kapp'n = island boat, no items; Brewster / Pascal / Lyle / Harriet / Lottie do not exist in AC GC) | | | | n/a |

### Design (TXN kind 21 EVNPC_CLAIM, TS service 9 EVNPC_CAPS)

- **One-phase transaction** `TXN_COMMIT` kind **21** (`PC_NETGAME_TXN_KIND_EVNPC_CLAIM`), the same journal / fence / replay / base / pre-image rules as every one-phase kind; its own host handler (`pcnetgame_handle_host_evc_txn`, "EVNPC SHARED" block at the end of pc_net_game.c). `tag.aux_cond` = the op:
  - 1 REDD_BUY: dest POCKET, slot = a free pocket slot, item = the piece the player was shown, flags = the lineup index 0..2, aux_item = the low 16 bits of the shown price. The host checks the visit (special kind + the special event type + today within its dates), `items[flags] == item` (else SOLD_OUT 50), the host price (`mSP_ItemNo2ItemPrice` x 4; mismatch = EVNPC_CHANGED 52), pays from the PRE-image wallet + money bags (the SHOP_BUY payer, NO_FUNDS 19), puts the piece into the slot and, in the same step, does the vanilla record (`items[i]` cleared, `pid` / `sold_items` / `used` / `end_time`).
  - 2 SAHARAH_TRADE: dest POCKET, slot = the pocket holding the carpet handed over, item = that carpet, aux_item = the shown price. The host recomputes the price from ITS `arabian.used`, pays, rolls the carpet with its RNG and replaces the slot; `used` + 1 (cap 4).
  - 3 ARTIST_TRADE: dest POCKET, slot = the pocket holding the fish, item = the fish. The host refuses a player already in `pids[]` / a visit with `used > 2` (EVNPC_CLAIMED 51 / SOLD_OUT 50), rolls the wallpaper excluding `walls[]`, replaces the fish, records.
  - 4 DESIGNER_GIFT: dest NONE, slot = a free pocket slot, item 0, aux_item = the minigame result (0 / 1). The host refuses a player already in `pids[]` (51) and a full visit (`used >= 3`, 50; this also removes the vanilla overflow), rolls the cloth (EVENT list for 0, COMMON for 1, excluding `gifted_cloths`), records.
  - 5 WISP_RETURN: dest NONE, slot = a pocket slot that holds a spirit (the item reward lands there), item 0, aux_item = the reward (0 weeds, 1 roof: 0x100 | palette, 2 item). The host counts the spirit points of the PRE-image (< 5 = NO_SPIRITS 54), refuses when its ghost event is not on or its `RETURNED_SPIRITS` is set (51), removes EVERY spirit from the post-image, grants the reward (weeds: `clear_grass`; roof: the claimant's house header on the host + house push; item: rolled with the claimant's collect bits), sets `RETURNED_SPIRITS` + `ghost_day = 0` once for the town and sends the host's Wisp away. Residents only (vanilla deletes the Wisp for a foreigner; a guest has no house): a guest is PRECOND.
  The roll uses the HOST's RNG; the "uncollected" preference reads the claimant's MIRROR record (Now_Private is swapped to it for the roll only).
- **Reasons**: 50 SOLD_OUT, 51 EVNPC_CLAIMED, 52 EVNPC_CHANGED (the client's view of the lineup / price is stale), 53 EVNPC_OFF (host kill switch), 54 NO_SPIRITS; plus the existing NOT_AVAILABLE 16 (no such visit), NO_FUNDS 19, PRECOND 9.
- **State to the other clients**: the special-event record already travels in TS service 5; the host refreshes and pushes it right after the APPLIED, so every client sees the sold piece / the used counters (a client inside Redd's tent takes a sold piece off its display when the push arrives). The Wisp's flag is not in service 5: it travels in the new **TS service 9 EVNPC_CAPS** (8 bytes: version 1, flags bit 0 = claims on, byte 2 bit 0 = the host's Wisp has its spirits back, 0, u32 LE mask of supported ops).
- **Client seams** (`pc_evnpc_shared.h`): `pc_evc_mode()` 0 = vanilla local (host / solo / AC_MP_EVENT_NPC=0 on either side / the host announced "off"), 1 = ask the host (kind 21), 2 = a client of a host without service 9 (a 0.2.x host): refuse with a polite row except Saharah (no one-of-a-kind stock: the 0.2.1 local trade). `pc_evc_begin(op, slot, item, flags, aux)` + `pc_net_game_ts_poll()`; the dialogue waits on its current page (Redd: sell_after, Saharah: menu close, Wendell: the hand-over check, Gracie: the result page, Wisp: the reward choice / roof pick) and continues with the vanilla line on APPLIED (the item is already in the pockets: the host post-image) or with a refusal row (pc_nook_house.c rows: sold out / already given / not enough spirits / link lost) otherwise; the loser keeps its Bells and items.
- **Old hosts**: a 0.2.x host never sends service 9, so the client never sends kind 21 (a 0.1.1 / 0.2.x host would answer an unknown kind with BAD_SHAPE + a record violation). A BAD_SHAPE answer to kind 21 latches the feature off for the session as a second guard.
- **Kill switch**: `AC_MP_EVENT_NPC=0` (host: service 9 says off, kind 21 is REJECTED EVNPC_OFF; client: local 0.2.1 behaviour). On by default.
- **The host's own player** keeps the vanilla talk (it is the authority and writes the same record); Redd's sale on the host refuses a piece a client bought while the talk ran, and a client's purchase takes the piece off the host player's tent display (`aBD_PC_SyncDisplayWithLineup`, also run on every client inside the tent when service 5 arrives).
- **Files**: `pc/include/pc_evnpc_shared.h`, `pc/src/pc_evnpc_shared.c` (pure logic + selftest), `pc/src/pc_net_game.c` ("EVNPC SHARED" block at the end + the small dispatch / TS / client-txn hooks), `pc/src/pc_nook_house.c` + `.h` (7 rows, count 52), `src/actor/ac_broker_design.c`, `src/actor/npc/event/ac_ev_broker2*.c(_inc)`, `ac_ev_carpetPeddler.c(_inc)`, `ac_ev_artist*.c(_inc)`, `ac_ev_designer.c` + `_talk.c_inc`, `ac_ev_ghost.c` + `_talk.c_inc`.
- **Tests**: `test_evnpc_shared_native.py` (2/2: the pure logic), `test_evnpc_shared_src.py` (39/39), `test_evnpc_shared_protocol.py` (54/54, FakeClients vs a REAL host: Redd race one APPLIED / one SOLD_OUT with the loser uncharged, REPLAYED resend, conservation, CHANGED / NO_FUNDS / BAD_SHAPE, a guest buyer, the lineup in the loser's service 5; Saharah's counter; Wendell's and Gracie's per-player and per-visit quotas; the Wisp's NO_SPIRITS / turn-in / once-per-town / guest refusal; no visitor; the kill switch), `test_evnpc_shared_real.py` (13/13: a REAL host + two REAL clients, the second asking for the piece the first bought), `test_evnpc_shared_oldhost_real.py` (6/6: this client on the PUBLISHED 0.2.1 host refuses locally, no violation). Hooks (AC_TEST_HOOKS=1): `AC_TEST_EVNPC_SEED=redd|saharah|artist|designer|wisp|none` (host), `AC_TEST_EVC_CLAIM=<op>[,<flags>[,<aux>]]`, `AC_TEST_EVC_WALLET`, `AC_TEST_EVC_ITEM` (client).
- **Not covered**: the dialogue seams were not driven through the real NPC GUI (no UI automation; the real tests call the same client API through the hook); a host restart between the APPLIED and the next town save loses the visit record together with the mirror pockets (the same durability as Nook's SHOP_BUY: no write-ahead sidecar); Joan / Katrina need no claim (no one-of-a-kind stock; Joan's price is host-mirrored); Jingle and the Fireworks night stall keep their state in non-mirrored save areas (S4 candidates of the REDD_BUY shape); Chip / Franklin / Jack / Miko have no unique stock.

## House synchronization is the default of every host (Patch 5)

`g_pc_house_sync` (HOST_CONFIG byte 1 bit 0) is now ON for **every** `--host` (dedicated or not) unless `--no-house-sync`; before, only `--dedicated` turned it on and a plain `--host` silently ran every house as a private local copy. The startup line for a plain host: `[PC] host services: house sync ON (default for every host; --no-house-sync disables)`. Town serving / personal sync stay `--dedicated` defaults (unchanged).

Audit of the guest / resident paths (read from code, then run with real processes): a guest has no house and is never pushed one (CANON_PUSH goes to every READY non-guest peer, `pcnetgame_hcl_own_house()` is -1 for a guest claim, a guest OWNER_COMMIT is NOT_OWNER before any `homes[]` index); a guest who buys a house is re-joined as the new resident and receives the canonical copies of every house at that join like any resident (the join push), so nothing is created for it on the way. The host's own edits are canonical (a host-originated change is pushed to every resident client).

The OWNER's client path (dirty test -> OWNER_COMMIT with the pockets -> APPLIED -> push to everybody else) had no process test; it is now exercised by two REAL clients: `AC_TEST_HOUSE_CLIENT_SWAP=<floor>,<cellA>,<cellB>` (TEST-ONLY, AC_TEST_HOOKS=1) swaps two layer-0 cells of the client's OWN house in its local save once the house + record are settled (the gate of the in-room commit), the unchanged commit machinery does the rest.

Test: `test_house_default_real.py` (REAL plain host + 2 REAL client processes + a scripted guest): the announcement with no flag, A's furniture move committed by the host (seq 1 -> 2, digest changed), B (another real process) receives the new canonical house, B restarted is pushed seq 2 at join, a guest is never pushed a house, the host restarted on the committed save has the same canonical digest. NOT run (needs UI to enter a room): the in-room live rebuild of a visitor, entering / leaving a house, disconnect while inside, drawer contents.

## Patch 6 - letters / designs / collections (scoped)

Ownership analysis (who owns it / where it lives / who may change it):
- Per-resident letters (mail[]), pockets, catalog orders, lotto: already per-character D3 record + host TXN (earlier milestones).
- Museum completion letter bits (`state_flags` 0xC0, `mPr_FLAG_MUSEUM_COMP_HANDBILL_*`): written only by the host's day-change code, but live as bits inside the CLIENT-owned u32.
  NEW (this patch): per-bit host ownership. The host keeps its bits on every upload merge (`pcnetgame_rec_merge_into_save`), a client always adopts the host's bits
  (`pcnetgame_crec_apply_staged`), the host-consumption digest covers them (`pcnetgame_rec_hostfield_digest`), the client-owned digest ignores them (`pcnetgame_crec_cown_digest`).
  Test: `tools/net_spike/test_museum_bits_protocol.py` (real host, scripted client; hook `AC_TEST_MUSEUM_BITS=<slot>,<mask>` with `AC_TEST_HOOKS=1`) - 7/7.
- Museum item collection (`museum_record`) was already HOST-owned (mail milestone R).
- NOT done (documented, no whole-blob migration attempted): a host-authoritative page store for town-shared letter boards / original designs. They remain per-player
  save data (diary PDATA machinery covers the diary only). Next milestone candidate.

## Patch 7 - indoor villager synchronization

ROOM_NPC (id 67, 28 B, unchanged size) now carries `flags` (was `_reserved`): bit0 TALK. The pose holder (host-arbitrated lease) samples position / facing / coarse action as before; a player who is talking to
the villager now ALSO samples (his pose carries TALK) and the host hands him the lease (a conversation is the visible truth); every other occupant applies the pose and holds the villager in `wait`
(`aNPC_pc_room_follow`). Presence (is_home / hide / forced schedule) is the N3 channel's job and is unchanged; the room's furniture / interior is the Patch 5 house canon.
Dialogue text, choices and the talk script stay local (by design: no full dialogue sync). A second player can still START a talk with a villager someone else is talking to (not blocked; follow-up).
Real two-process test: `tools/net_spike/test_room_npc_real.py` (host + client both walk into the same villager's house through `goto_other_scene` via the test hook `AC_TEST_ROOM_ENTER`, talk flag forced for a
window, leave and re-enter) 13/13. The talk flag is injected at the sampling seam (no GUI to run the dialogue); everything downstream is real code.

## Patch 8 - exclusive villager conversation lease (outdoor + indoor), conversation facing, idle look-at

One host-owned lease table, keyed by animal slot, serves outdoor AND indoor villagers (the M9-C talk hold became exclusive; no second system).
- Host: a BEGIN (NPC_TALK id 45) on a villager somebody else owns is DENIED (log `DENIED`); the host player's own talk goes through `pc_net_game_host_npc_talk_edge` (heartbeat every frame). Released on END, owner
  disconnect, the owner leaving the field / the villager's house (`pcnetgame_host_talk_hold_scene_left`), or the 30 s sweep. A restarted host starts empty (leases are not persisted).
- NPC_LEASE (id 70, 24 B, reliable, host -> every READY client, on change + at READY): the owner per slot. Clients refuse to start a talk with a villager another player owns (`aNPC_pc_talk_request_blocked`,
  one gate for NPC and NPC2) and a client that lost a near-simultaneous race ends its optimistic talk after 300 ms (`mMsg_request_main_disappear`; this abort path is NOT exercised by a real test).
- Facing: while a CLIENT owns the lease the host turns the held villager toward that player at <= 0x400 per frame (no per-frame network traffic: the owner is state, the pose stream carries the result).
  Idle: a head-look target at the nearest remote player when he is nearer than the local one (`pc_net_game_nearest_remote_player`, local presentation only).
- Tests: `test_npc_lease_protocol.py` (18/18, scripted clients, outdoor + indoor + disconnect + late join), `test_npc_lease_real.py` (9/9, TWO REAL processes, real dialogue: host talks, client refused, host ends,
  client's real talk accepted and finished), H7 of `test_client_villager_talk.py` updated to the exclusive semantics (the 'stopping the repeats -> EXPIRE' check is output-buffering flaky, pre-existing).

## Patch 6b - host-authoritative town-shared pages (notice board + Able Sisters designs)

Ownership: `Save.noticeboard[15]` and `Save.needlework.original_design[8]` are TOWN data (not per character); the host's Save is canonical. Per-character data (`my_org`, mail, diary) is untouched.
PAGE (id 71, 560 B, reliable, both directions): the host keeps a revision per page, rescans every 500 ms (its own player's edits bump the revision), pushes a changed page to every READY resident client and ALL
pages at join / reconnect (guests are never pushed: they keep their own town's pages). A client whose local copy differs from the canonical one it adopted sends ONE write built on that revision; the host
applies it iff the revision still matches AND the content validates (no control / message-tag bytes, sane palette / flag, exact length), else answers STALE / BAD with the canonical copy (the client's edit is
dropped and logged -- never a silent overwrite). Nothing is uploaded before the first canonical copy was adopted. Revisions are session-local; the CONTENT persists in the host save.
Tests: `test_town_pages_protocol.py` (21/21, scripted clients: initial state, apply, mirror, stale, race, invalid, independent pages, late join, reconnect, guest, host restart), `test_town_pages_real.py`
(8/8, real host + real client: the client's real dirty scan uploads its edit, the host applies it; the host's own edit is adopted by the real client). Not verified in the UI (Able Sisters / notice board screens).
Known limit: game logic that auto-writes a notice post locally on a client (day-change events) is detected as an edit and loses to the host's copy when stale.

## Collections - ownership decision (no code change)

`Private_c` is the CHARACTER record: the catalog bitfields (`furniture/wall/carpet/paper/music_collected_bitfield`), `aircheck_collect_bitfield` (K.K. songs), `my_org` (the character's own designs) and the fish / insect acknowledgement flags
(`complete_fish_insect_flags`) are per character in the vanilla game too, so they stay CHARACTER-owned (client-owned ranges of the D3 record, stored by the host). Town-wide collection state is the museum
(`museum_record`, host-owned since milestone R) and the museum-completion letter bits (host-owned bits, Patch 6). Nothing was moved to the host to make a table greener.

## Shop upgrade: announced 5-minute closure

**What the players see.** When the town's sales (and, for Nookington's, a foreign shopper) reach the next shop tier, the NEXT player who talks to Tom Nook -- a resident, a guest or the host's own player -- hears a new announcement instead of the normal greeting: business has been booming, he has been thinking about upgrading the shop, a bigger store would help everyone in town, the shop closes for 5 minutes while the work is done, everyone inside will be shown out (two acknowledgement choices "Sounds great!" / "All right."). Then "Splendid! I'll close up right away. See you in 5 minutes". Once the conversation is over everybody inside the shop (that player, other players, the host's own player) is walked out to the town through the shop's own exit data; the shop doors say "The shop is closed for its upgrade. Please come back in about N minutes"; a notice "Nook's shop is closed for 5 minutes for an upgrade" is drawn for ~8 s on every screen. After 5 minutes the shop is renewed (the next building, the new stock, the displaced items in the lost and found, the vanilla "new shop" leaflet in every resident's mailbox) and "Nook's shop has been upgraded and is open again!" is shown.

**Replaces the vanilla schedule (multiplayer hosts only).** Vanilla schedules a renewal two days ahead (`aSL_JudgeRenewShop`: `mEv_SAVED_RENEWSHOP`, `renewal_time`, a leaflet, the shop closed from `renewal_time - 1 day`) and performs it at a town load (`Shop_Level_Actor_ct -> aSL_RenewShop`). A HOST (listen or `--dedicated`) with the switch on never schedules it (`pc_shop_upgrade_host_replaces_vanilla()` in `aSL_JudgeRenewShop`); a vanilla schedule found in its save (an older build / single-player play) is taken over: event, `upgrading_today`, `send_upgrade_notice` cleared, the upgrade becomes PENDING. The closure leaflet ("closed on <date>") is not sent (Nook announces instead); the reopening leaflet (`mSP_SetRenewalChiraswhi_AppoDay`) is. Single player and every client keep the vanilla code (clients never renewed anyway: Stage 0).

**Switch.** `AC_MP_SHOP_UPGRADE` (host environment, read once): default ON; `0` / `off` / `false` = exactly the previous behaviour (vanilla 2-day schedule, `pc_host_renewshop_poll`). The host tells its clients through bit 0 of service 6 (a client of a switched-off host behaves as before).

**State machine (HOST, `SHOP UPGRADE` block at the end of `pc_net_game.c`).** IDLE -> PENDING (every 500 ms: `mSP_GetRealShopLevel() > mSP_GetShopLevel()` and a shop block exists) -> CLOSED (the first valid start; the closure is `PC_SHOP_UPGRADE_SECONDS` = 300 s of host time) -> RENEWING (the deadline) -> IDLE (the renewal happened and was published). The start needs PENDING + the matching generation / target, no restock running and `mSP_ShopOpen() == OPEN` (no sale / raffle day). Two players acknowledging at the same moment: the first request starts it, the second is ignored ("already started") and both dialogues show the closing row (the state says CLOSED with their generation). A failed start (timeout 5 s, link lost, an old host) shows "let me look over the plans a little longer, let's talk about it another time" and the shop stays open (the next talk announces again).

**Wire (v8 extended in place, protocol version unchanged).**
- Town service 6 SHOP_UPGRADE (16 bytes, `PC_NETGAME_TS_NUM` 6 -> 7): u8 state, u8 from_level, u8 target_level, u8 flags (bit 0 = the feature is on), u32 generation, u16 minutes of the closure left (rounded up; changes at most once a minute), u16 closure length in seconds, 4 zero bytes. Pushed on change and at READY (a late joiner / reconnect is told CLOSED). An older client logs "service 6 is reserved / unknown -- ignored".
- Message 85 SHOP_UPGRADE_REQ (client -> host, reliable, 8 bytes: msg, op 1 = START, target level, 0, u32 generation). No reply message: service 6 is the answer. The host requires a READY peer whose announced scene is a shop scene. An older host ignores the id.
- No new transaction kind / reason: a SHOP_BUY / SHOP_SELL / SHOP_RESTOCK during the closure is REJECTED `NOT_AVAILABLE` (16), nothing charged (host log: "the shop is closed for its upgrade").

**Closure on every process.** `mSP_ShopOpen()` returns RENEW while `pc_shop_upgrade_closed()` (host: its state; client: service 6) -- before the first-job override and the sale-event branch, so it is closed for everybody; `mSP_InRenewal()` likewise. The shop interior's restock exit latch (`ac_shop_indoor.c aSI_pc_restock_eject`) also fires for the upgrade (same rules: 0.5 s grace, never in the middle of a conversation, the existing hard cap of 18000 frames forces a stuck player out while still closed). The four doors show `PC_NOOK_MSG_UPGRADE_DOOR`.

**The 5-minute clock and the restart file.** The timer is the host's monotonic clock (`end_tick`), so a wall-clock change while the host runs does not move it. The absolute wall-clock deadline is written, BEFORE any client is told the shop closed, to `shop_upgrade.ini` next to `shop_restock.ini` (`servers/<id>/shop_upgrade.ini` on a dedicated server, `save/mp/shop_upgrade.ini` on a listen host): tmp file, `fflush` + `_commit`, atomic rename; rewritten at RENEWING; removed only after the renewed town was saved durably (`pc_save_write_authoritative_durable`). Format (text, `key = value`): `version = 1`, `town = <townkey>`, `gen`, `state = closed|renewing`, `from_level`, `target_level`, `total_ms`, `end_wall_ms` (unix ms). A restarted host resumes the remaining time (never longer than one closure: a wall clock that went backwards is clamped), renews at once when the deadline passed while it was down, ignores another town's file and drops the file when the save already has the new level. Not stored in the GCI (no save format change).

**The renewal (vanilla body).** In the town scene (the dedicated observer, a listen host outdoors): the vanilla due state is armed (event on, `renewal_time` = now) and `pc_host_renewshop_poll` does its same-position town reload once nobody is inside a shop building and the host player is idle, so `Shop_Level_Actor_ct -> aSL_RenewShop` renews exactly as vanilla (the live structure actor is torn down first; retried up to 3 times, then the shop reopens without the upgrade and it is not offered again in that host run). The poll now refuses a due VANILLA state that the upgrade did not arm (`pc_shop_upgrade_vanilla_renewal_allowed()`). In any other scene except a shop (a listen host in a house / the museum): `aSL_PC_RenewShopDirect()` runs the same body on the save's town field (no live town actor exists there). Inside the shop: wait (the host player is being shown out).

**Publication.** The shop acre is published from the host shadow with the AUTHORITATIVE values (`pcnetgame_shopup_publish_acre`): the structure tile `SHOP0 + level` (in the town scene the host's own tile holds RSV_NO / DUMMY_SHOPn of its live structure actor, which the diff never sends) and the ground-cleaned footprint RSV_NO (AMBIGUOUS values are otherwise withheld while the host is in the town) -- one FIELD_BLOCK, then SHOP + POLICE mirrors, then service 6 IDLE. A late joiner's snapshot carries the same shadow. A CLIENT standing in the town when the shop reopens patches its live shop structure actor's `npc_id` to the new tile (its teardown restore would otherwise write the OLD tile back) and does a same-position town reload when idle (`pc_town_same_position_reload`), so the new building and door appear; when its shop acre is not loaded it needs nothing (the field already holds the new tile).

**Console.** `shop-upgrade` prints the state (IDLE / PENDING / CLOSED / RENEWING, upgrade generation, level, qualifying level, sales, seconds left). `shop-upgrade start confirm` lets the operator start the closure of a PENDING upgrade (for a town whose players all run an older client that cannot hear the announcement). `shop-level-boost confirm` now explains that Nook announces the upgrade (refused while the town already qualifies).

**Test hooks (PC_TEST_HOOKS build + `AC_TEST_HOOKS=1` only).** `AC_TEST_SHOP_UPGRADE_SECONDS=<5..3600>` (closure length), `AC_TEST_SHOP_UPGRADE_QUALIFY=1` (host: the sales of the next tier once a peer is READY), `AC_TEST_SHOP_UPGRADE_HOST_START=<s>` (host: start the closure <s> s after PENDING = the host player's acknowledgement), `AC_TEST_SHOP_UPGRADE_CLIENT_START=1` (client: send the start request once PENDING and inside the shop).

**Tests.** `test_shop_upgrade_src.py` (17 source pins), `test_shop_upgrade_protocol.py` (real dedicated host + FakeClients: race, stale generation, not-in-shop refusal, purchases refused, late joiner, renewal waits for a player in the shop, acre publication, restart mid-closure resumed at the original deadline, operator start, switch off: 50/50), `test_shop_upgrade_real.py` (real dedicated host + a REAL guest client warped into the shop: Nook's real announcement dialogue answered by the autopilot, the request, the closure, the real exit, the renewal and the client's town refresh: 16/16), `test_shop_upgrade_listen_real.py` (real LISTEN host: renewal in the town via the reload, indoors directly on the save, the host's own player in the shop held while Nook's greeting is open, then shown out: 34/34), `test_shop_renewal_real.py` (the legacy phases now run with `AC_MP_SHOP_UPGRADE=0`; new RN-takeover). Re-pinned deliberately: `wire_baseline.py` (service 6 / array bound 7 / message 85), `test_stage0_town_safe_degrade.py` (m_shop.c include block), `test_shop_src.py` (push loop bound, the block's allowed Save_t.shop writes), `test_shop_restock_talk_src.py` T3, `test_map_line_src.py` L10, `test_events_src.py` / `test_hostcfg_src.py` (array bound), `test_boot_exec_gate_src.py` (allowlist), `test_shop_renewal_reload_src.py` R8.

**Limits / not verified.** The announcement, the door text and the notices were never looked at by a person or a screenshot (the dialogue ran in a real client, driven by the autopilot; text verified by the message selftest only). The host player's own announcement in a listen host was not driven through its dialogue (the host-start hook stands in for it). Mixed versions: an old client never hears the announcement and sees the shop open during a closure (its purchases are refused NOT_AVAILABLE); if every player is old the upgrade stays PENDING until the operator uses `shop-upgrade start confirm`. A player who keeps Nook's conversation open is shown out only when it ends (or at the existing hard cap). A listen host that stays inside a shop building delays the renewal until it leaves. The closure starts even if a resident is in its first-job chores (the chores continue after the reopening).

**Follow-ups (worker C).**
- (a) *Listen host indoors / busy.* Verified the combinations: host in the town (reload path), in another building (direct path), inside the shop itself (shown out, then the town path), clients in the shop (both paths wait). Fixed: an in-town attempt was counted every 15 s even when `pc_host_renewshop_poll` had not reloaded yet (the listen host's player in a menu / a talk, or a player still in a shop), so a host player busy for ~45 s lost the town its upgrade ("reopens WITHOUT the upgrade"). Now an attempt counts only after a reload was really issued (`pc_host_renewshop_reload_count()`, pc_m_card.c); while waiting the host logs "the renewal waits for the town reload" once a minute. The direct path (host indoors elsewhere) now also waits while a READY player is still inside a shop building (the old interior never meets the new level / lineup). A host that leaves during the closure is fine: the state and the deadline are host-global.
- (b) *Client in front of the shop during the renewal.* Verified with `test_shop_upgrade_door_real.py` (new TEST-ONLY `--nook-test warp,shopdoor`: the shown-out player stands in front of the shop like after a real door): the live structure actor is patched (its teardown restores SHOP0 + new level, never the old tile), the same-position reload builds the new shop, the local tile is the new one after the reload and the client WALKS INTO THE DOOR and enters the new interior (Nook 'n' Go): 13/13. No code change was needed.
- (c) *Leaflets.* The vanilla reopening leaflet goes to every house mailbox (residents get it through MAILBOX_LETTER; vanilla rule kept: a player still in the first job gets none). New: the same handbill is put into every guest's host-held mailbox (`pcnetgame_shopup_guest_leaflets`, the guest store written at once; house 0xFF on its client). `test_house_upgrade_protocol.py` H3: 12/12.
- (d) *Visibility.* PENDING now raises the on-screen notice "Tom Nook has news about his shop: go talk to him!" (~8 s) on the listen host's own screen and on every client (service 6 PENDING), besides the existing dedicated console line.

### Shop building on join

**Report.** On a dedicated server that went through the upgrade (Nook's Cranny -> Nook 'n' Go) the resident's and the guest's Nook's shop were different buildings.

**Evidence.** The host town GCI (`servers/default/town/card_a`): structure tile acre 1 tile 154 = `0x5805` (SHOP0 + 1), Shop_c level 1, the footprint tiles 120/121/135/136/151 = RSV_NO (ground clean). BOTH client saves (`bin_regular_client2`, `bin_guest_museum`: byte-identical copies from 10/3, a client never writes its town save) = `0x5804` / level 0, those footprint tiles still `0x0000` / `0x2800` / `0x0848`. No town cache is involved (no `save/mp/towns` on the guest). Client logs of earlier sessions on the same server: `snapshot tile parked acre 1 tile 154: local 0xFFFF host 0x5805 (local ambiguous in town)` (guest, 10/12); `FIELD_UPDATE acre 1 tile 154 = 0x5805 (seq 1)` long after the join (both clients, 10/13: the join snapshot had NOT carried the tile).

**Root cause (two halves).** (1) Host: the shadow init (`pcnetgame_host_init_shadow`) marks every non-STABLE tile unknown. The dedicated observer is parked at acre (3,1) next to the shop acre (2,1), so the host's live shop structure actor holds DUMMY_SHOPn / RSV_NO on the structure tile and the footprint holds RSV_NO (AMBIGUOUS, never learned while the host is in the town): a joining client got valid=0 for them and kept the tile of its OWN stale save. The renewal's `pcnetgame_shopup_publish_acre` fixed that only within the host session that renewed. (2) Client: the town scene and its shop structure actor are built from the local save before / while the snapshot lands; when the host's tile did arrive (snapshot or the later FIELD_UPDATE) it was PARKED behind the client's live placeholder (DUMMY_SHOP0 / RSV_NO in town) and the old building stayed until the shop acre unloaded (then the parked value was written and the next visit showed the new shop). Which client showed which building depended on whether its shop acre was loaded at that moment (the guest arrives at the station next to the shop; a resident may spawn elsewhere). The interior follows the building's door (Cranny door -> the Cranny interior), while Shop_c (service 3) already said level 1.

**Fix (ON by default; kill switch `AC_MP_SHOP_JOIN_SYNC=0` = the previous behaviour on both roles; protocol / wire unchanged).**
- HOST `pcnetgame_host_shadow_learn_shop()` (end of the shadow init): when the shop structure tile holds a placeholder, the shadow knows `SHOP0 + mSP_GetShopLevel()`; footprint tiles of that level holding RSV_NO and still unknown are known RSV_NO (exactly what `pcnetgame_shopup_publish_acre` sends). Log `[NET][SHOP] host: shadow: the shop structure tile acre A tile T is 0x.... (live placeholder 0x....)`.
- CLIENT (SHOP JOIN SYNC block, `pcnetgame_shopjoin_client_tick`, run from the shop upgrade client tick): once per session, after the first snapshot AND service 3 are applied (READY, the local world latched, no shop refresh running, not during a scene change), the building shown (the live structure actor's npc_id via `pc_town_shop_structure_npc()`, else the local structure tile) is compared with `SHOP0 + the host's level`. Match: `[NET][SHOP] client: shop building 0x.... matches the host's shop level N`. Mismatch: `[NET][SHOP] client: shop building 0x.... != host 0x....: refreshing (...)`; STABLE local tiles get the host's structure tile and the RSV_NO footprint (net-apply writes, display only); a live actor uses the shop upgrade's client refresh (re-tag its npc_id so its teardown restores the new tile, then ONE same-position reload when idle, reason "the shop building differs from the host's", bounded by the existing 10-minute limit, verify line 6 s later). Works for residents and guests alike, also against an older host (the level comes from service 3).
- Offline / single player / a host without clients: unchanged (the client part is client-role only; the host part only changes what the shadow knows).

**Tests.** `test_shop_join_sync_src.py` 17/17 (source pins), `test_shop_join_sync_real.py` 19/19 in ONE run (disposable `bin_fixture4_shopb_join`, w3 exe, Samsung, volume 1: the dedicated host upgrades the fixture town via the console and restarts -> host log `shadow: the shop structure tile acre 1 tile 154 is 0x5805 (live placeholder 0xFFFF)`; PROTOCOL: a fake client's join snapshot carries 0x5805; a REAL guest with the stale local save joins -> `snapshot tile parked acre 1 tile 154: local 0xFFFF host 0x5805` (the client half of the bug, reproduced) -> `shop building 0x5804 != host 0x5805: refreshing` -> one same-position reload -> `local structure tile acre 1 tile 154 = 0xF0F8 (shop level 1)` = the live Nook 'n' Go actor). Regression: `test_shop_upgrade_src.py` 17/17, `test_guest_arrival_hold_src.py` 20/20, `wire_baseline.py` 20/20.

**Limits.** No screenshot check of the building. Other structures behind live host placeholders (villager houses, mailboxes ...) never change level, so only the shop is learned; a client whose shop acre is loaded sees one black-fade reload after its arrival.

**Regression fixed: the guest spawned on the train tracks and was stuck.** The check runs right after the first snapshot (about +6.7 s). That is while the guest's own train arrival (RIDE_OFF_DEMO, train actions 0-4) is still running. The refresh reload then fired at about +7.4 s, while the player was riding the train. `pc_town_same_position_reload` only checked `mDemo_CheckDemo()` / `mPlib_able_submenu_type1`, and both pass during the ride. The reload clears the demo profiles ("never replay the arrival"), so the scene was rebuilt with the player at their position on the tracks and no arrival demo left to finish: no train action 3/4, no "control released". Fix:
- `pc_town_local_arrival_active()` (pc_m_card.c) returns 1 in any of these cases:
  - a RIDE_OFF_DEMO profile is pending in `demo_profiles[]`;
  - the RIDE_OFF_DEMO actor is alive (ACTOR_PART_CONTROL);
  - the player is in a demo main state (DEMO_WAIT..DEMO_WADE, the boat demos). The parked observer avatar is exempt.
- `pc_town_reload_moment()` is the shared "safe moment" check. It returns 2 while that arrival runs, 0 during a fade/wipe, menu, talk/demo or a busy player, -1 outside the town, and 1 otherwise.
- Every same-position reload waits for the arrival to end. `pc_town_same_position_reload` (shop refresh, shop join, day roll) uses `pc_town_reload_moment()`; the host shop-renewal reload and the G3.3 look reload check `pc_town_local_arrival_active()` directly.
- The client refresh in `pcnetgame_shopup_client_tick` logs once `[NET][SHOP] client: shop refresh deferred until the arrival finishes`. After `[JOIN] arrival: control released` it logs `the arrival finished: the shop refresh runs after 2000 ms idle in the town`. It then needs `PC_SHOPUP_REFRESH_SETTLE_MS` (2000 ms) of the safe moment without a break; any busy frame restarts the count. Only then does it do the ONE reload, with a black fade at the player's position (logged `refresh reload at the player's position (x,z)`).
- The verify line now also prints the player's position and the live shop actor's npc_id, and checks once more 6 s later while the new actor is still loading (tile RSV_NO, no actor yet).
- The check itself (field rewrite + re-tag) still runs at join time. It changes no scene state, and a mismatch with a live structure actor needs the reload anyway.
- The 10-minute limit is unchanged. The kill switch `AC_MP_SHOP_JOIN_SYNC=0` is unchanged.

Tests:
- `test_shop_join_sync_src.py` 26/26 (A1-A9 arrival-gate pins).
- `test_shop_join_sync_real.py`, run 2: 25/26. Fixture `bin_fixture4_sj_join`, port 35142. Porter's A is pressed by the TEST-ONLY autopilot `mash`. The log showed, in order: deferred during the arrival, train actions 3 -> 4, control released at +22.6 s, reload about 2 s later at (2220,840), and the player still at (2220,840) after the reload. The one FAIL was the old verify reading tile 0xFFFF 6 s after the reload: the new actor's bank was still loading. The guard log shows the teardown restored 0x5805. The verify now also prints the live actor's npc_id and the test accepts it; this was not re-run.
- `test_guest_g3_real.py` 44/44. The G3.3 look reload now runs after "control released", also at (2220,840).

## Guest museum donation (host-authoritative, donor nibble 5)

Status: **implemented, not committed** (protocol version 8 unchanged, no wire change). Guests (non-resident players with a guest record, record index `PLAYER_NUM + guest slot`) donate fossils,
paintings, fish and insects to Blathers through the existing MUSEUM_DONATE transaction (TXN kind 8); the G5.1 local refusal is gone.

**Save format (unchanged).** `Save_t.museum_display` (63 B) keeps ONE 4-bit donor per exhibit: 0 none, 1..4 resident slot + 1, 5 `mMmd_DONATOR_DELETED_PLAYER`; 6..15 fail the save check. A guest
donation is stored as **5**: it counts as donated everywhere (`mMmd_IS_DONATED`), the plaques show the vanilla "former resident" text (MSG_12192 / 12195 / 12197; every plaque indexes
`private_data` only for 1..4) and nothing is ever attributed to a resident.

**Host** (`pcnetgame_handle_host_ts_txn`): NO_DONOR_SLOT (reason 18) is kept only for `idx < 0` or a guest slot without a record (`pcnetgame_rec_txn_idx_ok`); everything else (READY / binding
gate, journal: fence / REPLAYED / CONFLICT, base, pre-image, the vanilla `mMmd_GetDisplayInfo` predicate, Blathers' two returned paintings, the post-image gate) is unchanged. The commit is the
new TARGET_PC helper `mMmd_PC_RequestMuseumDisplayDonor(item, donor)` (m_museum_display.c): the vanilla category ranges, the "not yet donated" test and the vanilla CLR + SET macros, with the donor
from `pcnetgame_museum_donor_nibble(idx)` (resident idx + 1 = the old vanilla value, guest 5). The `Common_Get(player_no)` swap around `mMmd_RequestMuseumDisplay` is gone (it would have
failed `mLd_PlayerManKindCheck` for a guest index). Races: the first commit wins, the other gets ALREADY_DONATED and keeps the item (two guests, guest vs resident). The museum service push
(service 2) mirrors the result to every client; the client already accepts nibble 5.

**Client.** `pc_net_game_ts_begin_museum_donate` is again the single `pcnetgame_ts_begin` call. Blathers (`ac_npc_curator_move.c_inc`): a READY guest client
(`pc_net_game_museum_guest_donor()`) is treated as "not foreigner" so it reaches the donation menu; the four "donated by you" tests use `aCR_IS_OWN_DONATION`, which on TARGET_PC never matches
for `player_no >= PLAYER_NUM` (a guest's `player_no + 1` IS 5, so every value-5 exhibit would otherwise read as its own): value 5 takes the DELETED_PLAYER row ("already donated", item handed
back, no transaction). An OLD host still answers NO_DONOR_SLOT, which the curator maps onto the existing give-back row (the item stays), so no capability bit is needed (none was added:
old clients reject unknown HOST_CONFIG bits).

**Manual-test bug (fixed): Blathers said "a former resident already donated it" and played the give-back for donations the host had APPLIED.** Root cause (client, every
network client, residents included -- pre-existing since town services milestone 1, made visible by guests): `aCR_msg_win_open_wait` re-derived the outcome row from the
LOCAL museum on every frame. The host sends the museum mirror of a donation right behind its TXN_RESULT and the client applies both in the SAME network poll, so on the APPLIED
frame the exhibit already read as donated: a guest got row 12..15 ("a former resident donated it"), a resident row 4..7 ("you donated it"); being < 16 the client branch was
skipped (the APPLIED result was never consumed) and the dialogue walked the give-back demo for an item the host had already taken out of the pocket (the item is NOT duplicated:
the hand-back demo never writes the pocket; the exhibit and the empty slot were correct, only the dialogue lied). The real-test log shows the race on 6 of 6 donations
(`re-derived now 14/15`). Fix: the row decided on the pre-donation museum is frozen at the begin (`aCR_ts_act_idx`) and restored on every awaited frame; the branch is also
entered while a transaction is awaited. Each decided row is logged (`[NET][TS] client: Blathers row for item ...`). Not reproduced: the report's "no transaction at all" for
two items (0x2302 / 0x2D00) -- in the real-dialogue run every offer of an exhibit the museum lacks begins a transaction; the most likely reading is that the misleading
give-back text made the earlier (applied) donations look refused.

**Durability caveat (crash window).** The host writes the GCI (museum_display) first and the guest store (`save/mp/guests/*.gst`, the guest's pockets) right after it, in the same save hook.
A host crash BETWEEN the two writes restores the museum WITH the exhibit and the guest record from the previous .gst, i.e. the guest still holds the donated item: an item duplicate is
possible, a double museum entry is not (the exhibit is already marked donated, a second offer is ALREADY_DONATED).

**Limitations / follow-ups.**
* No donor attribution for guests: value 5 does not say WHICH guest donated (the plaque says "former resident"). A sidecar (e.g. a new TS service 8 + `PC_NETGAME_TS_NUM` 9 -- service 7 is now the guest directory, see "Letters to guests"; service 6 is
  SHOP_UPGRADE) was deliberately deferred.
* Promotion (`pc_mp_promote_create`, guest -> resident): the guest's value-5 exhibits cannot be re-attributed to its new resident slot (no attribution data); they stay "former resident".
* A guest therefore never sees "you donated this" for its own exhibits, and the museum-completion letter stays resident-only (vanilla `mMsm_SetSendCompMail` walks `private_data[0..3]`).
* NO_DONOR_SLOT for a guest slot without a record is unreachable for a bound scripted client (a bound guest always has a record): source audited only.
* The Blathers dialogue with a guest was not driven through the UI (no UI automation for the dialogue): its guest branches are source audited; the real `--guest` client is driven by the
  default-off `--ts-test-donate` hook through the same entry point Blathers calls. Nothing was visually verified.
* Guest mailbox: implemented later, see [Guest mailbox via the post office](#guest-mailbox-via-the-post-office) and [Letters to guests](#letters-to-guests) (villager replies, letters from residents / guests, villager event letters, the museum completion letter).

**Real dialogue regression** (`test_guest_museum_real.py` phase B): a real `--guest` client with 3 fish + 3 insects (TEST-ONLY `AC_TEST_MUSEUM_POCKETS`), warped
into the museum entrance (`AC_TEST_MUSEUM_ROOM=entrance`), parked in front of Blathers (`AC_TEST_GOTO`, `ac_goto.txt` `c 40`), autopilot `mashf` (A presses, every choice
answered with its first option): all six offers take an accept row and are APPLIED, the observer's museum holds all six with value 5, six pocket slots emptied once.

**Tests** (fresh fixtures `pc/build64/bin_fixture4_museum*` with the `pc/build64_w3` exe): `test_guest_museum_src.py` (source audit), `test_guest_museum_protocol.py` (FakeClients vs a real
host: guest APPLIED with nibble 5 in the host museum service, the resident observer's mirror and the GCI after save; guest record pockets cleared; resend -> REPLAYED once; CONFLICT; two
guests racing and guest vs resident racing; ALREADY_DONATED / NOT_DONATABLE leave the record byte-identical; host restart -> GCI and guest store agree; kill between COMMIT and RESULT then
reconnect with the token -> pushed record without the item, the old COMMIT replays), `test_guest_museum_real.py` (a real `--guest` client + real host + a scripted resident observer).
Pins updated deliberately: test_guest_g5_src (B.1: the guard is now pinned as REMOVED), test_guest_src (A: the NO_DONOR_SLOT condition / no player_no write), test_ts_src (H: the helper
replaces the player_no swap), test_guest_protocol (X: a guest's apple is now NOT_DONATABLE, not NO_DONOR_SLOT).
Results (exe `pc/build64_w3/bin/AnimalCrossing.exe`, fixtures bin_fixture4_museum / _museumh): test_guest_museum_src 32/32, test_guest_museum_protocol 52/52, test_guest_museum_real 20/20;
resident regressions test_ts_protocol 110/111 (TS2 "no service 6+" pin predates this change: SHOP_UPGRADE is service 6), test_ts_real_client 22/22, test_museum_bits_protocol 7/7,
test_museum_refresh_real 14/14; test_ts_src 72/72, test_shop_src 65/65, test_stage0_town_safe_degrade 104/104. Failures in test_guest_g5_protocol (M5 catch: WORLD_CHANGED),
test_guest_protocol (guests.dat v2 vs the per-guest store, crashes in phase R) and test_guest_g4_protocol (max-guests 9 CLI / cap) are outside the museum path; their museum checks pass.

## Guest mailbox via the post office

Status: **implemented, not committed** (protocol version 8 unchanged; wire: reason 38 + MAILBOX_LETTER house 0xFF, no new message id; guest store file v3 -> v4). The user's request: guests get a
mailbox -- "use the NPC at the post office and have an option to access your mail; and whenever they buy a house the mail migrates over to the mailbox at the house".

**Storage (host only).** A guest owns no house, so its letters wait in a HOST-held 10-slot mailbox per guest slot (`s_guest_mbox[g].box`, a growable table next to `s_guest_rec`). It is
persisted in the guest's own `.gst` file as the **v4 extension block**: header (version 4) | id | the unchanged 9352-byte entry | `u32 ext_len` (2988) | `u32 ext_version` (1) | `u32 mbox_mask`
(bits 0..9) | 10 x 298 bytes (canonical big-endian letters, an unused slot all zero) | CRC32 over everything. The mailbox and the guest's carried letters (`mail[]` of the record) are therefore
changed by ONE atomic file write (no duplicate / lost gift across a crash). The writer emits v4 **only while the mailbox holds a letter**; an empty mailbox is written as plain v3 (byte-identical
to the previous build), and a v3 file reads as an empty mailbox. Parse rules: an unknown `ext_version` is VERSION_FUTURE (never reinterpreted), a wrong `ext_len` SHAPE, mask bits above 9 /
an unused slot with bytes / a used slot without bytes FIELD; the file digest folds the mailbox in (`pc_mp_gs_ext_digest`: unchanged for an empty mailbox, so no v3 guest is rewritten at the
upgrade). The host-local counters (today's letters sent, the day of the last villager-reply pass) are **memory only** (deviation from the first plan: persisting them would keep every guest that
ever wrote a letter in v4; a host restart resets the daily count).

**Downgrade (known, accepted).** An OLDER build refuses a v4 file as "a newer version", moves it aside to `<id>.gst.corrupt-<time>` and (no other readable generation) runs its guest store
UNTRUSTED: no guest is admitted, residents are unaffected; its log says "UNTRUSTED MODE: the guest store is unreadable or inconsistent ... remove the affected guest files AND their *.corrupt-*
files". Nothing is deleted. Only guests that hold unread mail at that moment are affected (all others are v3). **This build heals it**: at load, a preserved `*.corrupt-*` file of a guest
that has no readable generation and parses cleanly now is copied back as the primary (the preserved copy is kept; log `RESTORED guest file ... an older build had set it aside`). If the older
build loaded an older v3 generation (.bak) instead, that state wins and the letters of the newer v4 generation are lost (it is the downgrade cost).

**Delivery into a guest mailbox** is host-only and direct (`pcnetgame_guest_mbox_deliver`): the post office cannot be used (its load sweep deletes letters whose recipient owns no house,
`mPO_receipt_check_mail` refuses them). Today's sources: the villager replies (below) and the TEST-ONLY hook `AC_TEST_GUEST_MAIL_SEED=<n>[,<gift hex>]`.

**Copy to the guest client.** MAILBOX_LETTER (56, unchanged 316-byte layout) with `house = 0xFF` (PC_NETGAME_MBOX_HOUSE_GUEST) = the guest mailbox; the same digest / seq / all-10-slots-at-READY
scheme as a resident's house mailbox (`pcnetgame_host_guest_mbox_push`). The client keeps a SEPARATE copy (`s_gmbox_view`, never `homes[]`); a house-0xFF slot is refused by a non-guest, a
house slot by a guest. **An older client** refuses house 0xFF harmlessly ("house 255 is not the house this player owns", nothing written: it owns no house).

**Taking a letter.** MAIL_TAKE (kind 13) with a guest branch: the source is the guest mailbox slot, the same hash / gift / font / recipient checks and reasons (NO_SUCH_LETTER / MAIL_CHANGED with a
re-push, MAILBOX_FULL), the commit writes the guest MIRROR `mail[dst]` (`pcnetgame_rec_txn_write_mail`) and clears the mailbox slot, then **the .gst file is written durably BEFORE the journal
APPLIED and the RESULT**; a failed write undoes both halves and answers REJECTED BUSY. Journal / replay / CONFLICT are unchanged.

**UI.** The post office girl (`ac_npc_post_girl.c_inc`): for a READY guest whose guest mailbox the host fed (`pc_net_game_guest_mail_offer_available`; an older host / the switch off never
feeds it, so no row) the greeting row and the "anything else?" row get **"Check my mail."** inserted before their last choice (`pc_post_mail_msg_arm` -> `pc_nook_msg_patch`, the same
mechanism as the dresser's "More storage"; the choice mapping runs before the vanilla switch). With letters waiting the window closes and the VANILLA mailbox overlay opens
(`mSM_IV_OPEN_MAILBOX`); its own automatic transfer moves the letters (one MAIL_TAKE each); after it closes the girl says "There you go! Any letters you leave here will stay safe with us. / Please
come again!" (`PC_NOOK_MSG_POST_DONE`). Nothing waiting: "Let me check... I'm sorry, there's no mail for you right now. ..." (`PC_NOOK_MSG_POST_NOMAIL`). Every `Common_Get(now_home)->mailbox`
use of `m_mailbox_ovl.c` / `m_tag_ovl.c` goes through ONE helper `pc_ui_mailbox()` (`pc_guest_mail.h`): the guest copy for a guest client, else the vanilla `now_home` mailbox or NULL
(now_home is never faked; NULL-guarded). The overlay closed at the post office requests no mail-jump landing (`pc_ui_mailbox_is_guest`).

**Guest -> resident letters.** MAIL_SEND (kind 12) is open to guests: the G5.1 client guard and the host refusal are gone; the letter is read from the guest mirror record
(`pcnetgame_rec_priv_ptr`), no sender house is needed, the recipient must be a resident with a house on the host (NO_SUCH_ADDRESS), the sender is forced to the guest key. A host-local daily
limit per guest (`AC_MP_GUEST_MAIL_PER_DAY`, default 5, 1..100) answers **MAIL_LIMIT (38)** beyond it (the post girl's "can't take more letters" row; the letter stays).

**Villager replies.** `mNpc_RemailForSink` (m_npc.c) is the reply generator with a sink callback (`mNpc_RemailFor`, the resident post office path, is byte-identical); the host pass
`pcnetgame_host_guest_remail_tick` runs it for every READY, SYNCED guest once per host calendar day per guest slot (growable table), delivering into the guest mailbox. A full mailbox stops that
guest's pass with `send_reply` still set (retried on a later day), without affecting other guests; a delivery writes the store at once.

**Promotion (house purchase / console `promote`).** `pcnetgame_promote_exec` hands the guest mailbox to `pc_mp_promote_create` (`pc_mp_promote_set_guest_mailbox`, one-shot); after the house
is assigned and the record is migrated each letter is re-keyed (`pc_pid_rekey` of recipient / sender), an illegal gift cleared, and placed into the new house mailbox, else the post office, else
the new resident's carried letters, else the whole promotion is rolled back (never dropped). The rollback snapshot now holds the post office (the house mailbox is part of the home). The guest
table keeps its copy until the guest entry is retired after the durable save (its file is moved aside, kept), so a refused / rolled-back purchase loses nothing and a retry starts from the
same state. The new resident gets the letters through the normal MAILBOX_LETTER path. In practice the assigned house's mailbox is always EMPTY (vanilla house init), so with <= 10 letters the
overflow branch is not reachable (source audited only).

**Pre-existing defect fixed (found by the promotion test).** `trademark_cleanup` (m_trademark.c) cleared every house mailbox of the in-memory save. On PC the save was just re-read by
`trademark_init`, and a dedicated host starts its world from that in-memory save without another reload, so **every house-mailbox letter (residents' too) vanished at each host start**.
The clear is now skipped when `pc_save_loaded` (the vanilla Start path re-reads the save anyway; no save loaded: vanilla).

**Switches.** `AC_MP_GUEST_MAIL=0|off|false` (host environment, read once, default ON) turns it all off (MAIL_SEND / MAIL_TAKE answer NO_DONOR_SLOT as before, no mailbox push, no replies).
`AC_MP_GUEST_MAIL_PER_DAY`. Test hooks (PC_TEST_HOOKS build + `AC_TEST_HOOKS=1`): `AC_TEST_GUEST_MAIL_SEED`, `AC_TEST_GUEST_MAIL_SEED_REPLY=1`, `AC_TEST_GUEST_MAIL_REPLY_EVERY_PASS=1`; client
`AC_TEST_MUSEUM_ROOM=postoffice`, `ac_goto.txt` `g <dist>` (park in front of the post girl), autopilot `mashm` (A presses, choice "Check my mail." / cancel, B closes the overlay; new pad bit B).

**Tests** (fresh fixtures `pc/build64/bin_fixture4_gmail*` with the `pc/build64_w3` exe; Samsung display, volume 1):
`test_guest_mailbox_src.py --native` 39/39 (audits A..H incl. "host code never indexes private_data[] / homes[] with a guest index" + the native guest store selftest 96/96 with the v4
group: round trip, v3 as empty, block rules, CRC, lazy v3 -> v4 -> v3 rewrite, downgrade healing); `test_guest_mailbox_protocol.py` S 12/12 (guest -> resident letter via the forced vanilla
delivery, daily limit, NO_SUCH_ADDRESS), T+R 24/24 (house-0xFF sync, take APPLIED + the v4 file, REPLAYED, CONFLICT, NO_SUCH_LETTER / MAIL_CHANGED re-push, MAILBOX_FULL, host restart: mailbox XOR
mail[]), K 7/7 (kill between COMMIT and RESULT, reconnect, replay, the letter once in the file), V 9/9 (full mailbox keeps the reply owed, after a take the reply lands in the guest mailbox);
`--promotion` 19/19 (dedicated host: 5 letters, `fail_save` rollback with the guest file intact, retry applies once, house 3 holds the re-keyed letters, the guest file retired, the promoted
resident receives them as MAILBOX_LETTERs after a host restart -- this check found the trademark bug); `test_guest_mailbox_real.py` 18/18 (REAL `--guest` client + real host: house-0xFF copy,
warp into the post office, the real post girl dialogue with the patched greeting chosen by the autopilot, the overlay's own transfer took 3 letters through 3 MAIL_TAKE, the conversation ended,
the .gst written back as v3 with the 3 letters in mail[]).

**Limits / follow-ups.** Resident -> guest letters and event / birthday / goodbye / museum letters to guests: DONE, see [Letters to guests](#letters-to-guests); the villager reply's `send_reply` lives in the GCI and the reply in the .gst: a crash between the two saves can duplicate a reply (its gift); the daily send count is memory only;
nothing was looked at by a person (texts checked by the message selftest only); the overlay was driven by its automatic transfer (taking one chosen letter by hand was not exercised).

## Letters to guests

Status: **implemented, not committed** (protocol version 8 unchanged; wire: TOWN_SVC_STATE **service 7 GUEST_DIR** (324 B, array bound 8); no new message id, no new reason; **no
persistent-format change** beyond the guest store v4 of "Guest mailbox via the post office"). Follow-ups (A) letters from residents (and guests) to guests, (B) villager birthday / event /
goodbye letters to guests, (C) museum letters to guests -- all delivered into the guest's host-held mailbox (`pcnetgame_guest_mbox_deliver`), copied to a connected guest at once
(MAILBOX_LETTER house 0xFF), taken at the post office as before.

**Who is an address (deliverable).** `pcnetgame_guest_mail_target(g)`: a used guest entry of the host's CURRENT town that is not disposable -- confirmed (its client presented the stored
token) or holding accepted data (record rev > 0: a first-session guest that has played; such an entry is never re-minted / evicted, so its mailbox cannot be thrown away) -- whose record
PersonalID is the key. A bare claim that never synced (rev 0, unconfirmed), a removed guest, another town's guest or a never-seen key is NOT an address (NO_SUCH_ADDRESS).

**The GUEST DIRECTORY (TOWN_SVC_STATE service 7).** Chosen over a new message id 86 because the town-service mirror already gives exactly what a directory needs: a per-service seq (strictly
increasing for the life of the host process; a client ignores <=), an FNV digest, the ordered reliable push to every READY peer at join and on every change (the 500 ms digest poll), no
save needed on the client, and **an older client ignores an unknown service** ("service 7 is reserved / unknown -- ignored", HEAD's handler) -- a new message id would have needed its own
seq / late-join / reconnect logic. Blob (324 B): `u8 count (0..16)`, 3 zero bytes, 16 x 20-byte PersonalID (the guest KEY: name, land name, player_id, land_id; raw little-endian PC image,
host == client build), newest guest first, unused entries zero. Built from the guest table with the deliverability rule (listed == deliverable); **not built at all with
`AC_MP_GUEST_MAIL=0`** (never valid, never pushed). The client validates it (count, reserved / unused zero, listed keys real) and keeps it for the session (`s_gdir_client_blob`). More than 16
guests: only the 16 newest are listed (the address book page holds at most 8 rows anyway); the host still delivers to any deliverable key.

**Address book** (`m_address_ovl.c mAD_make_player_address`, TARGET_PC): the player page lists the other residents, then the directory's guests (never the local player), then the museum.
The page is drawn for at most 8 rows and the museum entry takes two (its gap), so the guests fill `8 - residents - (museum ? 2 : 0)`; `player_mail_name` is enlarged to 8 entries under
TARGET_PC. Solo / an older host / the switch off: no guest listed (vanilla). **Post office girl**: on a client a PLAYER letter passes the local check when a house OR a directory guest matches
(the host re-checks at MAIL_SEND); the HOST's own player (a non-dedicated host's resident) gets the same delivery locally (`pc_net_game_host_guest_letter_check` -> 0 / 2 = "mailbox full",
`..._post` instead of `mPO_receipt_proc`, sender forced to the host player, receive font).

**MAIL_SEND to a guest (kind 12).** After `mMl_hunt_for_send_address` finds no house: `pcnetgame_guest_mail_recipient` (switch on, PLAYER recipient, `pcnetgame_guest_find` = this town,
deliverable). Before anything changes: no house and no guest -> NO_SUCH_ADDRESS; a guest's letter to itself -> NO_SUCH_ADDRESS; no free slot in the guest mailbox -> MAILBOX_FULL; a GUEST
sender beyond its daily limit -> MAIL_LIMIT. Commit: sender forced to the bound identity, receive font, the letter into the free slot, the sender's mirror slot cleared through
`pcnetgame_rec_txn_write_mail` (the gift travels inside the letter, exactly like resident -> resident: it left the pockets when the letter was written), rev++, journal APPLIED (replay /
CONFLICT unchanged), the mailbox refresh (a connected recipient gets it on the next mailbox tick). Durability: a **guest** sender's slot and the recipient's mailbox both live in guest
store files -> written durably BEFORE the journal (a failed write undoes both halves, REJECTED BUSY); a **resident** sender's slot lives in Save_t -> the recipient's file is written with
the next host save (the post-save guest store write; at most the 60 s periodic save), so a host crash before that save loses both halves together (the letter is still in the sender's
pockets) instead of duplicating the letter and its gift. Guest -> guest is allowed (same rules, the daily limit applies). **Keying**: recipient = the guest KEY (its home PersonalID); a
reply from the guest is an ordinary letter (the resident is in its address book). At promotion the guest's mailbox letters move to the house mailbox re-keyed (`pc_pid_rekey`, unchanged);
letters others hold FROM the guest keep the old key (harmless: AC has no reply-to); a resident's unsent letter still addressed to the promoted guest's old key gets NO_SUCH_ADDRESS (the post
girl hands it back).

**Villager event letters (B).** Defined exactly from the code:
* **birthday** -- `ac_event_manager.c mail_event_check` (on a day change, the vanilla cursor window `(event_save_common.last_date, today]`) calls `mNpc_SendEventBirthdayCard2` for each
  resident whose birthday is in the window: every villager whose HIGHEST-friendship memory is that player sends a card with a RARE present (furniture / clothing / umbrella).
* **event** -- the same pass sends the **Christmas card** (Dec 25 in the window, same year, <= a week late; FTR_FAMICOM_COMMON01, festive paper) and schedules **Valentine's Day**
  (Feb 14 10:00, `valentines_day_date`), fired by `vt_wt_mail_check` -> `mNpc_SendVtdayMail`: each villager writes to its best friend of the OTHER sex with a present typed BEST_FRIEND /
  OK_FRIEND (>= 80) / NOT_FRIEND. (White Day exists only in the JP enum; the US code sends none.)
* **goodbye** -- `mNpc_SetGoodbyAnimalMail` (a villager leaves: forced removal, a transfer replacing one, the return-animal swap) -> `mNpc_SendGoodbyAnimalMail`: the goodbye letter
  (0x20E + looks * 3 + random(3), no present) to every resident.

Guest passes (host, `pcnetgame_host_guest_event_tick`, switch on, world + guest table ready): `mail_event_check` hands the window over **before the cursor moves**
(`pc_net_game_guest_event_window`); for every deliverable guest the birthday (from its record) and Christmas are checked with the SAME date rule (`pc_guest_event_due.h`, pure) and the cards
are built by the vanilla builders (`mNpc_GuestBirthdayCardsForSink`, `mNpc_GuestXmasCardForSink`: the guest is the villager's highest-friendship memory, keyed by the guest key; the vanilla
