`birthday_present_npc` exclusion -- the villager visiting a resident's door -- does not apply to a guest). Valentine: right after the vanilla pass (`pc_net_game_guest_vtday_due`), each
villager picks its best friend of the other sex AMONG the guests (`mNpc_GuestVtdayMailForSink`, vanilla tie and type rules). Goodbye: inside `mNpc_SetGoodbyAnimalMail` the departing
villager's 7 memories are COPIED (`pc_net_game_guest_goodbye_note`, before every caller clears the Animal_c) and the guests it has a memory of get its goodbye letter (residents keep the
vanilla "every resident"; a guest it never met gets none). **Dedupe without a new save format**: birthday / Christmas / Valentine use the residents' own dedupe -- the host's mail-event
cursor (`last_date`, `valentines_day_date`, persisted by the host save): the guest letters are persisted with the NEXT guest store write (the post-save write at the latest), so a host that
dies before saving re-sends residents' and guests' letters together at most once and never keeps a guest letter whose cursor move was lost. Goodbye is one departure (the Save_t change
itself). A full mailbox: birthday / Christmas / Valentine letters are dropped (vanilla: a full mailbox AND a full post office drop them too); a goodbye letter waits in a bounded memory queue
(8 departures, retried every minute; vanilla keeps its `deliver_to_bitfield` in memory too). **TODO.md "villager memory holds 7"** applies: guests compete with residents for the 7 memory
slots, so a villager can forget a guest (no birthday card / goodbye letter for it).

**Museum letters (C).** `m_museum.c`: the information letter (museum opened: `mMsm_OrderInformationMail` / `mMsm_SendInformationMail` -> it gives the "Museum" address), the result letters
(answers to letters TO the museum carrying a gift: `mMsm_SendMuseumMail` records the gift in the SENDER's `museum_record` resolved through `mPr_GetPrivateIdx`, then `mMsm_SendResultMail`),
the fossil letters (a fossil sent by letter), and the **completion letter** (`mMsm_SendCompMail`: every section complete -> 0x22F + FTR_NOG_MUSEUM, deduped by the host-owned state_flags bits
SCHEDULED / RECEIVED). Decision: only the **completion letter** goes to guests (`mMsm_GuestCompMailForSink`, host pass every minute: the museum is complete, the guest has not received it;
the two host-owned bits of the GUEST's own record are set only when the letter was taken, the host-field digest refreshed (rev++) and a bound client pushed, the .gst written at once: letter
and dedupe in one file). Guest donations are recorded as donor 5 ("deleted player"), so a guest's museum share cannot be attributed: the letter goes to every deliverable guest, like the
vanilla gives it to every resident. Not sent to guests, with evidence: the **result / fossil letters** answer letters TO the museum with a gift, and such a letter is still refused for EVERY
network client by the post girl (`ac_npc_post_girl.c_inc`: client + MUSEUM + gift -> row 1; the limitation still matters: `mMsm_SendMuseumMail` resolves the sender through
`mPr_GetPrivateIdx`, which never finds a guest, and a client's letter never reaches the host's museum path at all -- making it work needs a host transaction for museum letters); the
**information letter** would only add the "Museum" address book entry, whose only use (a gift letter) is refused for clients.

**Kill switch.** `AC_MP_GUEST_MAIL=0` also turns off: the directory (service 7 never exists), letters to guests (NO_SUCH_ADDRESS as before), the host player's local delivery, every event /
museum letter pass. Test hook (PC_TEST_HOOKS build + `AC_TEST_HOOKS=1`): `AC_TEST_GUEST_EVENT_MAIL=<birthday,xmas,vtday,goodbye,museum>` (once, for the first synced deliverable guest: a
villager becomes its best friend, then the REAL passes run with a forced window / a noted departure that does not remove the villager / the museum counted complete).

**Tests** (fresh fixtures `pc/build64/bin_fixture4_gletters`, `_glettr` with the `pc/build64_w3` exe; Samsung display, volume 1): `test_guest_letters_src.py --native` 31/31 (audits I..N +
the date rule 11/11 natively); `test_guest_letters_protocol.py` D/A/G 24/24 (the directory at a resident and a guest, newest first; resident -> guest with an apple: APPLIED, pushed at once,
the guest file NOT written by the commit and written by the next periodic save; MAILBOX_FULL; REPLAYED; retry after a take; NO_SUCH_ADDRESS for a never-seen key; guest -> guest written
durably at once; a letter to oneself refused), E + E2 15/15 (birthday, Christmas, Valentine, goodbye, museum letters in the guest mailbox; the museum bits in the .gst; after a restart the
museum letter is not sent twice), K 4/4 (the switch: no service 7, NO_SUCH_ADDRESS, no event letter); `test_guest_letters_real.py` 20/20 (REAL `--guest` client + real host + a scripted
resident: the real client applied service 7, received the 4 event / museum letters and the resident's letter with an apple, then took all 5 at the post office through the real overlay).
Not exercised automatically: the address book UI itself (listing guests on a real client: source audit only), the host player's local letter to a guest, a real Valentine's / birthday day
change (the date rule is unit-tested; the passes ran with forced windows).

## Able Sisters trades, villager outfits, museum letters with a gift, guest donor names (worker B)

**Able Sisters (Mable).** The shop's eight designs stay host-authoritative town pages (PAGE 71, kind DESIGN). New on top of Patch 6b:
- *Trade journal (client, no wire change).* An Exchange (the player's `my_org[k]` and the shop design swap) or a Display done at Mable on a network client is journaled around the
  vanilla `aNI_Exchange*` / `aNI_Copy*Data` call in the shop actor (`pc_able_net.h`; the live refresh and the ct copies are not trades). The page write of a traded design is built on the
  revision the player SAW (`s_pgc_seen`: set only while the shop's double buffer equals the Save, i.e. what the mannequins show; a host push adopted while a menu deferred the redraw is not
  "seen"). APPLIED settles it; STALE / BAD / a superseding host push / a reconnect reset roll the `my_org` slot back to the player's own design (only if the slot still holds what the trade
  put there) and log `[NET][ABLE] trade rolled back (...)`; the shop then redraws the host's copy. A link loss rolls back (worst case the player keeps a copy of a design that also reached the
  shop; designs are copyable, nothing is lost). A local edit of any page that loses to a newer host copy is now logged `SUPERSEDED` instead of being overwritten silently.
- *Gate.* Mable refuses an Exchange / Display (her vanilla cancel line "Oh, I see.", nothing requested) while the client is not READY, the previous trade is open, a design page was not
  adopted yet or a design write is in flight. Take-a-copy only writes the character and is not gated. (Mable has no vanilla "come back later" line for this menu; the cancel line is the one
  that leaves every state untouched.)
- *Guests.* The host now pushes every page to guests too (a guest plays a never-persisted copy of THIS town) and accepts a guest's DESIGN page write (vanilla visitors trade at the Able
  Sisters) with the same 20 writes/s limit and content checks; the notice board stays resident-only (BAD). A guest's `my_org` is its own record (client-owned range, uploaded like a
  resident's); the journal protects it the same way. `AC_MP_GUEST_PAGES=0` restores the old rule (no pages for guests).
- *Villager outfits (msg 90 VILLAGER_OUTFIT, host -> every READY client, reliable, 128 B: count 15 + 15 x {npc_id, cloth, cloth_original_id, umbrella_id}).* When the host applies a design
  write that changes a slot it runs the vanilla trend reset (`mNpc_SetDefAnimalCloth` / `_Umbrella` for every villager wearing that shop design) -- the trading client's own reset only touches
  its local copy. The host sends the outfit table at READY and on every change (1 Hz digest). A villager with a LIVE actor is skipped (host: the reset waits; client: the outfit waits): an NPC
  actor writes its drawn outfit back into `Save_t.animals` at its save (`ac_npc_save.c_inc`), which would undo the change; it is retried every second. An old client ignores id 90.

**Museum letters with a gift (TXN kind 19 MUSEUM_MAIL; reasons 42 MUSEUM_FULL, 43 MUSEUM_MAIL_OFF, 44 reserved).** Before, a client's museum letter with a gift was refused at the post
office. Now the post girl / `m_tag_ovl.c` treat it like a letter to a player: the letter stays in its slot, `pc_net_game_mail_begin_send` sends kind 19 (the MAIL_SEND tag: slot, gift echo,
24-bit hash; the letter is read from the host's mirror). The host (`pcnetgame_handle_host_mail_txn`, same gate / shape / journal / base / pre-image / letter checks) runs the museum's side on
THE SENDER's record (resident: `private_data[idx]`, guest: its guest record via `pcnetgame_rec_priv_ptr`) with the sender's donor nibble (`mMsm_PC_MuseumMailFor`: a fossil is stored for
appraisal, anything else is donated (`mMmd_PC_RequestMuseumDisplayDonor`) or recorded for return), never `Common_Get(player_no)`; a full record (30 fossils / 30 pending results, where vanilla
drops the gift) is refused MUSEUM_FULL and the letter stays. The mirror slot is cleared, rev++, journal, RESULT; `museum_record` is host-owned (residents get PUSH_HOSTFIELDS, a guest a
full push). Durability: a guest's letter + record are in its `.gst` file (written at once) unless the museum display changed too (a donation): then with the next host save, like MAIL_SEND.
The result letters follow the vanilla day pass: residents unchanged (`mMsm_DepositFossil` on the host); guests get the same pass (`mMsm_PC_GuestMuseumDay`, hooked into
`mMsm_DepositFossil`): result letters, appraised fossils and the information letter go into the guest's host-held mailbox (3 per pass, a full mailbox stops the pass), the information letter
marks the guest `contacted` = the Museum entry of its address book. Letters + records: one durable guest-store write. `AC_MP_MUSEUM_MAIL=0`: refused, no guest pass. Old host: kind 19 is
refused (the post girl's "no such address" row; the letter stays).

**Guest donor names (host sidecar `museum_donors.dat` + town service 8 MUSEUM_DONORS, 284 B).** Guest donations keep donor nibble 5 (no save-format change). The host records which guest gave
an exhibit (exhibit 0..119 -> guest key, `pc_museum_donors.c`, a pc_tabfile: magic "MDON", version 1, aux = town hash; atomic tmp -> MoveFileEx; a corrupt file is refused and left
untouched; listen host `save/mp/museum_donors.dat`, dedicated `servers/<id>/museum_donors.dat`, next to `work_jobs.dat`). An entry is only meaningful while that exhibit's nibble is 5;
orphans are dropped at load and before every write. Service 8: u8 count (<= 8 distinct guests), 3 zero bytes, 120 name indices (0xFF = none), 8 x PersonalID (native image). Plaques (fossil,
painting, fish, insect) show "donated by <guest>" (MSG_12191 / 12194) instead of "a former resident"; Blathers uses the named-donor row for another guest's exhibit and the "you already gave
me this" row for the viewing guest's own (rows < 16: no transaction starts). The guest -> resident promotion (`pc_mp_promote_create`) re-credits the guest's attributed exhibits to the new
resident (nibble slot + 1) inside the promotion; the 63-byte `museum_display` is part of its rollback snapshot (a rolled-back promotion restores nibble 5 and the attribution).
`AC_MP_MUSEUM_DONORS=0`: no attribution. More than 8 distinct attributed guests: the rest read "former resident" (documented limit).

**Wire / format summary.** msg 90 VILLAGER_OUTFIT; TXN kind 19 MUSEUM_MAIL, reasons 42 / 43 (44 reserved); TS service 8 MUSEUM_DONORS (array bound 9); new host sidecar
`museum_donors.dat`. No GCI / `.gst` / records format change. Protocol stays v8 (extended in place).

**Tests.** `test_able_trade_protocol.py` 14/14 (outfit table at join, host trend reset + new table, guests pushed 23 pages, guest design write applied + mirrored, guest notice write BAD,
guest stale write STALE); `test_able_trade_real.py` 18/18 (REAL host + REAL client in the shop, `AC_TEST_ABLE_TRADE`: exchange APPLIED = a real swap; stale-base exchange -> host STALE ->
`trade rolled back`, `my_org` restored, the mannequin redrawn); `test_town_pages_protocol.py` 21/21 (P7 re-pinned: guests are pushed, their notice write is BAD);
`test_museum_mail_protocol.py` 35/35 (guest fish -> DONATED nibble 5 + service 8 name at guest and resident + sidecar file; REPLAYED; PRECOND for a player letter / no gift; fossil stored ->
appraised fossil returned; apple returned; info letter + contacted; in-person donation attributed; resident letter -> nibble slot + 1; restart reloads the names);
`test_museum_donor_promotion_protocol.py` 19/19 (dedicated host: `fail_save` rollback keeps nibble 5 + name, the retry re-credits nibble 4, GCI checked, the orphan dropped at the next load);
`test_museum_mail_real.py` (REAL client `--mail-test-send=museum,gift`: kind 19 committed with the resident's nibble, APPLIED, slot empty); `museum_donors_selftest.c` 152/152;
`test_able_museum_src.py` 28/28. NOT verified: Mable's dialogue with the gate on screen, the post girl rows on screen for a museum letter, the plaques / Blathers text with a guest name on
screen, a real day change (the pass ran through `AC_TEST_MUSEUM_DAY=file`), MUSEUM_FULL (30 letters) by a process test (source audited).

## House upgrades via Nook (residents; worker C)

**What the players see.** A resident playing on a client now gets Tom Nook's vanilla house offers and announcements instead of the plain greeting (Batch G3 kept them away because every write was local): with the loan paid off Nook offers the bigger house (roof colour choice) / the basement; the house is built the next day (on a host that stays up: as soon as the house is free, see "Construction"), and the next visit to Nook announces it ("your house is done!") and sets the new loan (Medium 148,000, Large 398,000, Upper 798,000, basement 49,800 Bells), payable at the post office as usual; with the Upper house paid off Nook orders the statue. While the host answers, the vanilla text waits at its continue point (choices) or Nook says "Ah, <name>! Hello, hello! Just a moment, let me check my records, hm..." (talk start); a refusal shows "Hm, I'm afraid I can't take care of that just now. Nothing has changed", a busy house "my records for your house are being updated right now, ask me again in a moment", a lost link "the line went quiet ... I cannot say whether it went through".

**Authority.** Every write is the HOST's: the `homes[h]` header (size / next_size / renew / statue / basement flags, order date, ordered roof), `Save.num_statues` (town-global, serialised on the host thread: two residents ordering statues at the same moment get distinct ranks) and the resident's loan (client-owned record range 0x0088.., so it is written in a transaction that moves the record lineage). The client NEVER writes `homes[]`: the header reaches it through the house sync canon (CANON_PUSH, header included); on APPLIED of an "it was built" acknowledgement it sets its own loan to the same deterministic value and adopts the host's record base (epoch, rev, cdig). Paint (`next_outlook_pal` + a purchase + the pending flag) stays refused on a client (deferred: it needs a bells debit in the same transaction).

**Wire (v8 extended in place, no version bump, no new message id).** TXN_COMMIT kind 20 HOUSE_UPGRADE (one-phase, like 14..18): `tag.aux_cond` = op 1 ORDER_SIZE / 2 ORDER_BASEMENT / 3 ORDER_STATUE / 4 ACK_BUILT, `tag.aux_item` = ORDER_SIZE's roof palette (0..mHm_OUTLOOK_PAL_NUM-1, else 0), `tag.flags` = the stale-header guard of the client's copy (size bits 0..2, has_basement 3, renew 4, basement_ordered 5, statue_ordered 6, next_size == size 7), dest NONE / slot 0 / item 0, the usual pre-image + base. Reasons: 45 HOUSE_NOT_ELIGIBLE (the vanilla preconditions fail on the host's data), 46 HOUSE_BUSY (a house / record transfer of this player in flight), 47 HOUSE_CHANGED (the guard differs from the host's header; the newest canon is pushed). 48 reserved. Guests: PRECOND (not journaled). Malformed: BAD_SHAPE + one record violation.

**Host handler** (`pcnetgame_handle_host_house_upgrade_txn`, "HOUSE UPGRADE" block of pc_net_game.c): READY / binding / SYNCED gates -> shape -> journal (fence, replay, CONFLICT) -> base + pre-image -> the idempotence answer (the state already carries exactly this op, e.g. a resend after a host restart lost the in-memory journal: APPLIED REPLAYED, nothing executed) -> HOUSE_BUSY -> guard -> the vanilla preconditions (aNSC_start_wait / aNSC_set_talk_info_start_wait: an order needs no loan, nothing pending, size < Upper, size == next_size; a Large house without a basement may only order the basement; the statue needs the Upper house paid off; the acknowledgement needs renew or a built statue) -> vanilla's own field writes -> a DURABLE save (`pc_save_write_authoritative_durable`; a failed save rolls the header / num_statues / loan back and answers FAULT) -> APPLIED -> the house canon refresh (pushed to every client). Orders do not touch the floors (safe while somebody is inside); the dedicated console says "Tom Nook took a house order ...".

**Construction** (`pcnetgame_hu_host_tick`, 1 Hz on the host). Vanilla builds an order only at a game load (`mHm_CheckRehouseOrder` from `mSDI_StartInitAfter`), so an order on a host that stays up never happened. The host now builds a due order (order date != today) of a house nobody uses: not the host's own player inside / entering / leaving it (`pcnetgame_local_house_unsafe`), no READY player announcing a room of it, no owner commit of it in flight (else "DEFERRED: ..." once a minute). The body is `mHm_PC_RehouseOne` (m_home.c, TARGET_PC): the per-house body of `mHm_CheckRehouseOrder` (same branches, date test and writes incl. the main floor's wall / door markers) WITHOUT the all-houses outlook copy and WITHOUT the state_flags loop, and a no-op once built. Then a durable save and the canon push ("Tom Nook's construction is finished" on the dedicated console). The host's own house is built the same way. A network CLIENT's boot never builds an order in its copy any more (`mHm_CheckRehouseOrder` skips the houses for the client role): the host's canon is the only source.

**Client** (`pc_house_upgrade.h`). `pc_house_upgrade_available()` = a READY resident client with its own house, house sync on, the host announced town service 6 (the capability proxy: HOST_CONFIG is full), not latched "unsupported". `pc_house_upgrade_begin(op, arg)` waits for a CLEAN record (AWAIT_CLEAN like MAIL_SEND, bounded 5 s: the host's preconditions read its mirror loan, which must equal the client's) and begins the kind-20 transaction (resends / 60 s cap / session reset are the shared X1 machinery); `pc_house_upgrade_poll()` / `_last_reason()` / `_last_sent()` drive the dialogue. An old host (kind 20 unknown) answers BAD_SHAPE: the client latches "unsupported" for the session (at most one record violation per session) and keeps the plain greeting; a host older than service 6 never sees kind 20.

**Dialogue** (ac_npc_shop_common.c). `aNSC_start_wait`: without the capability the G3 divert stays (plain greeting); with it a talk start that must write (renew -> ACK_BUILT, a built statue -> ACK_BUILT, the Upper house paid off -> ORDER_STATUE, a Large house without a basement -> ORDER_BASEMENT) becomes `aNSC_WAIT_TYPE_PC_HOUSEUP` (the wait row, `aNSC_PC_HU_BEGIN` / `_PENDING`, then the vanilla row: MEDIUM / LARGE / UPPER / BASEMENT / STATUE_BUILT, REHOUSE_STATUE, REHOUSE_BASEMENT); DONE_REHOUSE keeps its vanilla offer and its three write sites (`aNSC_check_roof_col_order`, `_order2`, the basement choice of `aNSC_check_col_chg_or_make_basement`) call `aNSC_pc_hu_choice_step` instead: the message is LOCKED at its continue point (`mMsg_Set_LockContinue`) while the order runs, APPLIED unlocks it (the vanilla follow-up row), a refusal replaces the follow-up with a PC row (`PC_NOOK_MSG_UPG_FAILED / _LINKLOST / _BUSY`, appended rows 41..44, `PC_NOOK_MSG_COUNT` 45). Nook calls the player (the REHOUSE talk) only when the player ENTERS the shop (vanilla: the entry action).

**Test hooks (PC_TEST_HOOKS + AC_TEST_HOOKS=1).** Host `AC_TEST_REHOUSE_DUE=1` (an order placed today counts as due: built within ~1 s); client `AC_TEST_HOUSE_UPGRADE_SEQ=<op>[:<arg>],...` (drives `pc_house_upgrade_begin / _poll` without the GUI; op 9 = the loan paid off locally); `--nook-test warp,resident` (the warp into Nook's shop for a resident) and `warp,shopdoor` (the exit in front of the shop); `AC_TEST_CLEAR_ERRANDS=1` now also clears the first-job events of a CLIENT's own save (the fixture residents are still in the first job, which replaces Nook's rehouse talk).

**Tests.** `test_house_upgrade_protocol.py` (real dedicated host + FakeClients): H1 NOT_ELIGIBLE with a loan, HOUSE_CHANGED, BAD_SHAPE, the order + canon, REPLAYED resend, CONFLICT, construction DEFERRED while the owner announces its room then exactly one construction, ACK_BUILT rev + 1 and the 148,000 loan, guest PRECOND, the whole path to Upper for TWO residents and their simultaneous statue orders with distinct ranks, the statues built and acknowledged, the GCI after a graceful stop (sizes, loans, num_statues = 2): 36/36; H2 an order, a host restart, the identical resend answered REPLAYED from the state: 12/12; H3 the shop follow-ups (c)/(d): 12/12. `test_house_upgrade_real.py` (listen host + REAL resident client): R1 the hook-driven real path (pay, order, construction, ACK with the loan on both sides, pay, next order): 10/10; R2 the link lost in flight (`--txn-fault=drop_result`): REJECTED reason 0 sent 1 = the LINKLOST row, the order executed once: 5/5; R3 the REAL Nook dialogue driven by the autopilot: the roof-colour choice went through the choice seam (message held, APPLIED, the vanilla follow-up), the construction, the client re-entering the shop got the PC_HOUSEUP talk start (ACK, then MEDIUM_BUILT) and the 148,000 loan: 12/12. `test_house_upgrade_src.py` 31/31; the generated rows by `test_work_menu_patch.py` (pc_nook_house_selftest). Re-pinned deliberately: `test_batch_g1_g6_client_guards.py` (G3 divert now conditional), `test_txn_src.py` (the s_ctxn begin site list), `wire_baseline.py` + net_spike_lib (kind 20, reasons 45..47).

**Limits / not verified.** The rows and the vanilla messages were never looked at on screen (the flow is asserted from the logs). The exterior of a house built while a player stands in the town changes at that player's next town load (the house actor reads the size when it is created, vanilla). A house whose owner stays inside it is not built until it leaves (it would rebuild the live room). Paint at Nook stays refused on a client. The capability proxy (service 6) cannot tell a host built between the shop upgrade and this feature: such a host costs one BAD_SHAPE / record violation per session. The listen host's own player keeps the vanilla local flow (its orders are serialised with the clients' on the host thread).

## Player-vs-player tool hits

**What the players see.** Hitting another player with a tool now gets a reaction. The **axe** makes the other player trip (vanilla TUMBLE, then TUMBLE_GETUP) and slide a short way away from the attacker. The **shovel** gives the vanilla dismay shock (SHOCK 99: GAAAN1 -> GAAAN2, held ~1.4 s). The **net** gives a short flinch (STUNG_MOSQUITO 107: the MOSQUITO1 arm slap, ~1 s, then straight back to WAIT: the PvP entry carries `PC_PVP_FLINCH_LABEL`, so the vanilla "I got bitten" NOTICE_MOSQUITO dialogue never starts). Both play IN PLACE in the victim's own facing (user feedback 18:13 build: no turn toward the attacker; the shock's target angle is the victim's own rotation, the flinch has no turn step), no push. The net also stops on the player like it stops on a solid object (STOP_NET + the AMI_HIT sound). Rods, umbrellas and fans never hit anyone. It is **reactions only**: no item, Bell, pocket, axe-wear or field change, no damage. It is **always on**: there is no host switch and no per-player opt-out (the only off switch is the TEST-ONLY `AC_TEST_PVP_OFF=1` inside a `PC_NET_TEST_HOOKS` build). Residents and guests are treated the same. It works only in the **town field**: interiors never have hits.

**Vanilla before this change (verified at runtime, S0).** With the feature off (`AC_TEST_PVP_OFF=1`, the same build), an axe swung at an adjacent puppet came out as REFLECT_AXE (the states were 7 -> 39 -> 7) and the axe wear (Common `axe_damage`) went from 0 to 3, because `Player_actor_Check_axe_after` reflects off any collided actor except a ball and the puppet pipe is in the collision table. A shovel dug a hole under a puppet: vanilla refuses only within 39 units of a *villager*.

**Local fixes, no wire change.** `Player_actor_Check_axe_after` (m_player_common.c_inc, TARGET_PC) ignores a collided puppet (`pc_pvp_is_puppet_actor`: the actor's mv / dw procs are the puppet's), so the swing becomes a plain AIR_AXE (or chops a tree behind the player) with no recoil and no wear. `mPlib_Check_scoop_after` (m_player_lib.c) answers AIR_SCOOP within 39 units of a visible same-field puppet, right after the villager check (also keeps the inventory's "bury into this hole" from a puppet's tile).

**Wire (v8 extended in place, no version bump).** `91 PVP_HIT_REQ` client -> host only, reliable, 16 B (`PCPvpHitReqMsg`: target wire id, tool 1 axe / 2 shovel / 3 net, swing seq, the attacker's facing and x/z at the contact). `92 PVP_HIT` host -> the **victim only**, reliable, 12 B (`PCPvpHitMsg`: attacker wire id, tool, reaction 1 TUMBLE / 2 SHOCK, knock angle, host seq, knock speed 0..4). Old peers ignore both ids. No new TXN kind / reason, no town service, no HOST_CONFIG bit. The structs live in `pc/include/pc_pvp_authority.h` (pinned by `test_pvp_src.py`; `wire_baseline.py` lists 91 / 92 as documented extras).

**Attacker.** The attacker's own process detects the contact with its own geometry against the visible same-field puppets (`pc_pvp_puppet_targets`), never with the OCC result: the axe triangle (35 units ahead, +-8 deg, at y+31, vs the 16-unit puppet pipe) at frame 15 of SWING / AIR / REFLECT_AXE; a 39-unit circle one tile ahead at frame 13 of AIR / REFLECT_SCOOP; the net head segment from `Player_actor_HitBGCheck_Swing_net` (frame > 6; the puppet pipe is withheld during SWING_NET). One request per swing, at most one every 600 ms. A client sends PVP_HIT_REQ; the host's own player goes through the same host validation locally.

**Host validation** (`pc_pvp_host_validate`, pure): a valid tool and position; attacker and target are real READY players (the host's own player only when it is not the dedicated observer; never the attacker itself); both announced the town field; both have a last-known position; the attacker's MOVE stream showed a swing state of that tool within 600 ms (`pc_pvp_host_note_motion` from the MOVE handler / the host's live state); the claimed position is within 40 units of the attacker's last MOVE position; the target within 60 units (height 50) of the attacker from the host's own positions; the target inside a +-60 deg cone of the claimed facing (not below 12 units); neither player entered the town field within 10 s (arrival / scene-change protection); a newer swing seq; 700 ms per attacker; a victim cannot be hit again for 2 s. Rejections are logged with the reason (`[NET][PVP] host: hit by A on T ... rejected (reason N NAME)`).

**Victim.** The client handler only queues the hit. The local player's own move (`Player_actor_move_other_func1`, pre hook in m_player.c) consumes it only in WAIT / WALK / RUN / DASH / TURN_DASH / READY_NET / READY_WALK_NET and with no demo, no open submenu, no pause (Esc menu included): talk, menus, fishing, doors, beds, chairs, cutscenes, wading and pitfalls are never interrupted; an unconsumed hit expires after 700 ms; at most one reaction every 1.5 s locally. The real vanilla states are requested (`Player_actor_request_main_tumble`, static in m_player.c, priority 14 / `request_main_shock_all` start time 0). A held balloon turns a TUMBLE into a SHOCK (TUMBLE_GETUP would release it). After the TUMBLE was entered (post hook) the knockback is set: speed 3.5 along the angle away from the attacker (the tumble keeps the speed and brakes it).

**Other players** see the victim through its MOVE action state: TUMBLE / TUMBLE_GETUP already had puppet rows; SHOCK now has one (GAAAN1 STOP -> chain GAAAN2).

**Test hooks (PC_TEST_HOOKS + AC_TEST_HOOKS=1).** `AC_TEST_PVP_OFF=1` (feature off in that process); autopilot commands `face X Z` (a positioning aid), `swing` (one real A press once the player stands; answers with the main indexes the swing went through and the axe wear), `pvpstat` (wallet, equipped item, axe wear, an order-independent pockets+equipment digest, the PvP counters). `[NET][PVP] victim trace` lines (test builds) show the victim's speed after a reaction.

**Knockback.** Vanilla brakes a tumble by 0.175 per RENDER frame (not scaled by dt), so at a high frame rate a slide set by speed alone was only ~4 units (measured). The victim hook instead eases the speed from 7.5 to 0 over 24 frames at 60 fps (`PC_PVP_KNOCK_AXE` / `PC_PVP_KNOCK_FRAMES`), ~45 units at any frame rate (measured 43.4); walls and fences stop it as usual (one measured slide was 6.5 units against an obstacle).

**Tests.** Native `pvp_selftest.c` 212/212 (geometry, every reject reason, victim allowlist / balloon / TTL / interval, attacker contact). `test_pvp_src.py` 50/50 (wire baseline incl. ids 91 / 92 only, the pinned structs, axe filter, shovel AIR_SCOOP, net hook, hook placement, allowlist, balloon guard, no inventory / wallet / wear / field writes, the SHOCK row, no switch, the selftest builds). `test_pvp_protocol.py` 25/25 (real host + 3 FakeClients: PROTECTED, NO_SWING, SCENE, BAD_TARGET x2, RANGE, CLAIM_FAR, CONE, the valid hit delivers exactly one PVP_HIT to the victim only, RATE, OLD_SEQ, IMMUNE, victim-is-host TUMBLE, MALFORMED / ignored traffic). `test_pvp_real.py --s0` 34/34 (real host + real client + real observer client, all swings real A presses via the autopilot: S0 vanilla reflect + wear 3; A's axe -> the host trips (AIR_AXE, no wear); the host's axe -> A trips (host-local path, only A receives 92); shovel -> SHOCK with AIR_SCOOP (no hole); net -> STOP_NET + SHOCK; a paused host is not interrupted (the hit is dropped); the observer sees row=tumble / row=shock; wallet, pockets+equipment digest and axe wear unchanged on both).

**Limits / not verified.** Nothing was looked at by a person (animations, slide, sound). The hit is decided on the attacker's view (the host tolerates 60 units). The attacker's swing looks like a whiff even when the host rejects it. Old clients are never hit (they ignore 92); an old host ignores 91. A bee / axe-bounce shock shows on a puppet without its WAIT1 lead-in.

**Revision (user feedback on the 18:13 build): distinct shovel / net reactions, no turn.** Candidates looked at: SHOCK 99 (GAAAN dismay; a turn-toward step via its target angle -> neutralised), STUNG_MOSQUITO 107 (MOSQUITO1 arm slap; vanilla chains into the NOTICE_MOSQUITO dialogue -> skipped for the PvP label), STUNG_BEE 96 (HATI swelling face side effect -> rejected), NOTICE_BEE 97 (AnimationMove root motion, a dialogue -> rejected), TALK's BIKU1 startle / GAAAN (only reachable through talk demo orders, no own main index, so a puppet could not mirror it -> rejected), TIRED 85 (endless panting loop -> rejected), the KOKERU tumble (the axe's). Chosen: shovel = SHOCK held to 84 frames (~1.4 s), net = STUNG_MOSQUITO (~1 s). Both already had puppet rows (`shock`, `stung_mosquito`), so other players see the matching distinct animation. A safety net returns a flinch to WAIT after 3 s. `test_pvp_real.py --s0` 39/39 (adds: SHOCK main 99 and FLINCH main 107, facing before == after for both, flinch back to WAIT without 108, SHOCK 1405 ms > FLINCH 1004 ms, observer rows shock / stung_mosquito), `test_pvp_protocol.py` 25/25, `test_pvp_src.py` 55/55, `pvp_selftest` 215/215.

**Revision 2 (user feedback on the 18:38 build): the reactions' faces and sounds reach the other players.** Found: MOVE carries no eye / mouth field and the puppet always drew eye pattern 0 / mouth pattern 0 (pc_remote_player_dw). Vanilla sets the face two ways: the per-frame eye / mouth index tables of the CLIP (`mPlib_Get_PlayerEyeTexAnimation_p` / `_MouthTexAnimation_p`, used by `Player_actor_set_tex_anime_pattern`) and a few constant patterns some states set (SHOCK's GAAAN2 6/5, NOTICE_MOSQUITO / TIRED 4/4, STRUGGLE_PITFALL eye 6, SWING_FAN eye 5, WAIT_BED eye 2). `pc_remote_player_face_pattern` now derives both from what the puppet already mirrors (its own clip and frame + the state row): no wire change, and it is general (every clip with a face table: the tumble KOKERU, GAAAN1, MOSQUITO1, the axe / shovel swings, ...). Blinking is still not reproduced. Sounds: the puppet already played the tumble SE (sAdo_Get_KokeruLabel) and effects; new puppet tool rows: SHOCK spawns the vanilla shock effect at frame 5 (ef_shock plays its SE 0x2D itself at the puppet), STUNG_MOSQUITO plays the sting SE NA_SE_6A on entry (vanilla plays it from the mosquito actor, which only the stung player's process has); the PvP flinch victim plays NA_SE_6A locally too. All positional (sAdo_OngenTrgStart at the puppet, the usual puppet range / per-frame caps), so the attacker and every bystander hear the reaction at the victim. The attacker's own net hit keeps its local AMI_HIT; third parties hear the victim's reaction (AMI_HIT itself is not relayed). No new message id. Evidence: `test_pvp_real.py` 50/50 (the attacker A and the observer B log the tumble / shock / sting sounds of the victim's puppet and its changing face patterns, e.g. tumble eye 0 -> 1 -> 2 -> 6 / mouth 3 -> 5, flinch eye 1/2/7, mouth 3/4), `test_pvp_src.py` 60/60.

## Hotfix 0.2.1: guest arrival hold

**Field report (0.2.0).** Remote guests (never the guest playing on the host's own PC) were stuck at the train tracks after joining: no arrival train, no control; rejoining sometimes helped. The dedicated server's log showed the guest READY but never the line `[TRAIN] remote arrival of player N: starting the local arrival train` (printed when the guest's puppet first stands in the train). The guest's own items / Bells looked gone only because the record push had not been applied yet: the host copies of every guest were intact (no host-side zeroing path exists).

**Cause.** A guest's own client HOLDS its arrival (`ac_ride_off_demo_move.c_inc` `aROD_first_set` -> `pc_remote_player.c` `pc_remote_arrival_join_query` -> `pcarr_join_decide`) in DEMO_WAIT at the train start (1970, 760) until it knows whether another player is arriving (the shared train arrival): while the link is in HANDSHAKE, while the host's first world snapshot is not applied (`roster_pending`), and while another town player has a known scene but no MOVE yet (`unknown`). That hold had **no time limit**. Suspected (not reproduced, see Tests): over real latency the first world snapshot could take very long or restart from scratch again and again: a snapshot is paced (reliable backlog < 32 of the 64-message window) and any live world send that did not fit the window while the snapshot ran restarted it (field commits, wildlife, snowmen, meta), while 0.2.0 added join-time bulk pushes (23 town pages of 560 B six per frame with no backlog check, villager outfits, guest mailbox letters) that filled the window during the snapshot. On localhost acks return within a frame, so it never showed there. A second hole: the `unknown` count used the frame clock, which restarts with every GAME_PLAY, so a scene accepted in an earlier GAME_PLAY could read as "just accepted" for a long time.

**Fixes (no wire change, protocol 8).**
1. **The hold is bounded** (`pc_remote_arrival_logic.h`, pure + natively tested): `PCARR_JOIN_HOLD_READY_MS` = 4500 ms after the link reached READY, or `PCARR_JOIN_HOLD_TOTAL_MS` = 15000 ms since the hold began (any link state), whichever comes first. The arrival then starts with what is known (an arrival seen at the station / riding is still joined, otherwise the normal arrival); one log line `[TRAIN] arrival join: hold timed out after N s (4.5 s after READY | 15 s in total; link=..., roster_pending=..., unknown=..., others=...) -> starting the arrival`. The clock is the net layer's monotonic ms clock (`pc_net_game_now_ms`); a hold not polled for more than 1 s starts over. A hold that ends normally (the roster arrives in time) is unchanged. Accepted consequence: a guest released by the limit may ride its own train while another player is still arriving (two independent trains, as before the shared arrival existed); the world snapshot still arrives and applies afterwards.
2. **An unknown slot counts only for `PCARR_JOIN_UNKNOWN_WINDOW_MS` = 3000 ms after READY** (wall clock), so a quiet or lagging other player cannot hold a joiner.
3. **Join-time bulk pushes gated** (`pc_net_game.c` `pcnetgame_host_join_push_ok`): town pages, villager outfits (msg 90) and mailbox letters (guest and resident) go to a peer only while it has no world snapshot under way and its reliable backlog is below `PC_NET_RELIABLE_WINDOW / 2` (32), re-checked before every message; nothing is lost (each keeps its per-peer sent state and resumes). TOWN_SVC_STATE (at most 8 messages of <= 340 B, only on a seq change; HOST_CONFIG must precede the snapshot) is unchanged.
   **NOT in 0.2.1: the snapshot-restart throttle.** A snapshot restart from scratch on any live world send that does not fit a mid-snapshot peer's window (field commits, wildlife, snowmen, meta) is still the 0.2.0 behaviour. A version that deferred such restarts to SNAPSHOT_END and sent undeliverable field commits as 'owed' live FIELD_BLOCKs was written and then removed before release (lead decision): no runtime test ever reached a full window mid-snapshot, so those paths were never exercised, and the hold limit above already releases the guest whatever the snapshot does. Candidate for a later release once a reproduction exists.
4. **Diagnostics.** `settings.ini` `[Debug]` `client_log = 1` (default 0; env `AC_CLIENT_LOG=1` overrides, `AC_CLIENT_LOG=0` forces off) sends the game's stdout / stderr to `client_log.txt` next to `AnimalCrossing.exe` (appended, rotated to `client_log.old.txt` at 4 MB; never for `--dedicated`, which has `servers/<id>/logs`). A `[JOIN] +<ms since transport-connect>` stage trace: transport connected, identity sent, READY, world snapshot begin / RESTARTED / END ignored / first snapshot applied, record push adopted, arrival hold (every 2 s while held: link, roster_pending, unknown, others, stopped, riding), arrival decided (reason), arrival train actions 0..4, control released; host side: snapshot begin / END sent (duration) / RESTARTED. Test-only: `AC_TEST_ARRIVAL_ROSTER_STALL=1` (test builds + `AC_TEST_HOOKS=1`) makes the roster never count as known, to exercise the limit.

**Tests.** `test_guest_arrival.py` (native selftest, + 11 hold checks incl. three simulated timelines), `test_guest_arrival_hold_src.py` (source pins: thresholds, wiring, throttle, switch default off, trace), `test_guest_arrival_latency_real.py` (real host + guest A direct + guest B through a UDP relay with injected delay / loss; `--stall` uses the test hook). The 0.2.0 exe did NOT reproduce the stall through the relay in the test fixture (150 ms / 2 % and 300 ms / 8 %: snapshot applied after 0.6-1.4 s, no restart), so the root cause on the user's server is not proven; the hold limit is the guarantee whatever the cause, and the `[JOIN]` trace / `client_log` are there to catch it in the field.

## Day rollover on a long-running host

**Problem (TODO WS-01).** A dedicated server (and a listen host that never leaves the town) did not progress from one day to the next: villager move-in / move-out, the day's mail, the boot-time event init (special / weekly events, `mEv_init_force`), fish locations, beach shells, the gyroid / train / island bookkeeping and above all the overnight field growth stayed as they were at boot. (The weather, the daily event schedule and the Stalk Market DO move on while running, see below.)

**What vanilla does, and when (source evidence).**
- **At boot / continue only** (`m_start_data_init.c` `mSDI_StartDataInit` -> `mSDI_StartInitFrom|Pak` -> `mSDI_StartInitAfter`): `mEv_init_force` (the day's schedule incl. special / weekly events), `mSP_ExchangeLineUp_InGame` (shop line-up), `mNpc_SetRemoveAnimalNo` (who announces a move-out), then `mNpc_ForceRemove` (move-out), `mTM_renewal_renew_time` (compares `Save renew_time` with today's date; on a new date it stores today and sets ALL renewal flags: `mTM_RENEW_TIME_WEATHER`, `mTM_RENEW_TIME_DAILY`), `mEv_ClearEventInfo`, `mEnv_DecideWeather_NormalGameStart` (the weather roll; consumes the WEATHER flag), `mMl_start_send_mail`, `mPO_first_work`, `mNpc_Grow` (move-in), `Kabu_manager`, the villager lists / schedules / walks, `mNpc_Remail` (replies), `mPr_SendMailFromMother`, `mNpc_SendHPMail`, museum mail, `decide_fish_location`, `mFI_SetFirstSetShell` (beach shells), `mMsr_FirstClearMushroom`, `mSP_SetTanukiShopStatus`, wind range, climate, island restore, ... (Correction found in the first real run: `mTM_renewal_renew_time` is ALSO called by `mTM_time()` at the end of every frame, so `Save renew_time` and the renewal flags follow the calendar while running; `Save renew_time` therefore cannot tell whether the boot init ran for today.)
- **At every SCENE_FG field build** (`m_field_make.c` `mFM_FieldInit` -> `mAGrw_RenewalFgItem`, PC: `mFM_PcFieldInitGrowth`): the overnight growth (trees, flowers, weeds, buried fossils / haniwa, money rock, shine spot, turnip spoil, carp) once per **6 AM** boundary after `Save all_grow_renew_time` (or at once on a backwards clock), plus Christmas-tree lights, snowman melt, Wisp weed clear. A player who walks out of a building after 6 AM gets it; the dedicated observer never re-enters the town scene, so it never happened there.
- **While running** (every frame): `mTM_time` -> `mTM_renewal_renew_time` (new date: renew_time + ALL renewal flags) and `Kabu_manager` on a date change; the weather actor (`ac_weather.c`, outdoors) re-rolls the weather on the WEATHER flag (the host's observer stands outdoors, so the weather already changed at midnight; WORLD_META carries it); `mEv_run` re-derives the day's event schedule at midnight (`event->day != day`: `mEv_RenewalDataEveryDay`, weekly events, `update_schedule_today`); the postman (`mPO_business_proc`) delivers at his hours; the `ac_event_manager.c` mail-event cursor (birthday / Christmas letters). These already worked on a long-running host.
- **PC host paths already present, reused as they are:** field growth marks every acre dirty -> FIELD_UPDATE / snapshot resync (`mFM_PcFieldInitGrowth`); villager move-in / move-out are hooked (`pc_net_game_notify_villager_arrival|departure`, msgs 18/19); weather, renew time, Stalk Market and fish/bug terms are diff-polled into WORLD_META (msg 16); the shop line-up is the TOWN_SVC SHOP mirror; resident house mailboxes (MAILBOX_LETTER, msg 56) and guest mailboxes (`pcnetgame_host_guest_mbox_push`) are diff-pushed; residents' / guests' villager replies already run on a host calendar-day change (`pcnetgame_host_remail_tick`, `pcnetgame_host_guest_remail_tick`); a departing villager's goodbye letters reach guests (`pc_net_game_guest_goodbye_note`). Clients follow the host clock (CLOCK_SYNC, msg 27; a host `time_delta` change is broadcast as a DISCONTINUITY). The same-position SCENE_FG reload of the shop renewal (`pc_host_renewshop_poll`) is the precedent for a host reload while clients stay connected. Shop restock (TXN kind 15) is separate and untouched.

**Design (chosen: a controlled same-position town reload that runs the boot init in between).** Calling the boot routines on the live host (option 2) is unsafe: `mNpc_InitNpcList` / `mNpc_SetNpcList` / `mNpc_ForceRemove` rewrite the villager lists the live NPC actors point into, `mEv_init_force` re-rolls the schedule under live event actors, `mSDI_StartInitAfter` allocates from the zelda arena of a play, and the growth only exists as a field-build step. So the host does what a vanilla "save, quit, continue" does, inside one scene change:
1. **Detection** (`pc_day_roll.c`, pure, natively tested), only with role HOST (dedicated observer or listen host), never single player / clients; ON by default, kill switch `AC_MP_DAY_ROLL=0`:
   - **DAY** due = the game's own date (`Common time.rtc_time`, the game clock incl. `time_delta`, so time zone / DST / a manual clock follow the game's own date logic) differs from the date of the host's **last new-day init** (process memory: the boot's init, taken at the first world-ready frame, then every roll). No file is needed: every process start IS a vanilla boot init, so a restart (also mid-roll) re-does exactly what vanilla does and starts counting from that day;
   - **GROW** due = the vanilla 6 AM rule on `Save all_grow_renew_time` (the next 6 AM after it has passed, the clear code, or a backwards clock).
2. **Safe moment** (re-checked every poll, and again inside the reload): the host is in the town (SCENE_FG, no wipe / fade, its player idle: no menu, talk or demo; the observer is always idle), the world is ready, no peer is still joining (handshake / snapshot under way) and no peer has a two-phase pickup / drop / bury PENDING. Peers inside buildings: a peer in a **shop** (stock / purchase) or a **villager house** (that villager may move out) is a HARD blocker (wait, no limit); a peer in another building (museum, post office, police, other interior) is a SOFT blocker (wait up to 30 min, then roll anyway: those interiors do not depend on the rolled data); a peer in the town, on the island field or in a player house never blocks. A deferred roll logs on every state change and every 5 min; it is retried every poll.
3. **Mechanism.** One same-position SCENE_FG reload (the shop-renewal door: fade black, no train / demo replay). For a DAY roll, `play_init` runs `mSDI_DayRollInit` right before `mFM_FieldInit` (the old actors are destroyed, the new arena is up, no actor exists yet): the boot init for the host's current binding (resident: `mSDI_StartInitFrom` minus RTC / title / decoy; observer or guest binding: the observer PAK init, i.e. no gateway / return-animal / goodbye flush), then `mSDI_StartInitAfter(FALSE, ZELDA)`, then `mEv_init` again (boot order: force-init before the scene, normal init in `play_init`). The field build that follows does the growth. A GROW-only roll is the bare reload. At most one reload per (kind, date key); a roll that did not take is retried at most 3 times, 10 min apart.
4. **Clients** get the result through the existing channels listed above (field diffs / resync, villager arrival / departure, WORLD_META weather, TOWN_SVC shop and events, mailbox pushes); no client reloads. No new message id was needed (93 stays free).
5. **Durability.** After the reload settles, one authoritative save (`pc_save_write_authoritative`, the periodic writer) -> `[SERVER] day roll: saved`. A crash before that save loses nothing that the next boot would not redo (the boot init is the same day roll). The weather / renewal flags are not rolled a second time by the roll: the runtime renewal already consumed them at midnight (`mEnv_DecideWeather_NormalGameStart` only rolls on the flag).
6. **Never touched by the roll:** any resident's / guest's pockets, wallet, dresser, house or guest file (the init writes only the host's own binding: the observer record lives outside `Save_t`); a listen host's own resident gets what a vanilla continue gives it.

**Down for several days / clock changes.** The vanilla boot path is unchanged (it already re-inits; afterwards the date matches and no roll happens). A clock moved forward or back while running is a date change like any other (vanilla: the same on the next boot); rolls are spaced >= 10 min so a clock bounce cannot loop.

**Listen host inside a building at midnight.** The roll waits (host not in the town) and happens on the next idle frame in the town.

**Console / test hooks.** Dedicated console `day` (= `day status`): enabled, today, the last new-day init date, `all_grow_renew_time`, next growth, state (idle / due / deferred: why, since), last roll. `day roll now` (operator): ignore the soft-blocker wait for a due roll. Test builds + `AC_TEST_HOOKS=1`: `AC_TEST_DAY_ROLL_AT=<sec>[,<hhmm>]` moves the HOST game clock (`lbRTC_SetTime`, a real clock change that clients follow) to tomorrow at hh:mm (default 00:00:05) N seconds after the world is ready and a READY peer is connected, so the real detection path fires without a real midnight; `day advance [hhmm]` does the same from the console.


**Observed in the real runs** (host log, the clock moved to tomorrow by the hook): the moment the date changes, BEFORE any roll, the host already re-rolls on its own: Nook's catalog (`[NET][SHOP] the shop catalog changed (new exchange day)`, TOWN_SVC SHOP), the residents' villager-reply pass (`pcnetgame_host_remail_tick`), the guests' mail-event window. The roll then adds: the boot init (`new-day init ran (binding player_no 4)`), a re-rolled event state (TOWN_SVC EVENT seq +1 -> pushed to clients), notice-board pages (`[NET][PAGE]`), the museum / guest day pass, and the growth in the field build (`field-init growth changed 3 town acre(s)` -> FIELD_UPDATEs). A player who entered a shop during the reload's fade made the play_init re-check skip the init (`new-day init skipped inside the reload ... retried`); such a reload is not counted as a try and the roll is retried 1 min later.

**Tests.** `test_day_roll_src.py` (28 static pins + the native `day_roll_selftest.c`, 69 checks: calendar, DAY / 6 AM GROW rules incl. leap years / backwards clock / several days down, scene blocks, gate incl. the bounded soft wait and `roll now`, attempt limiter, hook parser). `test_day_roll_real.py` (REAL host processes + FakeClients, fixture `pc\build64\bin_fixture4_dr_*`, ports 21100+): DR-obs (one roll, init + growth, saved, WORLD_META renew time + weather, live field changes, EVENT state, a late joiner's tiles == the live client's tiles, villager count, pockets / wallet and every resident record in the saved GCI byte-identical, guest files unchanged, no second roll, restart on the rolled save: nothing due), DR-shop (deferred while a client is in Nook's shop, starts when it walks out), DR-off (`AC_MP_DAY_ROLL=0`), DR-mid (killed after the init, before the save: the restart is a clean vanilla boot, records unchanged), DR-listen (listen host, own resident binding), DR-ded (`--dedicated` console `day` / `day roll now` / `day advance`, a roll with no client, `server.log`). **Not exercised at runtime:** a real move-in / move-out during a roll, a listen host inside a building at midnight, the 30 min soft limit, a real midnight / DST change.

## Fishing and catching on puppets (worker P3)

Before: the rod / net rows of the puppet played the right clips but another player's **bobber was invisible** (BOBBER_STATE, id 74, is client -> host only), NOTICE_NET (47) was a fallback, and nothing showed the caught creature. PRESENTATION ONLY: nothing below can change the host's wildlife / fish AI, the catch validation or any item grant.

**Wire (protocol 8 extended in place, no version bump; old peers ignore both):**
- `PC_NETGAME_MSG_BOBBER_VIEW = 97`, host -> READY peers whose announced scene is the angler's, 24 B (`PCBobberViewMsg`, pc/include/pc_fishing_view.h): `msg_type, origin, active, scene_id, uki_status, gyo_status (s8), rod_type, rsv(0), float x, y, z, s16 angle, u16 seq`. UNRELIABLE while the bobber exists, at most one per angler per 100 ms (<= 10 Hz); one RELIABLE `active 0` ("gone") on reel-in, 1 s without input, disconnect or scene change (to every READY peer). `seq` is host-assigned per angler, shared by active / gone, so a late unreliable view after the gone is dropped. The host relays (a) its own bobber, sampled read-only from its uki actor (`pcwld_client_collect_bobber`), and (b) every client's BOBBER_STATE **after** `pcnetgame_handle_host_bobber_state` fed the fish-AI proxy exactly as before; the input is checked (finite, town coordinates, uki status CAST..CATCH, gyo -1..10, rod 0/1, within 480 units / 200 dy of the angler's last MOVE position, the angler's announced FIELD scene, the BOBBER_STATE seq). Receivers check shape / rsv / seq / their own scene and hide a view after 1.5 s without a message. A client-originated id 97 is ignored by the host.
- `PLAYER_ACTION` (id 46, same 10-byte struct) **kind 2 CATCH**: emitted by the host right after IT committed a catch (the X3 catch transaction of a client, after its despawn broadcast; the host's own local catch, inside the accepted branch). `item` = the host-derived creature item (ITM_FISH* / trash / ITM_INSECT* / ITM_SPIRIT*), `ut_x = ut_z = flags = 0`, the per-origin seq shared with PICKUP. Sent to the READY peers in the catcher's scene, never to the catcher; the host presents it on its own puppet of a client. Old receivers ignore kind 2 (their `kind != PICKUP` check).

**What the puppet shows (pc/src/pc_remote_player.c, P3 FISHING VIEW block, drawn from the puppet's own dw):**
- 49-55, 57 (rod states, rows unchanged): the **bobber** of that player: the vanilla `tol_uki1_model` (golden rod: `tol_uki2_model`) exactly like `aUKI_actor_draw` (scale 0.01, turned toward the angler, lying flat in flight, pulled under at the bite), at the relayed position eased per frame. Only while the puppet carries a rod. It is a display list, not a UKI actor: no fish (local or host) can see it. **No fishing line**: vanilla draws none (no line draw exists in ac_uki / m_player_item_rod).
- 56 NOTICE_ROD (GET_T2 row unchanged): the caught **fish** at the left hand (the item's ground model through `bg_item_clip->single_draw_proc`, the same draw as the remote PICKUP flying item), and for a killifish the YAJIRUSHI arrow after the vanilla 42-frame wait.
- 47 NOTICE_NET: now a row (GET_M1 + net NET_GET_M with BODY_CONTINUE / ITEM_CARRY = PULL_NET's pose held, as vanilla binds no clip) and the YAJIRUSHI for an ant / mosquito in the net head. The insect itself is **not** drawn: an insect / spirit item maps to the CAGE ground-item type whose model exists only in the police lost-and-found data; drawing it in the town crashed the observer in the first real run (fixed: `pc_fv_pp_item_drawable` allows fish and the three junk items only).
- 83 RELEASE_CREATURE (right after such a NOTICE): a fish leaves the hand in a 1 s arc and shrinks away (an insect: nothing drawn, see above); the body stays the vanilla WAIT1 fallback.
- The arrow is drawn directly (the `ef_yajirushi.c` draw, `ef_kore_modelT`) instead of spawning the effect: the vanilla removal `effect_kill_proc(YAJIRUSHI, RSV_NO)` kills every arrow, including the local player's own. No effect-pool slot or sound is used by this block.

**Not reproduced (and why):** YATTA2 (47 / 56) is only the "this completes the encyclopedia" branch and starts when the catcher closes a message window: neither the host nor the puppet knows either (the encyclopedia is client-local, the window is local input). The real fish / insect models: a fish model needs the GYOEI controller's texture segments, an insect is a live wildlife actor; the vanilla release actors (GYO_RELEASE writes the GYOEI controller's segment table, an insect release creates a REAL insect) are not created on puppets. The notice fanfares (BGM) are not played for other players. Bobber splash / ripple effects at the cast are not reproduced. The bobber is only drawn while the angler's puppet itself is drawn (an off-screen puppet hides its bobber).

**Tests:** `test_fishing_view_src.py` (37 source pins incl. the native `fishing_view_selftest.c`, 196 checks); `test_fishing_view_protocol.py` (real host + FakeClients, 23 checks: <= 10 Hz, same scene only, implausible not relayed, reel-in / disconnect gone, client-originated 97 ignored, kind 2 for an accepted catch only); `test_fishing_view_real.py` (real host + A + B, PC_PUPPET_DIAG=1: B's puppet of A draws the bobber and shows the catch; see TODO.md for the counts). Runner: `p3_run.py NAME TEST.py` (fixture `pc\build64\bin_fixture4_p3_<name>`, frozen `pc\build64_w3\p3_AnimalCrossing.exe`).

## Puppet ACTION_EXT (worker P2)

**What the players see.** Another player's puppet now plays the exact clip of states whose clip depends on data MOVE cannot carry: furniture handling (HOLD 19 HOLD_WAIT1/_O1/_H1, ROTATE_FURNITURE 22 LTURN1/RTURN1, OPEN / WAIT_OPEN / CLOSE_FURNITURE 23-25 KAGU_OPEN/WAIT/CLOSE_D1/H1/K1 with the vanilla root pin), the bed (LIE_BED 26 INBED_L1/R1, ROLL_BED 28 NEGAERI_L1/R1, STANDUP_BED 29 OUTBED_L1/R1: root pin, the body tilt on the bed, JUMP / BED_IN / BED_NEGAERI / BED_OUT / LANDING sounds), PICKUP_JUMP 31 (CLEAR_TABLE1 / CLEAR_TABLE_L1), talk and demo reactions (TALK 65 / DEMO_WAIT 74: GAAAN1 -> GAAAN2 loop with face 6 / 5 and the SHOCK effect at frame 5, BIKU1 -> WAIT1), trades (RECIEVE_STRETCH 67 GET_F1 / RETURN_MAIL1 / GET1, RECIEVE 68, RECIEVE_PUTAWAY 69, GIVE 70 TRANSFER_F1 / SEND_MAIL1 / TRANSFER1 + GASAGOSO, GIVE_WAIT 71), WASH_CAR 84 (WASH1-5 cycle, every restart), CHANGE_CLOTH 101 (MENU_CHANGE1 at 1.0 with the wear sound + KIGAE at frame 16, or ITAZURA1 + HALLOWEEN), RADIO_EXERCISE 111 (TAISOU1-7, the tempo-driven speed retuned) and the golden-tool demos 118 / 119 (YATTA1 / YATTA3). These states used to play the idle fallback. Furniture states only matter in a shared room (house sync commits the furniture itself later). **Won't fix:** the boat demos 112-117 (the island is not shared).

**Wire.** `PC_NETGAME_MSG_ACTION_EXT = 96` (v8 extended in place, no version bump), RELIABLE, 16 bytes (`PCActionExtMsg`, `pc/include/pc_action_ext.h`): msg_type, net_player_id, main_index, entry_counter, anim0, anim1, part_table, flags (bit0 REVERSE = negative owner speed, bits 1-3 root-pin mask = the vanilla `AnimationMove_ct_base` flags TRANS_XZ / TRANS_Y / ROT_Y, bit4 REPEAT, bits 5-7 zero), speed_q (|speed| x 32), start_frame (the owner's frame), face (eye << 4 | mouth, 0xFF none), variant (trade surface flag / WASH index), aux u16 (traded item / radio command), clip_seq (a new value restarts the clip, the same value only retunes the speed), rsv 0. Routing like BEE_SWARM: owner -> host (READY sender, valid message, announced scene) -> every OTHER READY peer whose announced scene id AND owner equal the sender's; the host stamps the origin and presents a client's message on its own puppet; a listen host's own player is relayed directly. Validation (sender, host and receiver): allowlisted index, counter < 16, anim < mPlayer_ANIM_NUM (157, not 158), part table < 5, eye < 8, mouth < 6, reserved bits / byte zero. Old peers: a 0.1.1 / 0.2.1 host or client has no branch for 96 and ignores it silently (no violation: 96 is outside the 47-50 / 59-61 range branches; proven by source on both releases and at runtime against the released 0.2.1 exe).

**Sender.** ONE generic hook, `pcnetgame_aext_tick()` (pc_net_game_poll, after the MOVE send): a read-only sample of the local player (now_main_index, the MOVE entry counter, animation0_idx / animation1_idx / part_table_idx, keyframe0 mode / speed / frame, keyframe0.animation_enabled) through the pure change detector `pc_aext_sender_step` (entry of an allowlisted state, a clip change inside it, a STOP clip restart, a speed retune of >= 1/16 at most every 15 frames, never for an ended STOP clip; hard cap 12 burst + 8 per second). No vanilla state file was edited: every allowlisted state sets its clip through `Player_actor_InitAnimation_Base1/2`, which writes exactly the sampled fields. ON by default; `PC_ACTION_EXT=0` disables sending.

**Receiver.** A DYNAMIC ROW per owner (`pc_remote_player.c`, block "PUPPET ACTION_EXT"): the messages are stored in a 4-record ring and applied only while the puppet's interpolated MOVE pair (main index, entry counter) equals a record's pair (a stale / mismatched counter never plays; a shown record expires when the pair is left, an unseen one after 300 frames; the newest record of a pair wins). While applied it owns the body clips like a static row (a static row of the same pair is released first): P1's `pc_remote_player_bind_body` (anim0 / anim1, STOP / REPEAT, the owner's speed and start frame, morph -5, REVERSE through P1's reverse playback), the part table, and the vanilla root pin (`Set_base_shape_trs(0, 1000, z)` with z 200 for the furniture states, `animation_enabled` = the sent mask). It also applies the bed tilt (shape rotation x / y of LIE / ROLL / WAIT / STANDUP_BED), the constant face of a clip without eye / mouth tables, and the state cues (sounds through their own gate: the puppet must be SHOWN, so bed sounds play in a shared interior; effects through the field-only budgeted effect path).

**Integration points left for worker P1's row infrastructure (lead):** (1) the root-pin mask is applied directly in the P2 block; it maps 1:1 onto P1's generalized root-pin masks; (2) the cue table (`s_aext_cues`) could move into P1's sounds / effects tables keyed by the dynamic row; (3) P1's blinking / face rules run before `pc_puppet_aext_face` (the dynamic row only overrides a constant face where the clip has no table); (4) the traded item (aux) is carried but not drawn (held-item drawing is P1's); (5) P1's facing rule (pc_net_game.c, MOVE facing_angle): the bed tilt adds the vanilla +-0x4000 offset on top of world.angle.y during LIE / STANDUP_BED; if the facing rule ever sends the shape rotation for those states the offset must be dropped; (6) `if (row_on && self->visual.row_active)` in pc_remote_player_mv keeps P1's row tempo / row_tick off a dynamic row (row_idx is not its row).

**Not reproduced:** the talk HALLOWEEN effect of demo order 0xFD (an effect without a clip change); held gifts / mail in the hand; WASH_CAR's car-driven speed between restarts is retuned at most every 15 frames.

**Tests:** native `action_ext_selftest.c` (68 checks: layout, validation, sender detector, receiver store); `test_action_ext_src.py` (32 checks: wire pins, id unique, 93-95 unused, relay scope, range validation pinned to vanilla, counter binding, sender hook, old 0.1.1 / 0.2.1 dispatchers); `test_action_ext_protocol.py` (real host + FakeClients, 14 checks: same-scene READY relay only, not to the sender or another scene, origin stamped, 7 malformed + 2 wrong sizes dropped, sender stays connected) and `--old` against the released 0.2.1 exe (8 checks: nothing relayed, no violation, sender stays connected); `test_action_ext_real.py` (real listen host + 2 real clients in the town field, 30 checks: the host's bed L / R + roll + standup, A's talk reaction GAAAN1 -> GAAAN2 (face 6 / 5) -> BIKU1 with the SHOCK effect, B's radio exercise, all applied as dynamic rows on the other two processes, bed sounds produced, rows end with the states). The max-id pins (`wire_baseline.py`, `test_pvp_src.py`) now allow exactly 96 / 97 / 98 above 92. **Not run:** a shared-house run (two clients in one house with --house-sync: open a dresser, bed) and the trade / wash-car / change-cloth / golden-demo states (sender + receiver are generic; the change-cloth test hook exists, `AC_TEST_AEXT_SCRIPT=cloth`).

## Clothes change visible to others (worker CL)

**Root cause:** equipping a shirt from the inventory never puts the real player actor into `mPlayer_INDEX_CHANGE_CLOTH` (101). The menu writes `Now_Private->cloth` and the texture itself at its doll's frame 36 (`m_hand_ovl.c:532-545` / `:786-800`) and only animates the MENU DOLL (`m_tag_ovl.c:7991-7993`, `:5873`: `mIV_ANIM_CHANGE` = MENU_CHANGE1 @1.0 STOP + `NA_SE_WEAR`). 101 is requested only by NPC code (shop try-on, needlework, Halloween), so ACTION_EXT (which samples the real actor) never saw the menu change; the other players' puppet just snapped to the new shirt when the APPEARANCE message arrived.

**Fix (receiver only, no wire change, ON by default; `PC_PUPPET_CLOTH_ANIM=0` = old instant swap):** `pc_remote_player_on_appearance()` HOLDS a clothing change (catalog item / design flag / custom-design pixels; never the first appearance; raw fields updated at once for relay and backfill, the render buffers keep the old shirt). `pc_puppet_cloth_step()` (called from `pc_puppet_aext_apply`) presents it: menu path = a synthesized clip like the menu doll (MENU_CHANGE1, frame 1, speed 1.0, STOP, normal part table) with the in-world change-cloth cues at frame 16 (wear sound + KIGAE effect) and the shirt swap at frame 36 (the vanilla texture frame), bound to the MOVE pair (ends at the clip end, a pair change, any row, a scene snap); owner in 101 (ACTION_EXT row) = swap at the vanilla texture frame of the row's clip (36 MENU_CHANGE1, 18 ITAZURA1); unshown puppet = instant; moving = instant with the puff + sound. The hold is bounded (150 frames; `pc_remote_player_poll()` releases an expired / actor-less hold). The clip lags the owner's doll by ~36 frames (the APPEARANCE is only sent when the menu writes the cloth). Puppets keep ticking behind a viewer's menu, so the animation is seen there too. Hats / glasses / accessories do not exist in this game (Private_c has none); umbrellas / tools are held items (MOVE item_kind + P1 rows).

**Not covered:** the shop try-on (101 with try_on TRUE changes only the local texture, not `Now_Private->cloth`, so no APPEARANCE is sent: other players see the try-on clip with the old shirt); the menu doll's own sparkle spiral (menu-only particles, no world effect).

**Tests:** `test_cloth_change_src.py` 29/29 (vanilla anchors, hold / poll / presentation pins, no new wire, plus the F3 removal pins); `test_cloth_change_real.py` (real listen host + 1 real client, Samsung, volume 1, `AC_TEST_AEXT_SCRIPT=clothes` = the menu's own writes, a real Halloween 101 change, WAIT, the menu restore; both observe each other) run 2 30/30 (run 1 24/30: only wrong anim enum values in the test's regexes: MENU_CHANGE1 = 73, ITAZURA1 = 125). Regressions: `test_action_ext_src.py` 32/32, `test_puppet_rows_batchA_src.py` all passed, `test_menu_world_live_src.py` all passed, `test_log_src.py` 53/53.

## Puppet animation coverage (worker P1)

**What the players see.** 22 more vanilla main indexes now play their real clip on another player's puppet instead of the idle fallback (MOVE `action_state`, no new message): PUSH 20 PUSH1, PULL 21 PULL1, PICKUP_FURNITURE 32 PICKUP1 + item pose, SITDOWN 34 SITDOWN1, STANDUP 36 STANDUP1, STOP_NET 46 (the running clip FREEZES at speed 0, as vanilla: the net swing that hit something, incl. the player-vs-player net hit, holds its pose instead of snapping to idle), TAKEOUT_ITEM 72 (umbrella UMB_OPEN1; anything else PUTAWAY1 played BACKWARDS, the item growing 0 -> 1 from state timer 36 to 54 and the carried pose clip switched in at 36), PUTIN_ITEM 73 (UMB_CLOSE1 / PUTAWAY1, the item shrinking 1 -> 0 over 18 frames; the kind is the MOVE kind or the last carried kind), DEMO_GETON_TRAIN 76 INTRAIN1, DEMO_GETON_TRAIN_WAIT 77 INTRAIN_WAIT1, DEMO_GETOFF_TRAIN 78 OUTTRAIN1, ROTATE_OCTAGON 86 FUKUBIKI1, THROW_MONEY 87 SAISEN1, PRAY 88 OMAIRI_US1, MAIL_JUMP 90 CONFIRM1, MAIL_LAND 91 CONFIRM1 reversed, READY_PITFALL 92 GURATUKU1, FALL_PITFALL 93 OTIRU1 (OTIRU2 with an umbrella), CLIMBUP_PITFALL 95 DERU1/DERU2 at speed 1.0, NOTICE_BEE 97 HATI3, WADE_SNOWBALL 104 (keeps the PUSH_YUKI1 clip), SWITCH_ON_LIGHTHOUSE 110 LIGHT_ON1. DIG_SCOOP 58 plays DIG_KABU1 when the reconstructed shovel target is a tree stump. The table now has 72 filled rows (incl. P3's NOTICE_NET 47) and 49 explicit fallbacks.

**Row infrastructure** (`pc_remote_player.c`): `s_row_pin[121]` = the vanilla `cKF_SkeletonInfo_R_AnimationMove_ct_base` mask (1 TRANS_XZ, 2 TRANS_Y, 4 ROT_Y; masks 1 / 3 / 4 / 5 / 7 occur) + the `Set_base_shape_trs` z (200 for the furniture push / pull / open / close states) of all 26 ct_base states (P2's furniture / bed and the boat demos included); a ROOT_PIN row pins exactly those root components (the old hard-coded TRANS_XZ|ROT_Y pin of DOOR / KNOCK_DOOR is the mask-5 case). `s_row_morph[121]` = the vanilla setup morph_counter: the puppet now blends into every row clip (-3..-9, +9 / +10 hold-then-blend for LIGHT_ON1 / PULL_NET) and into the fallback locomotion / idle (-5) instead of snapping; chain clips (AXE_BREAKWAIT1, HATI2, GAAAN2) blend with -5. `PC_ROWF_REVERSE` (Base3 = `cKF_SkeletonInfo_R_init_reverse_setspeedandmorphandmode`), `PC_ROWF_FREEZE` (stop_net), `PC_ROWF_ITEM_SCALE` + `pc_remote_player_row_tick` (item_scale curve; the held item is drawn with `Matrix_scale(item_scale)` like `Player_actor_Item_draw`). Kind-dependent clips: `PC_ROW_ANIM_FALL_PITFALL / CLIMBUP_PITFALL / TAKEOUT / PUTIN`. A `[NET][PUPPET][DIAG] player N root pin row=R mask=M base_z=Z pos=(x,y,z) facing=F` line is logged per pinned row start (PC_PUPPET_DIAG=1).

**Sender (value change only):** `facing_angle` carries `shape_info.rotation.y` for TURN_DASH and for EVERY ROT_Y ct_base state (`pcnetgame_facing_rot_y_state`: DOOR, SITDOWN, both train demos, ROTATE_OCTAGON, THROW_MONEY, PRAY, MAIL_JUMP, NOTICE_BEE, KNOCK_DOOR, SWITCH_ON_LIGHTHOUSE, the three boat demos), because AnimationMove_base turns the visual facing while world.angle.y holds the fixed target angle and the puppet pins the clip's own root rotation.

**Face / polish:** puppets BLINK (`pc_remote_player_blink_eye` = `Player_actor_set_eye_pattern_normal`: the 16-frame pattern, 60..179 frame waits, 0..2 extra blinks, a private per-puppet LCG, never the gameplay RNG) whenever neither the clip's eye table nor a state constant face applies; NOTICE_BEE returns to the normal eye after its clip. The face diag line keeps reporting the derived (non-blink) face. New tool-table sounds / effects (same gating / budget): PICKUP_FURNITURE ITEM_GET 10 + GASAGOSO 20, SITDOWN JUMP + HARD_CHAIR_SIT 18, STANDUP HARD_CHAIR_STANDUP + LANDING 17, TAKEOUT / PUTIN GASAGOSO, THROW_MONEY COIN_GASAGOSO 9 + COIN effect 33, READY_PITFALL ASE2 sweat at 5 (killed when the state is left), KNOCK_DOOR knock SE at 13 and 20.

**Held items:** balloons (8) and pinwheels (8) are drawn (`PC_HELD_BALLOON` / `PC_HELD_WINDMILL`): the item skeleton + clip of the take-out, the balloon sway (`Movement_balloon_normal`: hand-movement spring, walk bob, frame band 13..26) and the pinwheel spin (fan-joint travel + global wind, `windmill_CulcRotationSpeed`), ported puppet-owned. **Bee-sting swollen face:** APPEARANCE byte 8 (`_reserved0[0]`) bit 0 = `PC_NETGAME_APPEAR_FLAG_BEE_SWELL` (was always 0: old peers send / read 'not swollen'); in-process it rides bit 7 of `PCNetPlayerAppearance::sunburn_rank` (header layout frozen); the existing per-frame appearance change detection resends it; the puppet resolves the swollen face set (`mPlib_Load_FaceTexAndPallet(..., swell)`).

**Wire:** no new message id (98 not used), no size change: the APPEARANCE flag bit and the facing value only.

**Not reproduced / skipped:** custom-design (ORG) umbrellas (optional message 98 not done: they stay `deferred`); the chair's own SIT / STANDUP sound (furniture id not on the wire: vanilla default HARD_CHAIR_*); the pinwheel whirr (`sAdo_kazagurumaLevel` is one global level owned by the local player's pinwheel); the balloon lean coupling (puppets have no lean); the umbrella's own open / close actor animation (static open model, scale 1 as vanilla); KIGAE / HANATIRI effects (their states are P2's / not rows); the take-out item's mid-state pose for umbrellas (vanilla has none). Root pin of the furniture / bed states is in the table but applied by P2's dynamic row.

**Tests:** `test_puppet_rows_batchA_src.py` (36 checks: pin table == the parsed vanilla masks + base z of all 26 ct_base states, ROOT_PIN rows == pinned states, sender ROT_Y list == the parsed ROT_Y states, morph table == the parsed setup morphs, batch rows / clips / modes, reverse / freeze / scale / stump infra, tool rows, blink, labels); `test_move_action_state_wire.py` 38/38 (all 121 indexes incl. the new rows, real host); `test_puppet_held_item.py` S3 (all 79 kinds + none classified, balloons / pinwheels drawn walking) and S4 (swell flag: host puppet, relay to a later client with a clean sunburn byte, off again); `test_puppet_rows_real.py` (REAL host + A + observer B: take-out rows and held classes for umbrella / balloon / pinwheel / net, a placed pitfall: ready / fall / struggle / climbup rows on B, mask-3 pins, the pinned root within 4 units of A's own position, the ready_pitfall sweat on B).

## Outdoor furniture (C1) and the operator list

**Status: implemented, not committed; harness + real-game automated evidence (below); BLOCKED: manual verification required** (nobody has looked at the models / walked into one by hand yet).

Two actions on a furniture item outdoors (town field only, never the island / a room):

- **Drop** = the vanilla LEAF drop, unchanged: anyone (guests too) drops, anyone picks a leaf up. A host may refuse it: `settings.ini [Network] outdoor_furniture_leaf_drop = 0` (the vanilla "can't place that" window + the notice "Dropping furniture outside is off on this server").
- **Place** = new: **residents only**. The piece stands on the tile as its real model, rotated, with a collision box. **Pick up / Rotate** of a placed piece: any resident, any **operator**, the listen host's own player; never a guest that is not an operator. No per-owner lock, no gameplay limit.

**Design "C" (hybrid).** The town tile keeps the furniture id with the rotation in its low 2 bits (S/E/N/W = 0..3, `mRmTp_FtrIdx2FtrItemNo`): it is the persistent, synced SOURCE OF TRUTH (FIELD_UPDATE / snapshots / the town save; an old client sees a leaf, nothing crashes). The HOST keeps a sidecar of PLACED markers, `outdoor_furniture.dat` next to `work_jobs.dat` (`servers\<id>\` on a dedicated server, `save\mp\` on a listen host): per marker the tile, the base id, the rotation, the placer (kind resident / guest / host, the 20-byte identity key, the name) and the time. A marker is valid only while its tile holds exactly `base | rot`; the host GC (every 500 ms) drops a marker whose tile changed (police-box sweeps, a vanilla pickup, a new structure, any host write) and sends DEL.

- **Crash safety (write-ahead):** every PLACE / ROTATE / PICKUP saves the sidecar (tmp -> MoveFileEx, atomic) BEFORE the tile changes; a failed save or tile write undoes the marker. A crash between the sidecar and the town save leaves either a marker whose tile does not match (GC: the piece is a leaf / gone, nothing duplicated) or, for a pickup, the item still on its tile as a leaf (the pocket mirror was not saved either). A corrupt sidecar (bad magic / version / FNV) is refused whole: placed pieces stay leaves, Place is refused (FAULT) until the file is repaired / removed.
- **Rules of a placement (host):** a RESIDENT binding; the pocket slot holds the item in NORMAL condition; furniture of shape 1x1 (`aFTR_SHAPE_TYPEA`; 2x1 / 2x2: `OFURN_TOO_BIG` "That is too big to place outside (Drop it instead)" -- they can still be dropped as leaves); a town tile that is EMPTY, deposit 0, not reserved by another pickup / drop, `mCoBG_CheckPlace` (no water / cliff edge / bridge), none of its 8 neighbours a structure value (>= 0xF000: house / building footprints, doors); one of the 8 neighbours of the requester's tile as the HOST last saw it (never the requester's own tile; diagonal tolerated for the MOVE lag); the host is in the town (its collision is loaded: the same condition as the vanilla drop validation); the rate limit (200 ms between ops, 120 a minute per peer: generous, anti-spam only); the **technical safety ceiling** (NOT a gameplay limit; it protects the renderer / the 64-entry move-bg registry / the save): `outdoor_furniture_max_town = 512` (clamped to the 1024-entry table) and `outdoor_furniture_max_acre = 128`, refusal `OFURN_LIMIT` with the notice "This town has too much furniture outside".
- **Rotation:** chosen at placement from the player's facing (the piece faces the player: `(dir4(facing) + 2) & 3`); A in front of a placed piece rotates it by 90 degrees.

**Wire (protocol v8 extended in place, no version bump):**

- **TXN_COMMIT kind 22 OUTDOOR_FURNITURE** (one-phase, journalled like kinds 18..21: fence, byte-identical replay = REPLAYED, conflict, base (epoch, rev), pre-image validation, conservation): `tag.aux_cond` = op 1 PLACE / 2 ROTATE / 3 PICKUP, `tag.aux_item` = the tile `ut_z << 8 | ut_x`, `tag.flags` = the rotation. PLACE: dest NONE, slot = the pocket slot, item = the pocket item -> the post-image empties exactly that slot. ROTATE: dest NONE, slot 0, item = the tile value the client sees, flags = the new rotation -> pockets unchanged. PICKUP: dest POCKET, slot = a FREE slot, item = the tile value the client sees -> the post-image puts the BASE item (rotation 0) in the slot (`NO_ROOM` 22 when the slot is not free).
- **Reasons 55..61:** 55 OFURN_OFF (Place off: `AC_MP_OUTDOOR_FURNITURE=0` / `outdoor_furniture_place = 0`), 56 OFURN_NOT_ALLOWED (a guest places / picks up / rotates a placed piece), 57 OFURN_TOO_BIG, 58 OFURN_BAD_TILE, 59 OFURN_LIMIT, 60 OFURN_RATE, 61 OFURN_GONE (the piece is no longer there / was rotated by somebody else); plus the generic PRECOND / NO_ROOM / BUSY / FAULT. A malformed kind 22 is BAD_SHAPE + a record violation (never sent by a correct client).
- **Message 98 OUTDOOR_FURN** (host -> one READY client, RELIABLE, fixed 268 bytes: `u8 type, u8 kind, u8 count, u8 flags, u32 gen, u32 total, 32 x {u8 ut_x, u8 ut_z, u16 item, u32 placed_time}`): kinds 1 BEGIN / 2 CHUNK / 3 END = the full list at join and reconnect (sent only while the peer has no snapshot under way and its reliable backlog is < 32, like the other join pushes; a change mid-list restarts it), 4 SET / 5 DEL = deltas on change (to SYNCED peers), 6 PERMS = the peer's rights in `flags` (1 place, 2 pickup/rotate, 4 operator, 8 leaf drop; re-sent whenever they change, e.g. at `op` / `deop`). PRESENTATION + UI only: the tiles travel as FIELD_UPDATE. An old peer ignores the id (the client dispatcher's "unrecognized: ignore"); a client-originated 98 is dropped by the host.
- **Town service 10 OFURN_CAPS** (8 bytes: `u8 version 1, u8 flags (bit 0 Place on, bit 1 leaf drop on), u16 max_town, u16 max_acre, u16 0`; `PC_NETGAME_TS_NUM` 10 -> 11). Service 9 could not be extended: a 0.2.x client refuses the whole EVNPC_CAPS blob for a non-zero reserved byte or an unknown op bit. An old client ignores service 10 (reserved / unknown).
- **Old hosts:** a client sends kind 22 only after a validated service 10 AND the host's PERMS granted the right, and never again in the session after one BAD_SHAPE (latch, like kinds 19 / 20 / 21): a 0.1.1 / 0.2.x host (which counts an unknown kind as a violation) never sees kind 22.
- **The vanilla pickup gate:** `pcnetgame_validate_and_resolve_pickup` refuses the PICKUP_REQUEST of a PLACED tile (marker + matching value) from a peer that is neither a resident nor an operator (a guest, an old client of a guest); a resident's / operator's vanilla pickup is accepted and grants the rotation-0 item (the marker is GC'd). A leaf without a marker stays everybody's. A new client refuses a guest's local B pickup of a placed piece at once with a notice.
