**Client (PC only, `pc/src/pc_ofurn_draw.c`, nothing of it writes a tile / deposit / house / pocket):** from the field-item actor (bg_item_common.c_inc hooks: move / draw / destruct) in the town field: for every placed piece of the VIEW (host: its table; client: the message-98 mirror) within 24 tiles whose tile still holds it, ONE private FTR_ACTOR + 2 KB dma bank (up to 160 models, nearest first) built by `aMR_PC_StandaloneCt` (ac_my_room.c TARGET_PC block: the catalog's construction -- dma_proc, the rig, the vtable ct_proc -- and a trimmed `aMR_DrawOneFurniture`: light, texture, palette, kankyo map, registered model, skeleton, vtable draw_proc with a NULL room actor like the catalog); NEVER a move_proc (no behaviour: no my_room_clip, music, drawers, lights, sitting) and never the room's banks. Profiles whose vtable has a `dt_proc` (the 10 structure models that zelda_malloc a palette) are not run through their vtable; when nothing else is drawable the vanilla leaf stays. The vanilla leaf of a drawn piece is not drawn (`pc_ofurn_hide_leaf` in `bg_item_common_classifyitemDataTable`; a view change requests the FG redraw). Collision: `mCoBG_RegistMoveBg` with the profile's move-bg type, height 100, for the up to 24 pieces within 4 tiles of the local player (the registry holds 64 for the whole scene); a new play scene forgets the indices (`mCoBG_InitMoveBgData`). Diagnostics: `[OFURN][DRAW] model built / released`, `[OFURN][COL] collision registered`, `[OFURN][INPUT]`, a status line every ~30 s.

**UI (minimal):** the inventory tag menu of a plain furniture item in the town field gets **Grab / Place / Drop / Quit** (PC: the unused vanilla tag slot `mTG_TYPE_76`) when this player may place (a resident of a host with service 10 + PERMS place, or the listen host); otherwise (guests, old hosts, a shovel menu) the vanilla menu. Standing still in front of a placed piece: **A = Rotate**, **B = Pick up** (C2 replaced A with the vanilla grab / push / pull / rotate, see "Outdoor furniture C2"; the wait-state seam of `m_player_main_wait.c_inc`; the vanilla shake / pickup of that frame are skipped, otherwise unchanged). Refusals show one notice line for ~4 s (`pc_net_notice_draw`, plain ASCII). No interactions with placed pieces (sit / lie / lights / dressers / music) in C1.

**Operator list.** `ops.ini` next to the server's other files (`servers\<id>\ops.ini`, a listen host `save\mp\ops.ini`; versioned text, atomic write, tolerant parser: unknown keys / malformed lines are skipped): `op = resident|guest <40 hex digits> <name>`. The key is the authenticated identity the host binds a connection to (resident: the save's PersonalID = name + town name + player id + land id; guest: its guest key), never a display name alone. `pcnetgame_player_is_op(peer)` / `pc_net_game_peer_is_op()` is the reusable host check; the listen host's own player is implicitly an operator. Console (`--dedicated`): `ops`, `op <player>`, `deop <player>` (online name / a resident / a guest of this town / `peer N`; a name with two identities is refused as ambiguous -- the host already refuses a second guest with a used name, so this is rare), and `furniture list [acre]`, `furniture remove <x> <z>|#<index> [delete]`, `furniture clear acre <n>|player <name> [delete]` (removed pieces go to the town's lost and found via `mPB_keep_item` unless `delete`). See docs/console-commands.md.

**Switches (host, ON by default):** `settings.ini [Network]` `outdoor_furniture_place = 1`, `outdoor_furniture_leaf_drop = 1`, `outdoor_furniture_max_town = 512`, `outdoor_furniture_max_acre = 128` (there is no `server_config.ini`: a dedicated server's `server.ini` is rewritten by the server at every start, so the host settings live in `settings.ini` like `max_guests` / `resident_tokens`); env `AC_MP_OUTDOOR_FURNITURE=0` turns Place off in that process.

**Evidence.** Native: `test_ofurn_native.py` (pc_ofurn_selftest + pc_ops_selftest, -Wall -Wextra -Werror). Source: `test_ofurn_src.py`, `test_old_host_compat_src.py` K22. Protocol (real listen host + FakeClients): `test_ofurn_protocol.py` MAIN / GC / OFF / LEAF / CAP / RS. Console (real dedicated host + FakeClients): `test_ofurn_console_protocol.py`. Real game (real host + real client, Samsung, volume 1): `test_ofurn_real.py` (the client draws the model, registers the collision, A rotates and B picks it up through the real input seam; the model of all 1266 furniture ids built / drawn / torn down on host and client without a crash).

**Not in C1 (C2 / C3):** multi-tile (2x1 / 2x2) placement (footprint reservation of 2-4 tiles, rotation of the footprint, the marker spanning tiles), furniture interactions outdoors (sit, lie, lights / toggles, music players, storage) -- that needs a behaviour host without the My_Room actor; villager path-finding around placed pieces (villagers collide with the box but do not plan around it); a placement preview / ghost model and a proper menu instead of A / B; the dresser / storage contents of a placed piece; placing on the island / in front of buildings with a door model; per-acre limits as a gameplay option; an operator UI in the game for a listen host (it has no console); a visual (image) check of the models.

## Outdoor furniture C2: multi-tile pieces and grab / push / pull / rotate

**Status: implemented, not committed; harness + real-game automated evidence (below); BLOCKED: manual verification required.**

**User report:** "with furniture being placed outside, the grab and pull and rotate aren't properly coded in. Also I can't place down dressers / wardrobes / etc."

**Why dressers could not be placed (two causes):**

1. **The renderer refused their model.** C1 (`ac_my_room.c` `aMR_PC_UseVtable`) ran a profile's vtable only when it had NO `dt_proc`, to keep away the 10 structure models whose `ct_proc` zelda_mallocs a palette. But 123 of the 208 furniture vtables carry an EMPTY `dt_proc`, among them every dresser / wardrobe / closet (their model is the skeleton their `ct_proc` builds; the profile has no display list). With the vtable skipped, nothing was drawable and the placed piece stayed a vanilla leaf. In the real run before the fix the client log showed `0 model(s) drawn` for the dresser. **Fix:** every vtable runs and `aMR_PC_StandaloneDt` calls the `dt_proc`, so a `ct_proc` allocation is always freed. All 18 non-empty `dt_proc` bodies were checked: `zelda_free(pal_p)`, `switch_bit` copies, and a haniwa `sAdo_RhythmStop` of a rhythm that was never started. Real run 2 built, drew and tore down all 1266 furniture models on host and client without a crash.
2. **Multi-tile pieces were refused** `OFURN_TOO_BIG` (57), on the client before any round trip and again on the host. Counts by furniture id: 1081 are 1x1, 130 are 2x1 (`aFTR_SHAPE_TYPEB_0`) and 55 are 2x2 (`TYPEC`). Storage pieces: 35 ids, of which 26 are 1x1 (hit by cause 1) and 9 are 2x1 low chests (cause 2). Nothing else excluded storage furniture; there was no class or profile check.

**Footprint design (pure, `pc_outdoor_furniture.c`; native selftest):**

- **Cells** follow the indoor engine exactly (`aMR_SetInfoFurnitureTable`, `aMR_GetTypeBPlaceInfo`, `shape_type = l_typeB0_table[rot]`):
  - 1x1: the origin.
  - 2x1: the origin plus one cell, which by rotation is +x (rot 0, `TYPEB_0`), -z (rot 1), -x (rot 2) or +z (rot 3). The second cell swings around the origin when the piece rotates.
  - 2x2: origin, +x, +z and +x+z, for every rotation.
- **Model position** (`aMR_UnitNumber2Position`): the origin cell centre for 1x1 and 2x1, the shared corner of the 4 cells for 2x2. The y is the STATIC ground (`mCoBG_GetBgY_OnlyCenter_FromWpos`); `GetBgY_AngleS` would include the piece's own move-bg box, which is 100 units high. That bug blocked every move in real run 2.
- **Collision** is the profile's own `move_bg_type` with the angle, the same call as indoors. The TYPEB / TYPEC boxes (60 / 40-unit extents) cover the footprint.
- **Field tiles:**
  - The ORIGIN holds `id | rot`: an old client sees one leaf.
  - Every other cell holds **`RSV_FE1F`**, the vanilla room filler for exactly this job (`ac_my_room_move.c_inc` `aMR_SetFurniture2FG`). Evidence that it is safe:
    - STABLE for the field diff (`pcfa_transient_kind`). `RSV_NO` is AMBIGUOUS and is withheld while the host is in town, so a dedicated server's clients would never get it.
    - Never drawn by `bIT_individual_draw_check` (only types below WARP are drawn).
    - No collision column.
    - Blocks drops (`m_tag_ovl.c` needs `EMPTY_NO`), weeds and spawns (`m_all_grow_ovl.c` needs `EMPTY_NO`), digs (`pcnetgame_fa_validate_adapter_dig_hole`: EMPTY or a plant) and pickups (not a pickupable class).
    - Ignored by the police-box sweep, which takes only FTR / ITEM1 values. A second furniture id as filler would duplicate the piece there and on old clients.
    - The snowman build now refuses it too.
  - No vanilla code clears `FE1F` on the field, so the host GC does.
- **Marker:** the sidecar marker holds the origin, the shape (`PCOfurnMarker.shape`, the former zero `_rsv`) and the rotation. The host never trusts a filler value alone: a marker is valid only while EVERY cell holds its value.
- **GC (every 500 ms):** when ANY cell changes (a sweep, a 0.3.0 client's vanilla pickup of the origin leaf, a structure, any host write), the marker is dropped, the cells still holding `FE1F` are cleared, and the surviving origin stays a leaf (never lost).
- **Writes:** every write of a footprint is all-or-nothing (`pcnetgame_ofurn_write_cells`: read every old value, write, restore on a failure), always after the write-ahead sidecar save.
- **Placement (host):** every cell passes the C1 tile rules (empty, deposit 0, not reserved, `mCoBG_CheckPlace`, no structure neighbour; the filler is not a structure), and the ground is flat (|dy| <= 4 on the static ground). The footprint stays in the town FG, the player stands next to it and not on it, and the C1 ceilings apply (one marker per piece).
  - Placing from the tag menu puts the origin on the front tile with the piece facing the player. A 2x2 in front of a player facing north or west moves its origin one tile further so the square stands wholly in front.

**Grab / push / pull / rotate (the vanilla scheme, `pc_ofurn_draw.c` driver):**

The indoor driver lives in the My_Room actor. The player states HOLD (19), PUSH (20), PULL (21) and ROTATE_FURNITURE (22) are generic. HOLD and ROTATE poll `my_room_clip->judge_player_action_proc` every frame; PUSH and PULL play their clip and re-request HOLD. Outdoors `my_room_clip` is NULL, so `m_player_main_hold.c_inc` and `m_player_main_rotate_furniture.c_inc` (TARGET_PC only, vanilla unchanged in `#else`) ask `pc_ofurn_grab_judge` instead, with the same return codes: 0 release, 1 hold, 2 push, 3 pull (wpos0 / wpos1 = the player's end), 4 rotate right, 5 rotate left. Without an outdoor grab it answers 0. No furniture behaviour code runs outdoors (no `move_proc`, no `my_room_clip`).

| Input (as indoors) | What happens |
|---|---|
| **A** in front of a placed piece (WAIT state) | Grab: `mPlib_request_main_hold_type1`. The player snaps to its tile centre facing the piece: the real HOLD state and the HOLD_WAIT1 clip |
| Hold A, stick **full towards** the piece for 16 frames | PUSH one tile: the PUSH1 clip, the piece slides with the player |
| Hold A, stick **full away** for 16 frames | PULL one tile: the PULL1 clip, the player steps back. The tile behind must be walkable: empty, a dropped item or a leaf |
| Hold A, stick **full sideways** (re-armed by a neutral stick) | ROTATE 90 degrees: the player's left = LTURN1 (+90), right = RTURN1 (-90). Never into the player's own cell or an occupied cell |
| Release A | Let go (back to WAIT) |
| **B** in front (not grabbing) | Pick up. In vanilla B picks furniture up and A never does; B while holding does nothing, as indoors |

- **Feedback:** a step blocked locally, or refused by the host, gives the vanilla `eEC_EFFECT_BUBU` puff and no retry until the stick leaves that direction (`aMR_SetBubu`).
- **Sounds:** `NA_SE_STONECOIN_DRAG` for a move (outdoors there is no room floor for `sAdo_FloorTrgStart`) and `NA_SE_ROTATE` for a turn.
- **One step at a time:** each step is ONE host transaction, and the next waits for the answer.
- **Optimistic presentation:** the model slides (800 ms, the clip length) or turns (270 ms) at once. On APPLIED the view catches up (FIELD_UPDATE + 102 MOVE). On a refusal the model snaps back in 150 ms with the notice "It won't budge"; a refused move also lets go, because the player already stepped.
- **Other players** see the vanilla PUSH / PULL rows of the puppet. HOLD and ROTATE come through ACTION_EXT, which has no scene gating (verified in the real run: the observer saw 19, 20, 21 and 22). The model slides from its old pose (`pc_net_game_ofurn_take_slide`).
- **Rights:** as C1. Move and rotate need the PICKUP right (residents, operators, the host); guests get NOT_ALLOWED.
- **The C1 shortcut** (A = rotate +90 in the wait state) is replaced by the grab. B = pick up stays (vanilla).

**Wire (protocol v8 extended in place, no version bump):**

- **kind 22 op 4 MOVE** (new):
  - Tag: dest NONE, slot 0, `item` = the origin tile value the client sees, `flags` = direction (0 S / 1 E / 2 N / 3 W, where the PIECE moves) | 4 PULL, `aux_item` = the ORIGIN.
  - Host checks:
    - the PICKUP right;
    - the marker with ALL cells matching;
    - the player's side: PUSH stands behind the piece, PULL in front, orthogonally adjacent. The host's copy of the position may lag one step: the 4 orthogonal neighbours are tried, never a diagonal;
    - every newly covered cell passes the placement rules.
  - Journal, replay (REPLAYED) and rate limit as the other ops. The rate limit is now 150 ms / 240 a minute for all ops.
- **op 2 ROTATE:** now also validates the swing cell of a 2x1 and refuses a turn onto the requester's tile.
- **op 1 PLACE:** accepts 2x1 / 2x2. `OFURN_TOO_BIG` is now only for an unknown shape.
- **Message 102 OUTDOOR_FURN2** (new id; host to one READY client, RELIABLE, the same 268-byte `PCOfurnMsg` layout):
  - Kinds: 7 CAPS (`flags` 1 multi-tile, 2 move; sent first at join), the list of EVERY piece (BEGIN / CHUNK / END), SET / DEL, and 8 MOVE (e[0] = the old origin, e[1] = the new origin + item, for moves and rotations).
  - The footprint is derived from the item's profile and is never sent.
  - **Why a new id and not new 98 kinds or bits:** a 0.3.0 client takes 98 only at exactly 268 bytes, and its `pc_ofurn_msg_valid` drops the whole message for an unknown kind or PERMS bit. It would also draw a multi-tile entry as a 1x1 model on the origin. So **98 is unchanged and carries 1x1 pieces only**. A 0.3.0 client sees a multi-tile piece as one leaf on its origin plus invisible filler, and ignores id 102 (its dispatcher's "unrecognized: ignore", pinned against the v0.3.0 tag in `test_old_host_compat_src.py` O9).
  - A C2 client uses the 102 list as its view once it has arrived.
- **Old hosts:** a client sends op 4 or a multi-tile PLACE only after the 102 CAPS. A 0.3.0 host never sends 102, and it would answer op 4 with BAD_SHAPE plus a violation (O10). Against a 0.3.0 host a C2 client still grabs 1x1 pieces and can only rotate them; a push or pull gets "won't budge".
- **Service 10 / PERMS / reasons:** unchanged (no new bit; a 0.3.0 client would refuse one).

**Sidecar `outdoor_furniture.dat` version 2:**

- The same 52-byte record; `shape` (u8) and `flags` (u8, 0) replace the zero u16.
- A version 1 (0.3.0) file loads losslessly: its records are 1x1 and byte-identical. The host keeps `outdoor_furniture.dat.v1.bak` (never overwritten) and rewrites the file as v2 (log `is a version 1 (0.3.0) sidecar: N piece(s) converted to version 2`).
- The loader drops overlapping footprints, footprints leaving the town and unknown shapes.
- **Downgrade:** a 0.3.0 host refuses a v2 file as corrupt (O11). Its pieces stay leaves, the `FE1F` cells stay as invisible blocked tiles, and Place is refused until the file is removed. Restore the `.v1.bak` to go back.

**Storage outdoors (S3): done in C3** (see "Outdoor furniture C3: storage and place/pickup animations" below). The C2 list of what it needed:

- **A key in the dresser store:** owner = a town sentinel, floor 0xFC, layer 0, ut = the town origin, plus the furniture id with the rotation masked.
- **The open path:** an outdoor "open" interaction (the vanilla HOLD codes 6 / 7 / 8 = `open_furniture`, which today needs `my_room_clip` for the door animation), or a PC UI entry from the grab.
- **The host re-keys the store entry on every MOVE**, the same logic as a house move. ROTATE keeps the key because the id is masked.
- **PICKUP is refused while the store holds items** (the indoor rule: move / rotate allowed, pickup blocked). The vanilla pickup gate and the console `furniture remove` / `clear` need the same check, or must send the contents to the lost and found.
- **GC:** losing the marker while it holds items must move them to the lost and found, never drop them.
- **The 0.3.0 client's vanilla pickup of the origin leaf** must be refused while the store is non-empty.
- Protocol tests for all of the above.

This was too big to do safely in this pass, so it was left out whole.

**Switches:** as C1 (`AC_MP_OUTDOOR_FURNITURE=0`, `settings.ini [Network] outdoor_furniture_*`). C2 is always on when Place is on.

**Evidence:**

| Test | What it covers | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `test_ofurn_native.py` | `pc_ofurn_selftest` incl. the C2 footprint / rotation / move geometry / v1->v2 / 102 checks | PASS |
| `test_ofurn_src.py` | C2 audits C2W1-4, C2H1-4, C2P1-5, D1-D3 | 47/47 |
| `test_old_host_compat_src.py` | K22c, O9-O11 against the v0.3.0 tag | 25/25 |
| `wire_baseline.py` | id 102 documented | PASS |
| `test_ofurn_c2_protocol.py` (real listen host + FakeClients) | MAIN: 2x1 dresser + 2x2 table placement, every cell checked, rotation swing, blocked swing / push BAD_TILE, push / replay / wrong side / guest / pull, pickup clears all cells, a 0.3.0-style vanilla pickup + GC of the fillers, no violation, 98 stays C1. GC: a filler overwritten. V1: conversion + `.v1.bak`. RS: restart | 46/46 (MAIN 30, GC 4, V1 5, RS 7) |
| `test_ofurn_real.py` (real host + real client, Samsung, volume 1) | see below | 3 runs |

The three real runs:

- **Run 1** found cause 1 of the dresser bug: the model was never built.
- **Run 2** (after the vtable fix, with the full model draw cycle: 1266 models, no crash) found the move-bg height bug: every step was blocked locally.
- **Run 3:** 17/17. The 2x1 dresser was drawn with collision, grabbed in HOLD 19, then pulled, pushed and rotated twice (all APPLIED by the host). The observer's puppet showed 19 / 20 / 21 / 22 and its model slid. B picked the piece up.

The C1 `test_ofurn_protocol.py` K1 / P3 now expect multi-tile placement (not re-run in this pass: its ports are outside the current port range).

**Known limits / risks:**

- The grab directions assume the town camera looks north (stick up = -z), as vanilla outdoors.
- The pull "walkable" check refuses flowers and other ITEM0 values (conservative).
- Remote players are not checked against a moved piece's new cells; the collision pushes them out.
- On a client the 102 MOVE and the FIELD_UPDATE normally arrive in the same frame. If they do not, the moved piece may show its leaf for a moment.
- A multi-tile piece's filler cells block a 0.3.0 client's drops with the vanilla "can't place" message, without a reason shown.
- The pocket check for picking up a dresser does not count the (outdoor-empty) storage.

## Outdoor furniture C3: storage and place/pickup animations

**Status: implemented, not committed; harness + real-game automated evidence (below); BLOCKED: manual verification required.**

**User report (testing C2):** (A) "I'm unable to open the drawers / dresser" (a storage piece placed outdoors had no storage: C2 left S3 out); (B) "I want an animation of placing down the furniture like in the house, and when the player takes it back I want the animation for that too."

### A. Outdoor storage

**Which pieces:** every furniture whose profile is item storage (`aMR_pc_ftr_is_item_storage`: drawers, wardrobes, closets, chests, low chests; 1x1 and 2x1), placed in the town field.

**Controls (as built): the vanilla indoor gesture.** Indoors `aMR_ManageMoveBottun` grabs on the A press and, when A is released before the switch timer reaches 14 (60 Hz) frames, runs the piece's interaction instead (the open). Outdoors the same:

| Input in front of a placed piece | Result |
|---|---|
| A **tap** (released < 233 ms = 14 frames, no push / pull / rotate tried), storage piece, player in the FRONT row facing it (C3b) | the 27-slot storage screen opens |
| A tap from behind / beside a storage piece (C3b) | nothing opens (a short grab, as before) |
| A **held** | the C2 grab (HOLD; stick = push / pull / rotate); releasing never opens |
| A tap, other piece | nothing (a short grab, as before) |
| B | pick up (C2), now with the pickup animation |
| **Guest** (C3b): A in the front row facing a storage piece | the storage screen opens on the press (a guest has no grab, so there is no tap to end) |
| Guest: A anywhere else on a placed piece / B | the C1 notice "Only residents can do that with placed furniture" (no grab / push / pull / rotate / pickup / place, unchanged) |

A resident on a host without C3 gets "This server can't open storage outside"; the listen host's own player gets "Storage outside is for connected residents" (the host player has no host-side extra storage indoors either); a guest on a host without C3b (the first C3 build answers the dresser probe of residents only) gets "Only residents can use storage outside" once its probe gave up ("Try again in a moment" before that).

### C3b: guests open outdoor storage; only from the FRONT

**User requests:** (1) guests must be able to open outdoor storage pieces and use the storage screen like residents (put / take / move / swap), while still never grabbing / moving / rotating / picking up / placing outdoor furniture; (2) a piece opens only from its front (from behind or beside it, it used to open too).

**The front (from the source).** Indoors the open needs `contact_direction == aMR_CONTACT_DIR_FRONT` (`ac_my_room_action.c_inc` `aMR_JudgeDemoStart`, the contact computed from the edge normal and the furniture angle in `aMR_SearchContactPartName`) and the player facing the edge within 60 degrees (`aMR_ContactCheck`). Outdoors the rotation (low 2 bits of the tile, 0 S / 1 E / 2 N / 3 W) is chosen at placement as `(player facing + 2) & 3`: the piece faces the player who placed it, so **a piece of rotation `rot` faces direction `rot`** (rot 0 = +z, toward the camera, the vanilla default). The 2x1 second cell lies in direction `(rot + 1) & 3` (`aMR_GetTypeBPlaceInfo`), i.e. across the front: the piece is wide along its front edge. **The front row** = the tiles one step along `rot` from every footprint cell that are not cells themselves: 1 tile for a 1x1, 2 tiles for a 2x1 / 2x2 (the whole width). Pure helpers in `pc_outdoor_furniture.c`: `pc_ofurn_fp_in_front(fp, rot, ox, oz, pux, puz, max_depth)` and `pc_ofurn_faces_piece(rot, angle, tol)` (native selftest: every rotation, 1x1 / 2x1 / 2x2, behind, both sides, the diagonal, standing on the piece, an unknown shape).

- **Client** (`pc_ofurn_draw.c` `pc_ofurn_open_from_front`): the player's tile is in the front row (depth 1) and its quantised facing is exactly `(rot + 2) & 3`. A resident's tap from behind / the side stays a short grab (log `A tapped (not in front of the piece: no open)`); a guest's A there gets the C1 notice.
- **Host** (`pcnetgame_ofurn_storage_select_ok`, i.e. at the SELECT and again at every kind 18 commit): the requester's tile (the host's last copy of its position) in the front row up to **2 tiles** out (position lag) and its last facing (`pc_remote_player_get_last_facing_angle`) within **67.5 degrees** of the piece. Otherwise the SELECT is logged (`not in front of the piece`) and not answered, a commit is PRECOND: existing behaviour, **no wire change**. The 2-tile nearness, the in-town context and the live-marker / all-cells / storage-profile checks are unchanged.

**Guests (host).** Nothing on the wire changes; the gates moved:

- `DRESSER_SELECT` (84): a guest slot may send the **probe** (answered with the existing STATE only while outdoor storage is offered, `pcnetgame_ofurn_c3_flags()`: no house, so no v1 migration and no house info) and an **outdoor** select (floor 0xFC); any house floor is still dropped before a `private_data[]` subscript. `pcnetgame_ofurn_storage_select_ok` requires a READY peer with a bound identity (resident or guest) instead of a resident.
- kind 18: a guest slot passes the guest gate only with its own current outdoor selection (`s_dresser_sel[peer]` valid, floor 0xFC, `_r == idx + 1`); otherwise the old `NO_DONOR_SLOT` "a guest has no house" refusal (before the shape check, not journalled). The house branch and the owner-house journal peek are guarded `idx < PLAYER_NUM`; a guest without a selection searches the town prefix only.
- Fencing / journal: per identity in `s_txn_res[idx]` (the guest table grows it with the guest slots); the shared record uses `pc_dresser_store_apply_shared` as for residents. Pocket pre-image validation, the guest record write (`pcnetgame_rec_txn_write_inventory` works for guest records) and the durable save (the guest store file at the host save, like every other guest transaction) are the existing paths.
- **Restart roll-forward** now also runs for a guest slot of an outdoor record. To keep it safe with several identities journalling into ONE record, the pocket pre / post digests of an **outdoor** journal entry are salted with the requester's PersonalID (`pcnetgame_dresser_pocket_dig_k`): before, two players with identical pockets (two fresh guests: empty pockets, 0 bells) could match another player's journalled pre-image and receive its TAKE on the next SELECT after a crash. House records keep the plain digest (their persisted journals stay valid).

**Wire / compatibility:** no new id, no layout change, no new reason. A guest client of this build on an older host never sends an outdoor SELECT or kind 18 (both need the probe answered). A first-C3 client (resident) on a C3b host that taps from behind sends a SELECT the host now refuses: it times out with notice 6 ("Try again in a moment").

**The key (no sidecar format change).** The same 16-byte dresser key of `dresser_store.dat` v3 (`pc_dresser_store.h`): bytes 0..7 the town sentinel `T O W N 0xFC O F U` (no house owner digest: ONE shared store per placed piece), byte 8 floor `0xFC` (house floors are 0..2, 0xFD the v1 legacy overflow, 0xFF the probe), byte 9 layer 0, bytes 10 / 11 the piece's ORIGIN tile in town units (x 16..95, z 16..111: fits a byte), bytes 12..13 the base furniture id (rotation masked), 14..15 zero. Every house code path filters records by the house owner digest, so it never touches these.

**Wire (no new id, no layout change):**

- **SELECT:** the existing `DRESSER_SELECT` 84 (8 bytes) with `floor = 0xFC`, `layer = 0`, `ut_x / ut_z` = the origin, `ftr_item` = the tile value. The host answers with the existing `DRESSER_STATE` 83 (76 bytes, floor 0xFC echoed) only when: the feature is on (below), the requester is a READY bound player (**resident or, since C3b, guest**), the marker lives at that origin with that base id, every footprint cell matches, the profile is storage, the requester (the host's own copy of its position, `PLAYER_CONTEXT` in town) is within 2 tiles of a cell, and (C3b) it stands in the piece's front row facing it. Anything else is logged and not answered (the client times out: notice 6).
- **Capability:** the 102 CAPS message's `total` field, bit 0 `PC_OFURN_C3_STORAGE`. A C2 client checks only `count` and `flags` of a CAPS message, so the field was free; a 0.3.0 client never gets 102. A C3 client sends floor 0xFC only after that bit.
- **Transactions:** the existing kind 18 PUT / TAKE / MOVE / SWAP with `tag.flags` = the selection generation. For an outdoor selection the host re-checks the marker and the nearness at every commit, SKIPS the house scene / owner check, and applies with `pc_dresser_store_apply_shared`: identical to the house apply except that the RECORD-level nonce fence is off (two residents take turns at one piece; the record fence would FENCE the first one as soon as the second one used it). Fencing is per identity in the host's per-resident journal; the record journal still replays a journalled (nonce, seq) and answers CONFLICT, also after a restart (a retry without a selection searches the town prefix too). Pocket pre-image rules, the write-ahead sidecar save and the restart roll-forward are unchanged.
- **Reason 62 `OFURN_NOT_EMPTY`** (new): "Take the stored items out first". A 0.3.0 client shows its generic refusal line.

**Host rules:**

- **MOVE** (kind 22 op 4, the listen host's own push / pull): the storage is re-keyed to the new origin FIRST and the dresser sidecar saved, then the C2 marker / tiles (write-ahead). A failure afterwards re-keys it back. A destination key that holds another piece's (orphaned) items refuses the move as BAD_TILE (never merged).
- **ROTATE:** the key stays (rotation masked; the origin never moves on a rotation).
- **PICKUP is refused while the store holds items:** kind 22 op 3 (reason 62), the listen host's own B (notice), the vanilla `PICKUP_REQUEST` of the origin leaf (what a 0.3.0 client does: refused for anybody), and the console `furniture remove` / `clear` (refused unless `delete`).
- **A piece lost any other way** (the C2 GC: a police-box sweep, a host-side write, a crash between the two sidecar saves; a console `delete`; a pickup while the storage feature was off): every 500 ms after the GC a sweep marks an outdoor record whose origin has no marker of its id as an **ORPHAN** (the items are kept, logged) and drops an EMPTY one (keeps the 256-record table free). Decision: an orphan, NOT the lost and found (the police box holds 20 items and would silently lose the rest; furniture / fish / insects / tools mixed): the next piece of that furniture id **placed** outdoors, or a SELECT of a piece of that id without storage of its own, re-attaches the lowest-cell orphan (the indoor re-attach-by-id rule).
- **Switches:** outdoor storage is on when the dresser storage feature is on (`AC_MP_DRESSER_STORAGE`, ON by default) and outdoor furniture is not killed (`AC_MP_OUTDOOR_FURNITURE=0`). Off: no CAPS bit, SELECTs unanswered, kind 18 DRESSER_DISABLED, pieces pick up normally (a store that already holds items becomes an orphan when its piece goes and re-attaches when storage is on again). `settings.ini outdoor_furniture_place = 0` only stops new placements.

**Client open path (`pc_ofurn_draw.c`, no My_Room actor):** the tap (a guest: the A press) from the front row sends the SELECT; a wall-clock wait like the indoor direct open (notice 12 "Asking the host..." after 500 ms, one re-ask with the same generation at 1.5 s, notice 6 at 3 s); cancelled when the player walks away. With the host's view in hand and the submenu idle, `pc_dresser_ui_begin` (no vanilla keep slots outdoors: all 27 cells are the host's) and `mSM_open_submenu(INVENTORY, HANIWA_ENTRUST, 0)`: the same gyroid-style overlay in dresser mode as indoors (`data1` is never read in dresser mode). When it closes (the submenu's `menu_type` back to NONE), a transaction still in flight is polled to its end, then `pc_dresser_ui_end`. No door / drawer animation outdoors (that is the piece's move_proc, which never runs outdoors).

### B. Place / pickup animations

**What the house does (read from the source):**

- **Place** (`mTG_room_put_proc` -> `aMR_ReserveFurniture`): the menu closes, the player does NOT animate. The room reserves the piece for 46 frames: the actor is built invisible (BIRTH_WAIT, scale 0), one frame later the `eEC_EFFECT_KAGU_HAPPA` leaf (the item's leaf icon) flies from the player to the piece, at frame 46 `aMR_FtrBirth` starts: `NA_SE_ITEM_HORIDASHI`, a squash-and-stretch wobble (`scale_y = 1 + sin(c) * m`, `scale_xz = 1 - sin(c) * m`, c += 6000 a frame, at every upward zero crossing m *= 0.4 and the step += 1500, done below 0.02: ~33 frames) and `eEC_EFFECT_DUST` at the footprint corners 2 and 6 frames in.
- **Pickup** (B: `search_pickup_ftr_proc` -> `aMR_PreparePickingUpFurniture`): the piece shrinks (`aMR_FtrBye`: -1/12 a frame, gone below 0.25) with a dust puff at its centre 2 frames in, while the player plays `PICKUP_FURNITURE` (main index 32: the PICKUP1 clip, ITEM_GET at frame 10, GASAGOSO at 20; the room's leaf flies to the hand).

**Outdoors (`pc_ofurn_draw.c`, curves pure in `pc_outdoor_furniture.c`):**

- **Place:** the tag menu's Place (client: when the kind 22 PLACE is SENT; listen host: before its commit) queues a local note: the KAGU_HAPPA leaf flies at once (optimistic), and the model, built when the host's SET + FIELD_UPDATE are in, waits until 46 frames (767 ms) after the Place, whichever is later, then plays the birth (sound, wobble, dust). Log `[NET][OFURN] place anim contact`. A refusal: the vanilla "can't place" / notice, no model (the leaf has flown).
- **Pickup:** B begins the kind 22 PICKUP and, when it was sent, requests `PICKUP_FURNITURE` (state 32) through `mPlib_PC_request_pickup_furniture_outdoor` (`m_player_main_pickup_furniture.c_inc`, TARGET_PC: outdoors, i.e. without `my_room_clip`, the setup skips the pocket put-in and the exchange, and the leaf code that dereferenced `my_room_clip` is skipped) and starts the shrink. The item reaches the pocket only with the host's APPLIED post-image (unchanged). A refusal (e.g. 62) brings the piece back with the birth wobble. Log `[NET][OFURN] pickup anim contact`.
- **Other players:** the 102 SET / DEL (98 SET / DEL with a C1 host; the listen host's own broadcast) queue a birth (at once, no leaf: the leaf starts at the local player) / a shrink of that piece; a slot whose marker vanished with a DEL note shrinks before it is released. The picker's puppet plays row 32 (it existed); the placer has no clip (as indoors).

### Evidence

| Test | What it covers | Result |
|---|---|---|
| `dresser_store_selftest.c` (native) | outdoor key layout + rotation mask, a house key never outdoor, the shared apply (two nonces, replay, conflict) vs the house apply (FENCED), re-key both ways, orphan + find by id, drop_empty never drops an item, persistence | PASS |
| `test_ofurn_native.py` | the tap rule, the birth curve (volume-keeping, ~33 frames, peak 1.5, dt-scaled), the bye curve, the reason 62 text | PASS |
| `test_ofurn_src.py` | C3K1-2, C3W1-3, C3H1-5, C3C1-2, C3A1-3 (+ the C1 / C2 audits) | 62/62 |

Not run: the console `furniture remove` refusal / `delete` orphan (`test_ofurn_console_protocol.py` not extended; the code path is the C3H4 audit).

### Known limits / risks

- C3b: guests share the same store as residents: a guest can take out what a resident stored in a placed piece (and vice versa), exactly like two residents. Placing / moving / picking up stays residents' (and operators').
- C3b: the host's front check uses its last copy of the requester's position and facing; a player who turned at the very last moment before a SELECT may be refused once (the client re-asks after 1.5 s with the same generation).
- C3b: outdoor journal entries written by the first C3 build carry unsalted pocket digests; after the upgrade such an entry no longer rolls forward (logged, left alone) -- only relevant to a crash in the C3 window before the upgrade.
- C3b: a guest's kind 18 retry across a host restart, before it selected the piece again, gets the guest refusal (NO_DONOR_SLOT) instead of the journalled answer; its pocket side is reconciled by the roll-forward at its next SELECT.
- A refused pickup still plays the bend-down clip (state 32 is requested optimistically); vanilla would not start it.
- The record journal of a shared piece holds 16 entries for everybody: a retry of a transaction evicted by 16 other residents' transactions on the same piece across a host restart would be re-executed (in-process the per-resident fence covers it; across a restart the pocket pre-image check catches a PUT / TAKE whose pocket side was already rolled forward).
- An orphan re-attaches to the next piece of the same id placed ANYWHERE outdoors (by any resident): deliberate (never lost), but surprising.
- `pc/build64_w3`'s CMake cache still holds `PC_DRESSER_STORAGE_DEFAULT_ON=OFF`: an exe from that tree has dresser (and outdoor) storage OFF unless the host sets `AC_MP_DRESSER_STORAGE=1` (the tests do).

## Frame-rate independence of PC waits

Clients may render uncapped, which is the user's own setting (vsync = 0, max_fps = 0), and also at 144 or 30 fps. The game advances one update per
rendered frame, and vanilla timers are dt-scaled (`graph_dt_60hz_ticks`). A PC-added wait that counts calls or frames is therefore only "N / 60 s"
at 60 fps. Rule: a wait that means TIME (a network answer, a commit, a grace, a cap) uses `pc_net_game_now_ms()`. A wait that means "N game updates"
(a state machine step, a pad pulse, a log throttle) may stay frame-based.

**Fixed (2026-10-16), all wall clock now:**

| Wait | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Dresser direct-open wait | 180 frames | 3000 ms; notice 12 "Asking the host..." after 500 ms; vanilla menu + notice 6 at the cap |
| Dresser SELECT re-ask | 180 frames | 3000 ms; a re-ask of the same unanswered piece keeps its generation |
| Dresser screen deferred put / take give-up | 600 polls | `PC_DRESSER_UI_DEFER_MS` 10000 |
| Shop restock / upgrade eject grace | 30 frames | 500 ms |
| Shop restock / upgrade eject hard cap | 18000 frames | `aSI_PC_EJECT_HARD_CAP` 300000 ms; 18000 frames was 18 s uncapped and could force a player out of a Nook talk |
| In-room house commit quiet window | 30 frames | 30 game frames AND `PC_NETGAME_HCL_QUIET_MS` 500 (identical at 60 fps or less) |

The dresser fix is detailed in docs/dresser-storage-redesign-plan.md 6x.

**Frame-based by design (checked, unchanged):**

- **Already dt-scaled or in ms.** Every puppet timer in `pc_remote_player.c` uses `graph_dt_frame_time()` (dt-scaled), including the ACTION_EXT
  pending TTL of 300 frames. The pickup / drop / field-action timeouts (`graph_dt_period_elapsed`) and the notice timers (ms) are also fine.
- **Render-frame identity.** Puppet foot / hand / feel capture staleness (<= 3 frames), the collider snap hold (2 frames) and the per-frame effect /
  sound caps.
- **Steps of a state machine.** The 120-frame "submenu open processed" wait in the dresser screen close path.
- **Cadences, throttles and diagnostics.** `pc_ofurn_draw.c` nearest-set refresh (every 6 frames) and its log; the dresser attempt-gap diagnostic
  (`PC_NETGAME_DRESSER_ATTEMPT_GAP` 30 game frames, accounting only); the Nook intro cache refresh (every 120 calls; correct, just more file reads when
  uncapped); the restock "exit held" log (now every 5 s with --verbose).
- **TEST-ONLY waits.** Fish / bug catch triggers (240 / 120 frames), scene / collide test hooks, and the dresser lock / store / ftrmix drivers
  (600 / 900 / 1500 frame give-ups). They are frame-based and assume the fixture's capped 240 fps; uncapped they shrink up to 16x. The "direct" dresser
  hook is wall clock now because its timing variants run uncapped.

## Villager authority range and stale fallback

**Bug (user report):** "villagers sometimes get stuck outside, don't move, and I can't push them." **Root cause (confirmed in code and in a real run):**
a READY client runs no movement AI for a regular villager (`aNPC_pc_is_client_consumer`, ac_npc_move.c_inc). It only applies the host's NPC_MOVE stream,
and with no sample it held the villager where it was, forever. NPC_MOVE is only sent from a live HOST villager actor. The host only has villager actors
around its own player: the NPC manager scope (ac_set_npc_manager.c) and `Actor_delete_check` (m_actor.c) are both centred on `play->block_table`. On a
dedicated server that is the hidden observer standing at the station acre. In the fixture town, `npc` showed **0 of 7** villagers with a host actor.
Every other villager was a frozen statue on every client. It could not be pushed either: the only real push (`aNPC_pc_host_puppet_push`) runs inside
the host actor, and the client's own collision push is never integrated. The same happened when a villager walked out of the host's range: its last pose
was held forever.

**Host range (spawn host actors around every peer) was rejected as unsafe.** A villager's house entry / exit needs the house STRUCTURE actor
(`aNPC_request_house` -> `Actor_info_fgName_search`), which only exists near the host player, so a far villager would wait at its door forever. The
structures' collision offsets (houses, bridges: `mCoBG_SetPluss5PointOffset_file` in the structure ct) are only written by structure actors that exist.
The NPC manager's spawning is also a wade-driven state machine. The BG collision itself is valid for the whole town (`mFM_SetBG` fills every block).

**What ships instead (ON by default):**

| Part | Behaviour | Constants / switches |
|---|---|---|
| Client stale fallback (`pc/include/pc_npc_authority.h`, pure + `npc_authority_selftest.c`) | Per villager slot: HOST mode (mirror NPC_MOVE) -> LOCAL mode when no sample came for 2.5 s, or none came within the 4 s grace after the actor was created / the session became READY. In LOCAL mode the villager runs the unmodified single-player AI + physics on the client (the same path the talk lease uses, so pushing works). Back to HOST after 5 consecutive fresh samples (each <= 400 ms apart: hysteresis). The first host pose is a hard set. A stale ring is cleared when the actor is (re)created. Never while the villager is hidden in its house or another player owns its conversation; event NPCs are excluded as before. One log line per transition: `[NET][NPC] client: slot N npc 0x.... host silent for X ms -> local AI` / `... host stream back (5 fresh samples) -> host`. | `PC_NPC_AUTH_STALE_MS 2500`, `_GRACE_MS 4000`, `_FRESH_SAMPLES 5`, `_FRESH_GAP_MS 400`; `AC_MP_NPC_FALLBACK=0` or settings.ini `[Network] npc_fallback = 0` restores the old hold |
| Relay (message 99 `NPC_PROXY_MOVE`, client -> host, 28 B, the NPC_MOVE layout, <= 10 Hz) | A client in LOCAL mode reports its villager pose. While the host has no live actor for that villager (none within 500 ms), it accepts ONE relay owner per villager (the first sender; it lapses after 1.5 s of silence or when the owner leaves) and re-sends the pose as an ordinary NPC_MOVE to every OTHER ready peer, so several clients near a far villager see the same one. The owner never gets its own echo. The host also writes the reported x/z into its own villager list (not for a villager at home), so a host actor spawned later starts where the clients saw it. Validated: READY, the host animal table npc_id, the sender is in the town field and within two acres. An old host ignores id 99. | `PC_NPC_PROXY_SEND_MS 100`, `PC_NPC_PROXY_LEASE_MS 1500`, `PC_NPC_HOST_ACTOR_ALIVE_MS 500`; `AC_MP_NPC_RELAY=0` / `npc_relay = 0` |
| Conversation hold cap | A client talk hold now ends 120 s after it BEGAN, however often the 10 s keepalive repeats BEGIN. A capped (or operator-freed) peer's keepalives are refused until its END. Client side: the keepalive probe now returns 2 = a conversation is visible (talk / speak demo of that villager, or the message window open) or 1 = only the talk state is set; after 3 such keepalives in a row the client sends END itself. | `PC_NPC_TALK_HOLD_CAP_MS 120000` (`AC_MP_NPC_TALK_CAP_MS`, 0 = no cap), `PC_NPC_TALK_PROBE_MISSES 3` |
| Observer does not attract villagers | With `--host-observer` / `--dedicated`, every NPC measures its "player" distance / angle to the NEAREST real remote player in the host's scene (one acre at most) instead of the hidden observer. With nobody in range it sees no player (distance 10000, straight ahead), so villagers near the station idle normally. | m_actor.c `Actor_pc_observer_npc_player_dist`, `pc_net_game_observer_npc_target` |
| Diagnostics | The relay, hold, cap and expiry lines go to `servers/<id>/logs/server.log` too (`pcnetgame_npc_log`). Console `npc`: per villager, who simulates it (host actor / relay peer N / no actor), last NPC_MOVE age, hold (owner, age, keepalive age, capped peers), at home / outside, position + acre. `npc free <slot>` releases a stuck hold. | |

**Known limits.** ~~A villager driven by a client relay can only be pushed by the relay owner~~ (fixed: see "Villager push by non-owner clients" below). The local AI consumes the client's own qrand() stream (it was never synchronised with the host) and may set the client's local
`is_home`; neither is sent to the host, and NPC_STATE stays authoritative once the host simulates the villager again. Host load: the relay is one
validation + one fan-out send per relayed sample (<= 10 Hz per far villager); no host actors are added, so the actor pool is untouched.

Tests: `npc_authority_selftest.c` (41 checks); `test_npc_puppet_push_src.py` section 3 (source); `test_npc_fallback_protocol.py` (real dedicated host + FakeClients: relay / exclusivity / lapse / validation / hold cap / `npc free` / server.log);
`test_npc_fallback_real.py` (real dedicated host + a real autopilot client: the far villager moves; hand-over to a stream and back within ~3 s; the control with the fallback off reproduces the frozen, unpushable villager).

## Villager push by non-owner clients

**Bug (user report, test server built from e9f1ccb):** "the GUEST player can move the villager that's stationary, but with the RESIDENT it wouldn't work."
**Root cause (code + the live server's logs):** the villager stood far from the dedicated server's observer, so the host had no actor for it and relayed ONE
client's local AI (the relay owner, the first NPC_PROXY_MOVE sender). server.log: `slot 5 npc 0xE0B3 not simulated by the host -> relaying peer 1's villager
(pos 1060,2020)` at 05:41:00, peer 1 = the GUEST (`peer 1 READY: GUEST slot 0`); both players' last positions in the client logs were within ~60 units of that
spot. The resident's client mirrored the relayed stream (HOST mode: `NPC_MOVE first sample accepted slot 5 ...`, never `-> local AI` again for slot 5), and a
mirroring client never integrates its own player's push (`aNPC_pc_client_consume_move` sets the pose). The only explicit remote push,
`aNPC_pc_host_puppet_push`, was HOST-role gated, and the puppets' OC pipe does not pair with villagers, so on the owner's (guest's) machine the resident's puppet
walked straight through it. Pushing therefore worked only for the relay owner, whichever client that happened to be (the earlier doc's "known limit"). Not the
cause: the host-side push treats residents and guests the same (`pc_remote_player_host_npc_push` loops over every live field puppet, no player_no / roster
test); no talk hold was involved (no hold line in server.log).

**Fix (ON by default, kill switch `AC_MP_NPC_PUSH_FIX=0`, client side, no wire change):** the client that RUNS a villager (stale fallback LOCAL mode = the relay
owner, or about to be) now integrates the remote players' puppet push into it exactly like the host does for its own villagers
(`pc_net_game_npc_puppet_push_ok` -> pure rule `pc_npc_puppet_push_applies(is_host, is_ready_client, slot_local, fix_on)`, pc_npc_authority.h). The pusher's
client sees the move through the relay (puppet delay + relay ~100-250 ms, the same feel as pushing a host-simulated villager). The relay ownership never changes
because of a push (one exclusive owner, 1.5 s lapse, as before: no flapping), the stale fallback hysteresis and the hold cap are untouched; a consumer client
and single player never run it. Diagnostics: the owner's client logs `[NET][NPC] client: slot N (local AI, relayed) pushed by K remote player(s) at (x,z)` (at most
once per 5 s per slot); the console `npc` shows `relay peer N for S s`. Rejected alternative: a push-claim (move the relay ownership to the pusher on contact):
needs a wire change and an ownership hand-over race (two players pushing at once), with no gain over the owner-side push.

**How to check on a live server (console of the dedicated server):** stand both players next to a stationary villager far from the station and type `npc`.
The villager's row reads `relay peer N for S s` (N = the owner; `peer N READY: RESIDENT|GUEST` in server.log names it), `host actor` (the host simulates it: the
host push serves every player equally), or `no actor` (nobody runs it). `HELD by peers ...` = a conversation hold (`npc free <slot>` releases it). With the fix,
the non-owner's walk-into moves the villager and the owner's client_log.txt shows the `pushed by` line.

Tests: `npc_authority_selftest.c` section 12 (54 checks in all: the rule + a resident/guest relay scenario: the non-owner stays a consumer, no ownership change, its
push is integrated on the owner, nobody integrates it with the fix off); `test_npc_puppet_push_src.py` section 4 (source); real `test_npc_push_nonowner_real.py`
(dedicated host + a real resident + a real guest client: the non-owner and then the owner walk through the villager; it moves on both clients; the owner logs
the remote push; the relay owner does not change). Results: selftest 54/54, push_src PASS, wire_baseline PASS; real run 2: 17/17 (owner = the resident, peer 0;
the guest's 4 walk-ins gave 28 remote-push contact lines on the resident's client, push direction cos 0.95 with the walk, both views within 10 units; then the
owner's own push, views within 2 units; no ownership change, no mode transition). Run 1 (14/15) used a straight walk-through from 60 units off while the
villager walked away (no contact in the measured window; the remote-push line did fire at the guest's teleport next to it); the trial was changed to walk into
the villager's current spot. Not covered by a run: the reverse role (guest owner, resident pusher, the user's exact case): the rule has no resident / guest input.


## World keeps running behind menus

User request: in a multiplayer session, opening the inventory, any other submenu or the Esc menu must not freeze the world around the player (other players keep walking, villagers move, bugs / fish / weather / effects go on).

**Evidence (vanilla):**
- `Game_play_move_fbdemo_not_move` (m_play.c) runs `CollisionCheck_OC/clear`, `Actor_info_call_actor`, `mMsg_Main` only while `submenu.process_status == mSM_PROCESS_WAIT`: every actor is frozen while a submenu is up. `Game_play_move` returned at once while `g_pc_paused` unless the process was a HOST with a READY peer, so a CLIENT's Esc menu froze its whole world.
- The scene behind a submenu was a STATIC picture: on the menu's `PRERENDER_INIT` frame `makeBumpTexture` copies the EFB into `prbuf`, and on every `PRERENDER_DONE` frame it draws that texture and returns before `Actor_info_draw_actor`. On the GameCube the menu overlay was loaded over the player actor's code (`SubmenuArea_dlftbl` "submenu_ovl" / "player_actor") and over the object banks (hence `mSc_dmacopy_all_exchange_bank` + `load_player` in `mSM_move_End`), so the world could not even be drawn. On PC everything is statically linked and no `*_ovl.c` writes `object_exchange`; the "submenu area" is pure bookkeeping, BUT `Player_actor_draw_call` (m_player_call.c) still calls `load_player()`, which unlinks the open menu (`mSM_submenu_draw` then draws nothing). Found the hard way: the first live-draw build showed the world and no menu.
- Why a full actor pass during a menu stays unsafe (pinned in test_menu_world_live_src.py): villager action / think code births bank-backed tool actors, the NPC control actor creates actors and registers cloth DMA, a menu opened from a conversation would run the talking NPC against stale message state, the OC register / clear pair would drop the frozen player, and the LOCAL player's own state machine owns the menu (it must not run).
- Time of day, lighting and BGM already ran during menus: `mTM_time()` is called every frame from game.c, `Global_kankyo_set` / `mEnv_WindMove` sit outside the WAIT block, the submenu only changes the sound spec.

**Design (implemented):** one predicate `pc_net_game_menu_world_live()` (pc_net_game.c): a HOST with >= 1 READY peer or a READY CLIENT, AND the feature on (settings.ini `[Network] menu_world_live = 1` default, env `AC_MP_MENU_WORLD=0|1` wins). Single player, an unconnected client and a host alone return 0 and every new path is skipped (vanilla byte-identical).
- **Esc menu:** `Game_play_move` returns early only when paused AND no READY peer AND not live, so a client's world now runs exactly like a host's did. Pads stay zeroed (padmgr is role-blind), so the local player stands idle; the Esc talk guard (`Game_play_pc_host_paused_live`: `mDemo_Main` skipped, no new demo can start for an idle player) now covers the client too. Incoming PvP hits are already blocked while paused / in a menu (pc_net_game.c allowlist).
- **Submenu tick** (`Game_play_pc_submenu_world_hold`, non-WAIT branch): the host keeps its F / F2 behaviour (NPC_MOVE heartbeat, idle villagers tick). A live client runs the same guarded villager move proc (on a client it only CONSUMES the host's NPC_MOVE / NPC_STATE: no schedule / action AI; hidden villagers tick too so a house exit applies). Then, only while the menu is fully open from FREE PLAY (`PLAY`, no fade / wipe, no talk actor, message window hidden), a WHITELIST runs its own move proc through `Game_play_pc_submenu_tick_actor` (the exact per-actor prologue / epilogue of `Actor_info_call_actor`, under `g_pc_submenu_world_tick`: no actor creation, no exchange bank, deletes only marked = deferred, OC registrations dropped): remote-player puppets (ACTOR_PART_UNUSED; their collider is held while the flag is set), the effect control, the weather (a client never rolls the weather), the insect control (the mosquito's sting request is suppressed while the flag is set, ac_ins_ka.c) and the fish control. Water / texture scrolling (`evw_anime_add_scroll_phase`) keeps flowing.
- **Live draw:** decided once per menu on its first `PRERENDER_DONE` frame (latched; a peer leaving mid-menu never flips back to a stale picture): live session + free play -> `makeBumpTexture` skips the prbuf rect and draws the normal world (the world draw is bracketed by `mSM_pc_area_save / _restore`, m_submenu.c, so the player's `load_player` cannot unlink the menu); `Game_play_draw` writes the menu's POLY_OPA / POLY_XLU commands into sub-lists (the gfxopen / gfxclose idiom of the fades) CALLED from LIGHT_DISP, i.e. after the world's translucent pass, starting with `PC_NOOP_CLEAR_DEPTH` (new emu64 tag -> `pc_gx_clear_depth_now`, flush + depth-only clear, so the inventory's 3D player model sees the empty depth buffer it saw over prbuf) and `PC_NOOP_WIDESCREEN_STRETCH_OFF` (the UI keeps its 4:3 correction). FONT / OVERLAY still come after the menu, as in vanilla. Menus opened from a conversation / demo keep the vanilla snapshot.

**What stays frozen behind a menu (and why):** the LOCAL player (its menu state machine; also input is zeroed), the NPC control actor and shop / event NPCs (actor creation, cloth DMA, scripted), villagers that are talking / have a pending tool or hand-over birth, BG items / trees (they handle the player's tool hits and FG writes; only their filtered cosmetic pass runs, see "Cosmetic world reactions behind menus" below), bees (they request player states), the ball (OC pair with the player), the set manager (no NEW bug / fish spawns until the menu closes), structures, the camera (the view stays where it was). A menu opened from a conversation (shop, Nook, gyroid, dresser...) keeps the full vanilla freeze and the snapshot. The listen host's own submenu gets the same live tick + live draw (its F2 villager tick is unchanged); the dedicated observer has no menus.

**Tests:** test_menu_world_live_src.py (source audit, 55 checks: offline unchanged, whitelist exact, flag discipline, no create / delete / bank in the tick, input still suppressed, draw structure, settings / kill switch); test_menu_world_live_real.py (REAL host + client A + client B, TEST-ONLY autopilot `submenu inv|map|notice|calendar|close` (m_submenu.c hook, any role) and `pause 1|0`: B's puppet advances on A behind A's inventory and behind A's Esc menu, villagers move on A, puppet / control ticks > 0, no bank / actor change, pockets + wallet unchanged, A's own player does not move, map / notice / inventory cycle, the host's own inventory live too, screenshots `mwl_A_inventory.png` / `mwl_A_map.png` show the menu over the live world).

### Cosmetic world reactions behind menus

User feedback on the first version: "the moving behind menus works but the animations that happen for things like shaking trees only play after you leave the menu". Cause: what ANOTHER player does to the town is presented by actors that were outside the whitelist:
- the **tree sway / chop / fall** is the EffectBG actor (`ac_effectbg.c`, ACTOR_PART_EFFECT, a 3-slot keyframe pool). The puppet (`pc_remote_player.c` `pc_puppet_tree_shake_fire`) already started a slot behind the menu (`make_effect_bg_proc` only fills a slot), but the EffectBG move never ran, so the canopy stood still in its first pose until the menu closed;
- the **falling fruit / bells / furniture** of a shaken tree (TREE_DROP 77, `pc_bg_item_present_tree_drop`) and fly-away items are entries of the field-item actor's drop pools (bg_item, ACTOR_PART_ITEM), which was frozen;
- **tile changes the host / tile sync writes** (tree -> stump, items dropped / picked up, holes, pulled weeds, planted flowers) only become visible when the field-item actor rebuilds its draw tables (`mFI_FGisUpDate` -> `bg_item_common_renew_draw_and_crs_data_perfect`), i.e. in its move.

**Implemented** (m_play.c `Game_play_pc_submenu_cosmetic_tick`, called from the live tick: same live session / free-play / PLAY gate and kill switch `AC_MP_MENU_WORLD=0` / `menu_world_live = 0`, same guarded `Game_play_pc_submenu_tick_actor` with `g_pc_submenu_world_tick` set):
- the EffectBG actor runs its normal move: keyframes, the timer, leaves through the effect control (no actor), sounds; it only READS the grid (`EfbgBgitemTreeCheck`) and the ground height;
- the field-item actor (bg_item and its cherry / winter / xmas variants; not police / post) sees the flag in `bg_item_common_move` and takes a FILTERED pass (`bg_item_common_pc_menu_cosmetic_move`, bg_item_common.c_inc): only presentation fly drops (flag 0x4 = the landing never calls `mFI_SetFG_common`, no hole flag 0x8, not the honeycomb whose wait state moves the bee actor), the shine-spot glow, item fade-outs, and the draw-table refresh when ONLY `update_fg` is pending (no born item / wade / `remove_cut_tree_info_bitfield`, whose hole / beecomb clears wait for the menu), then the vanilla culling pass.
- Proof hooks: the town map (Save fg + deposit) is hashed before and after the cosmetic pass every frame; a change is counted and logged (`[NET][MENU] WARNING: the town map changed inside the cosmetic menu tick`). The hold END is followed by `[NET][MENU] cosmetic reactions END efbg_ticks= efbg_active_frames= bgitem_ticks= drop_ticks= drop_held= redraws= redraw_held= field_changed_cos=` and the first occurrence per menu of each reaction is logged (`[NET][MENU] tree reaction during menu: EffectBG animating` / `item drop presentation animating` / `ground items redrawn`). The listen host's own submenu runs the same code.

**Still frozen behind a menu (why):** a hole being dug / filled and a bury (the hole effect writes the tile when it finishes), pitfalls (they grab the player), the money-rock coin window (a gameplay timer plus a reset write; a remote player's rock hit is not presented on other clients anyway), every drop that writes its tile on landing (local drops, plant drops), the honeycomb / bees (bees chase and sting the LOCAL player), outdoor placed furniture models (`pc_ofurn_bgitem_move` registers move-bg collision: a piece placed by someone else appears when the menu closes), the ball, structures / shop doors (they react to the local player and scripted demos). Not presented at all on other clients today (independent of menus): a remote axe chop's falling-tree EffectBG and rock-hit sparkles beyond the puppet's own tool FX (those are effect-control effects and already play behind menus).

**Tests:** test_menu_world_live_src.py (now 73 checks: + the cosmetic tick touches only EffectBG / the four field-item profiles, through the guarded helper; the filtered pass has no hole / pit / money-rock / furniture / unfiltered drop step and no field write or actor creation; the drop filter; evidence that the fly drop's only tile write is under `(flags & 4) == 0`, that flag 0x8 is never set with 0x4, that TREE_DROP drops carry 0x4, that the refresh / shine / fade / culling / EffectBG code writes no tile, that the only block-state writes of the refresh need the cut-tree bitfield the gate requires 0, that the effect control creates no actor); test_menu_reactions_real.py (REAL host with `--field-action-test-seed` + client A with its inventory open + client B shaking the seeded fruit tree for real, FakeClient TREE_SHAKE fallback: R1 the sway animates on A while the menu is open, R2 A presents the TREE_DROP and the drops animate during the menu, R3 counters > 0 and `field_changed_cos=0`, the ground view refreshed, R4 pockets / bank / position unchanged, R5 the same on the listen host's own inventory, R6 no crash).

**Evidence (2026-10-17, pc/build64_w3, 3 real runs):** the drop / refresh / map-integrity half PASSED in all three runs: A presented the 3 TREE_DROP falling apples while its inventory was open and they animated (`drop_ticks` 511-512, `drop_held=0`), the ground refresh ran once (`redraws=1`), `field_changed_cos=0` on A and on the listen host, pockets / wallet / exchange bank / A's position unchanged, no crash (run 3: 19/23). The EffectBG sway is NOT runtime-verified: the EffectBG actor ticked every menu frame (`efbg_ticks` ~1900-3600) but no sway was ever started, because the shaker setup itself failed: B's real A press next to the seeded tree (56,104) sent no TREE_SHAKE, and the FakeClient puppet's shake at the west spot logged `budget=notree` even in the menu-CLOSED control (R0), i.e. the shake-tree search (vanilla: tile filter, |dy| <= 10, distance, 45 degree facing) rejects that spot independently of menus. The test now probes the four sides of the tree in the control phase and uses the first one A's puppet accepts; that version is written, NOT run.

## Notice board latency (worker BB)

**Report:** "the bulletin board is super slow".

**Cause 1, the main one (board UI, every role, worst on an uncapped client):** `src/game/m_notice_ovl.c` `mNT_add_calc_dt` converted the easing rate (`1 - (1 - rate)^dt`) and both step limits (`* dt`) to the frame's dt, and then called `add_calc` (m_lib.c), which ALREADY does exactly that. The single page turn (`mNT_Play_page_move`, move_time 1: C-left / C-right / stick), the slide to the new-post page (A) and the slide back to reading (cancel) therefore eased with dt^2: vanilla at 60 fps, but proportionally slower above it. Measured on the real function text (test_board_latency_native.py): one page turn 183 ms at 60 fps, 367 ms at 120, 729 ms at 240, 1518 ms at 500, **3033 ms at 1000 fps, 6065 ms at 2000 fps**; the board ignores input while a page moves. The user's install runs `max_fps = 0` (uncapped) with `vsync = 0`, i.e. hundreds to thousands of fps on the board. (The calendar's identical wrapper `mCD_add_calc_dt` had already been fixed to a pass-through; the notice copy was missed.) **Fix:** `mNT_add_calc_dt` is now a pass-through to `add_calc`: 182-188 ms per page turn at every rate from 60 to 2000 fps. The scroll back after editing (`mNT_roll_control2`, a per-frame ramp that ran faster with fps and slower below 60) now runs once per elapsed 60 Hz tick (`graph_dt_60hz_ticks`): ~117 ms at 30..2000 fps.

**Cause 2 (network, small):** the town page sync (Patch 6b, PAGE 71) found local edits only by a periodic scan every **500 ms** on both sides: a client's post waited 0..500 ms (avg 250) before the WRITE left; a listen host's own post waited the same before its new revision was pushed. The host side was already immediate for a client's write (APPLIED -> the other peers on the next host frame; FakeClient measurement on loopback: write -> other client's push 1..4 ms). **Fix:** `mNtc_notice_write` (m_notice.c, every post, the full-board shift, the automatic notices) calls the new `pc_net_game_pages_dirty()`: the next page tick scans at once (client: WRITE on the next frame; host: new revision pushed on the next frame); the periodic scan stays as the fallback for the other page writers (Able Sisters designs) at `PC_NETGAME_PAGE_SCAN_MS = 250`. A full board shifts all 15 posts = 15 page writes in one scan: the client now caps its own uploads at `PC_NETGAME_PAGE_CLIENT_WR_BUDGET = 16` per second (the host refuses above `PC_NETGAME_PAGE_HOST_WR_LIMIT = 20` per second per peer, unchanged), the rest go on a later scan, so a burst can never be refused BAD and lose a post. A WRITE the transport refused (reliable window full) is no longer marked in flight (it used to wait 3 s before the retry). Unchanged: base-revision check, STALE / BAD answers with the canonical copy, content validation, 6 page pushes per frame per peer, the join-push gate, guests read-only for notice posts, the wire format (no protocol change, old clients compatible). The client already showed its own post optimistically (it writes its save first); the host answer reconciles as before.

**Diagnosis lines:** client `[NET][PAGE] board action took N ms (<open (board slid in) | page turn | page jump | slide to the new post page | slide back to reading | post written ... | close>, ~F fps)` (m_notice_ovl.c, wall clock) and `[NET][PAGE] board action took N ms (notice post write kind 1 index i: local edit -> host APPLIED; upload wait U ms, round trip R ms)`; host `[NET][PAGE] host: pushed K changed page(s) to peer P, M ms after the change (oldest)`.

**Tests:** test_board_latency_native.py (native harness on the real `add_calc` / `mNT_add_calc_dt` / `mNT_roll_control2` text at 30..2000 fps + source checks, 16 checks); test_town_pages_protocol.py P9 / P9b (REAL host + FakeClients: write -> APPLIED and -> the other client's push 1..4 ms; a 14-write board-shift burst never refused, all mirrored in 11 ms); test_town_pages_real.py G4 / G5 (REAL host + REAL client: the client's latency line, the host's own notice edit pushed 0 ms after the change through the dirty hook). Not tested: the board UI driven by real input at a high frame rate (the native harness runs the real functions; the in-game look of a page turn was not watched).

## Full-pocket pickup and police lost-and-found (worker PK)

**Reports:** (1) "picking up items with pockets full will just not give the proper interaction and pretend to pick it up"; (2) "I can only get one item from the police lost and found and I have to re-enter the building before getting another item there. Check if it's synced as well."

**(1) Cause:** outdoors (field type FG) vanilla always starts the pickup (`can_exchange`), with full pockets too, and then offers the swap / put-back menu (pickup_exchange). A network client cannot run that menu against a host-owned field item, so the client seam (`Player_actor_setup_main_Pickup`) forces `exchange_flag` off, and `pc_net_game_request_pickup()` sends nothing when no pocket is free. Result: the full pickup animation played, the item flew into the hand, nothing was asked of the host and the item stayed on the ground (a pretend pickup, no message). **Fix:** `pc_net_game_client_pickup_no_room(item)` (client only: no EMPTY pocket and not a money bag the wallet takes whole) is asked in the outdoor branch of `Player_actor_CheckAndRequest_main_pickup_all` BEFORE the pickup is requested; when it says no room the vanilla refusal runs instead (`Player_actor_Request_main_refuse_pickup_all`: REFUSE_PICKUP main index, the yellow "pockets full" report message 0x17AD, no animation, the item stays, nothing sent). A late refusal (pockets filled while the pickup was pending: the Bug 3 ABORT, the queued TXN cancel with POCKETS_FULL, or request_pickup's own pockets-full branch) raises a one-shot flag (`pc_net_game_take_pickup_full_refusal`, 3 s, cleared by the next request) that the end of the pickup animation turns into the same refusal. A wallet-fitting money bag is still picked up with full pockets (wallet credit, unchanged). Single player, the host and a client in its own room keep the vanilla flow (incl. the swap menu). Known deviation from vanilla: a network client never gets the outdoor swap menu (refusal instead) and never stacks a ticket / paper into an existing stack (the host grants into a plain free slot, as before). Other pickup paths checked: bug / fish catches (own putaway / exchange gate, unchanged), dig (pockets-full check before the request), tree drops (ordinary ground items -> the new check), outdoor furniture pickup (kind 22: same outdoor branch -> the new check), Nook purchase (row 4 "pockets full" before the transaction), police claim (0x0781 before the transaction), mail take (own checks). The host's answer to a forged full-pocket commit is REJECTED(PRECOND) with nothing moved (no new reason id).

**(2) Cause:** `aPOL2_check_answer` (ac_npc_police2_move.c_inc) only runs while the message window sits at its continue point (`mMsg_Check_MainNormalContinue`). For a client the claim is a host transaction: the first frame begins it and the state returned every frame while it was pending, but the message window was not held, so it ran on to the next message (an A press, or the text's own force-next) before the answer arrived. The state never saw the continue point again: the officer stayed in CHECK_ANSWER for the rest of the visit (no `message_ctrl` -> no new claim dialogue), the item still arrived in the pocket through APPLIED, and only a re-entry (new actor) reset it. **Fix:** the claim holds the window with `mMsg_Set_LockContinue` while the host answers (also while another pocket transaction delays the begin) and releases it before the vanilla continuation (APPLIED -> "here you go", REJECTED -> 0x0781); a belt-and-braces fallback polls a pending claim even when the window has left the continue point and returns the officer to TALK_END_WAIT -> WAIT. The pointed (slot, item) is relocated by value in the CURRENT mirror right before the begin (`aPOL2_pc_locate_claim`): the host compacts its list after every claim when its own player is not in the police box, so a slot index seen before another claim can shift; identical items are interchangeable, a vanished item keeps the pointed slot and the host refuses it (NOT_AVAILABLE -> 0x0781).

**Sync status of the police box (verified):** host-authoritative. The host's `Save_t.police_box.keep_items` is the only truth; every claim is TXN_COMMIT kind 9 POLICE_CLAIM (slot, EXPECTED item, free pocket slot) validated against it, journaled (replay-safe), then the TOWN_SVC_STATE service 1 mirror is pushed to EVERY ready peer (the requester gets it right after its APPLIED). A second claim in the same session is APPLIED with reason NONE (rev + 1 each; the base advances with each RESULT, no STALE / CONFLICT). Two players racing for the same item: exactly one APPLIED, the other REJECTED(NOT_AVAILABLE), the item exists once. A client standing in the police box redraws the lost-and-found from each mirror (`mFI_SetFGUpData`); clients never compact or write their mirror themselves (mPB_keep_item / exit compaction are host-only). Items added on the host (acre sweep, placement, snowman, events) reach clients through the service's digest poll. Visual note: when the host player is outside the police box the list compacts at once, so the remaining items shift one place to the left on a client that is inside (vanilla compacts only at the exit); a claim always takes the item the player confirmed.

**Tests:** `test_pk_src.py` (source audit, 22 checks: the pre-check, the late refusal flag, the refusal at the animation end, the lock / unlock order, the fallback, the relocation, no client pocket / mirror write, host compaction rule); `test_pk_pocket_police_protocol.py` (REAL host + FakeClients, 25/25: full-pocket record upload, pickup POCKET / WALLET with full pockets -> PRECOND, tile and record unchanged, reservation released, money bag with full pockets -> WALLET APPLIED; two claims in a row APPLIED/NONE with rev + 1 each, mirror seq + 1 per claim to both clients, stale pre-compaction index NOT_AVAILABLE, value-relocated index accepted, race -> one APPLIED + one NOT_AVAILABLE, no duplicate); `test_pk_real.py` (REAL host + REAL client): P 9/9 (pockets filled, B press -> REFUSE_PICKUP 3 every sample, never PICKUP 30, the refusal log line, nothing sent / committed, player free after the message; control with one free pocket: the same B press runs PICKUP 30 + TXN APPLIED). K (police claim twice in one visit through the real dialogue): the run reached the police box and parked the player, but the test's parking spot was one unit short (the A press talked to the officer instead of pointing at the item; the `ac_goto k` aid is corrected to 30 units since) and was NOT re-run (real-run budget): the multi-claim fix itself is covered by the source audit and the protocol test only. Test-only aids added: autopilot `mashb` (B presses), `take HEX` (reverse of `give`), `AC_TEST_MUSEUM_ROOM=police`, `ac_goto.txt` `k <n>`.

## Text chat (worker CH)

**Request:** "press T to type to chat and Enter to enter it; a bubble / text box above you shows what you typed."

**Use:** in a multiplayer session press **T** (keybindings.ini `Chat`) while you can walk freely (no menu, conversation, cutscene, Esc menu, tool wheel or text field; the field or any room). A one-line entry opens at the bottom (`Say: ..._`, `n/60`); while it is open the keyboard is the line's (your character does not move, Esc does not open the pause menu). **Enter** sends, **Esc** cancels, **Backspace** edits, **Ctrl+V** pastes. The line appears as a speech bubble above your head for everyone in the same place (the town field, or the same house / shop / building) and in the chat log in the bottom-left corner (`Name: text`, the last 6 lines, ~10 s after each, all lines while typing). A bubble stays 6 s + 0.1 s per character (at most 12 s) and fades; a speaker keeps at most 2 (the newer on the head); bubbles of different players that would overlap are stacked. Bubbles show only over the live world (not over the inventory / map); the log stays.

**Font / characters:** the text uses the game's own message font (glyph codes, not UTF-8): letters, digits, punctuation, Latin-1 accents (é ñ ü ß ¿ ...), a few symbols (♥ ♪ ★). `#` `/` `;` `+` `\` are mapped to the font's own glyphs; a character the font does not have becomes `?`; control / zero-width characters are dropped. Drawn with the game font atlas (pc_text_draw) and flat PC overlay boxes (no new texture). IME composition is not shown inline (the composed text arrives when committed).

**Network (protocol 8 extended in place, no version bump):** message **100 CHAT_SEND** (client -> host, reliable, 68 B: len, seq, 64 font codes) and **101 CHAT_MSG** (host -> client, reliable, 80 B: origin wire id, flags, len, seq, scene id, the speaker's bound name, text). The host validates every line (READY + bound identity, exact size, length 1..60, every byte a chat font code, zero tail; then the chat switch, a rate limit of 3 lines burst / one per 0.7 s, the mute list), sanitizes it (blank runs collapsed), logs it (`[CHAT] name: text`, on a dedicated server in the console and `server.log`) and relays it to every READY peer whose announced scene (scene id AND owner) is the speaker's, plus the speaker's own echo (so everyone sees the host-validated text). A refused line answers the sender with a one-line notice (muted / too fast / chat off, at most one per 2 s). Each READY peer gets a capability message (CHAT_MSG flags CAPS+ON) at join and whenever the switch changes; a client enables the chat key only after it: on an older host the key shows "Chat is not available on this server". Older clients ignore 101 and older hosts ignore 100 (the dispatchers' silent fallthrough). No item, bell, world or save state is touched; the text is never interpreted.

**Host settings / admin:** `settings.ini [Network] chat = 1` (default; 0 = off for everyone, or on a client: no chat key, no bubbles), env `AC_MP_CHAT=0` (kill switch). Dedicated console: `say <text>` (a server announcement to everyone, gold `Server:` line, no bubble), `mute <player>` / `unmute <player>` / `mutes` (`servers\<id>\mutes.ini`, identity keys like `ops.ini`; a listen host keeps it in `save\mp\`), `chat [on|off]` (session switch + counters). Guests and residents chat alike; the dedicated observer has no player and sends nothing.

**Code:** `pc/include/pc_chat.h`, `pc/src/pc_chat.c` (pure: charset, sanitize, wrap, timing, rate limiter, store, mutes.ini; native selftest), `pc/src/pc_chat_ui.c` (input line, free-play gate, bubbles projected from the speaker's world position: the local player or the live puppet via `pc_remote_player_get_draw_position`, the log), the `CHAT` block at the end of `pc/src/pc_net_game.c` (validation, relay, caps, console), hooks in `pc_main.c` (events), `pc_pad.c` (the pad reads neutral while typing and until the Enter / Esc that closed the line is released), `src/graph.c` (draw), `pc_keybindings.c` (`Chat = T`), `pc_settings.c`, `pc_dedicated.c`.

**Tests:** `test_chat_native.py` (pure selftest), `test_chat_src.py` (40 source pins: wire, relay scope, validation order, caps, old peers, no gameplay writes, the key gate / pad zeroing, drawing, settings / console), `test_old_host_compat_src.py` K100 / O8, `wire_baseline.py` (ids 100 / 101 documented), `test_chat_protocol.py` (REAL dedicated host + 3 FakeClients, 32 checks: caps, scope, echo, rate, malformed, sanitize, mute / unmute + mutes.ini, `say`, `chat off|on`), `test_chat_real.py` (REAL listen host + REAL client: the chat key / text / Return pushed as SDL events through the real event loop, the opener's character swallowed, both bubbles drawn on both screens, screenshots `chat_*_bubble.png` / `chat_A_typing.png`). Test-only hooks: `AC_TEST_CHAT_SEND=<text>` (the real send path), `AC_TEST_CHAT_TYPE=<text>` (synthetic key / text events), both behind `AC_TEST_HOOKS=1` in `PC_TEST_HOOKS` builds. Not tested automatically: physical key presses (the zeroed pad with real WASD held while typing), an IME, a gamepad-only player (no on-screen keyboard: chat needs a keyboard).

## Nook bulk sell basket

**Request:** "Currently you can only sell one item at a time, or if it's the same item you can sell all of them. I'd like it to be a GUI instead, like the dresser, where you can place items inside and sell MIXED items (fish, insects, items ...), but not the stuff Nook wouldn't accept (delivery items ...). Then you confirm and he tells you the total amount like normal and how many things you're selling."

**Use:** in a multiplayer session (a client, or the listen host's own player) talk to Nook and choose "I want to sell". The basket screen opens: the dresser window (flat tan panel) with a 5 x 3 basket grid (one cell per pocket) and a summary on its right ("N items" / the total / "Bells"), the pockets below. A on a pocket item -> "Add to Basket" (the item moves into the next free cell; its pocket looks empty); A on a basket cell -> "Take Back".
